      FB IS BIPOLAR AND 64 REALLY IS ZERO — at FB 64 the response is flat to +/-0.3 dB across the
      band, no comb at all. Above 64 the teeth deepen with FB; below 64 the pattern INVERTS, peaks
      landing where the positive-feedback case has notches.

    FltPhase — A PHASER, AND THE Notch PARAMETER IS THE NOTCH COUNT. First notch frequency against
      that parameter, at Freq 75: 2098, 1254, 1020, 750, 656, 539 Hz for settings 0..5, with more
      notches appearing across the band as it rises. FB sets depth and nothing else: -7 dB at FB 96,
      -13 at 112, -28 at 127, and FB 64 is again exactly flat. The three Type settings are real and
      distinct — Notch and Deep differ by about 5 dB of notch depth, and Peak inverts the sense.
      THIS ONE IS THE LEAST FINISHED OF THE SIX: no Freq sweep was taken, so the notch positions are
      not yet tied to the dial, and the module adds up to +8 dB of its own gain which needs
      separating from Level before any drawing is scaled from it.

    WHAT IS NOT DONE, so nobody reads this as finished: nothing has been drawn. These are the
    responses only. The Res-to-Q law for FltStatic and FltNord, FltComb's 1.67, and FltPhase's Freq
    tracking are the three loose ends, and each is one short sweep.
    HARNESS NOTE — MASK THE FLOOR FROM THE DATA, NEVER BY A CONSTANT. The first pass fixed the mask
    at -45 dB, which is below FltLP's floor (-58) and ABOVE FltHP's (-45): the FltLP fits were clean
    and the FltHP ones returned confident nonsense, N=1 at five times the right corner with a 5 dB
    residual. Estimating each curve's own floor and masking 15 dB above it fixed every one of them.
    EQ (4)           EqPeak  ShelvEQ  Eq2Band  Eq3band
    ENVELOPES (11)   EnvADSR*  EnvH  EnvD  AR-Env  EnvADDSR  EnvMulti  EnvADR  EnvAHD  ModADSR
                     ModAHD  EnvDX
    OSC / LFO (7)    OscShpB*  OscShpA*  LfoB*  LfoShpA*  PulseOsc  LfoD  OscNoise
    SHAPERS (6)      Clip  Overdrive  WaveWrap  ShpExp  Saturate  LevScaler
    OTHER (8)        Mux8-1X  SeqA  Vocoder  SeqNote  DXRouter  Operator  DrumSynth  RndTrig
  Vocoder, SeqNote and Operator carry TWO graphs each; everything else one.
  THOSE THREE ARE NOT ALIASES, THEY ARE GAPS — settled 2026-08-24 by comparing gModuleProperties[]
  against the full set of modules the instrument supports. ShelvEQ, PulseOsc and LfoD are simply
  Unknown slots in our table, along with 19 others:
     ShelvEQ  PulseOsc  LfoD  EnvDX  AR-Env  SeqA  Mux8-1X      (these seven all draw a graph)
     SyncOsc  S&H  T&H  PeakFollow  RndStep  RndState  RndChaos
     PolarPan  PolarFade  Mixer6-1A  Mixer6-1B  OutBusA  OutBusB  ClkDivFix  AudioIn  BusIn
  ONLY ONE REAL ALIAS EXISTS in the whole table: moduleTypeSandH is the instrument's "S&H". Both
  facts are now commented on the enum itself in types.h, which is where someone will look.
  HOW TO FILL A SLOT: the type NUMBER is the missing piece and nothing about it is derivable from the
  name. The instrument's own patches carry it — load a factory patch that uses the module and DUMP
  reports its type against a name we render as "Unknown". A sweep of the factory banks would identify
  most of the 22 in one pass, and is worth doing before any of them is planned as a graphics job.

    THE GRAPH FOLLOWS THE ORIGINAL, THE ICON DOES NOT HAVE TO — CT, 2026-08-24: if the original editor
  draws no green-on-black graph for a module, we do not need one either. So the <#Graph list above IS
  the graph list, and it is closed; a module absent from it gets wave ICONS on its picker and nothing
  more. That is what settles the scope for everything below.

  OSCA, OSCC AND OSCD NOW SHOW WAVE ICONS (2026-08-24). One set — shapeOscATypeStrMap: Sine, Tri, Saw,
  Sqr50, Sqr25, Sqr10 — shared by all three, so one sample function serves three modules. None of the
  three is in the <#Graph list, so per the rule above they get icons only, no top-right graph.
    - THEY REACH IT BY TWO DIFFERENT ROUTES. OscA keeps the waveform in a PARAMETER (index 4); OscC
      and OscD keep it in a MODE (index 0 each, modeLocationList). Both routes already existed —
      module_wave_picker_param() for the first, module_wave_picker_mode() for the other two, which is
      the path OscShpB opened — so this was three lines plus the sample function.
    - THE FOUR TEXTBOOK WAVES ARE NOW PRIMITIVES, not copies: basic_sine(), basic_triangle(),
      basic_falling_saw() and basic_pulse(duty) sit above lfob_waveform_sample(), which now delegates
      to them and is otherwise unchanged. LfoB's measured cycle is untouched by being named.
    - THE THREE SQUARES ARE PULSE WIDTHS AND THE NAMES SAY SO — 50%, 25%, 10% — which is the one part
      of this set that needs no interpretation.
    - NOT YET CAPTURED, AND THE CODE SAYS SO. The shapes are transferred from measurements of OTHER
      modules: LfoB's cycle for the first four, OscShpB's Pulse for the convention that a pulse is
      drawn full-swing with no DC removal. Two things to settle on the hardware, both of which this
      family has already proved can go either way:
        - THE SAW'S DIRECTION. LfoB's falls, OscShpB's DblSaw rises. An oscillator saw cannot be
          assumed to match the LFO's; if OscA's rises, this is one negation.
        - WHETHER THE PULSES CARRY EDGE RAMPS. OscShpB's measured Pulse ramps into and out of its
          steps (pulse_edge_width()); these are drawn as ideal steps for now.
      MEASURED 2026-08-24, AND BOTH TRANSFERS WERE WRONG. OscA into 2-Out, waveform swept 0-5 with
      measure.py, cycle recovered by autocorrelation and cycle averaging, model checked by COMPILING
      osc_a_waveform_sample() straight out of moduleGraphics.c — the same harness discipline that
      caught DblSaw upside down, and it earned its keep twice more here:
        - THE SAW RISES. Drawn falling, carried over from LfoB whose saw genuinely does fall, it
          correlated 0.516; rising, 0.988. Three saws measured on this instrument now, in two
          directions (LfoB falls, TriSaw falls, DblSaw and OscA rise), so saw polarity is per-module
          and must never be carried across unmeasured.
        - "SQR10" IS A 1/16 PULSE, NOT A 10% ONE. Fitting ideal pulses of every width against the
          captured cycle puts it at 6.25%: 10% scores 0.787, 6.25% scores 0.990. Measured twice — at
          E4 (145 samples per cycle) and three octaves down (1165) — agreeing to within the fit's own
          resolution. THE MANUAL SAYS 10% (p174: "Sine, Triangle, Sawtooth, Square, 25% Pulse or 10%
          Pulse") AND IS WRONG on that one entry; its 25% is exact and so is the square's 50%. All
          three widths are then binary fractions, 1/2, 1/4, 1/16, which is what a DSP would give.
          The NAME is left alone — it is the manual's and the original's — only the drawn width moved.
        - THE PULSES NEED NO EDGE RAMPS. Drawn as ideal steps they correlate 0.992 and 0.989, so the
          band-limiting OscShpB's Pulse needed does not show on this family.
      Final correlations, rotation-free, against the instrument's own cycle:
        Sine 1.0000  Tri 0.9981  Saw 0.9880  Sqr50 0.9917  Sqr25 0.9888  Sqr10 0.9903
      CT confirmed all three modules look right on menu selection.

      TWO TRAPS IN THE ANALYSIS ITSELF, both worth knowing before the next sweep:
        - AUTOCORRELATION READ A FIFTH OF THE PITCH. Normalising by only the first window's energy
          makes the score climb with lag, so the search settled on 728 samples for a 145-sample wave
          and every "averaged cycle" held five real ones. Duty still measured correctly (it is a
          fraction of time either way), which is what made the error look like a modelling failure
          rather than an analysis one. Normalise by BOTH windows and take the SMALLEST lag scoring
          within 10% of the best.
        - THE LEAD-IN IS NOT A SETTING. measure.py holds each value for period*repeats seconds after a
          2 s settle, and during that settle the module still holds whatever it was set to before —
          which reads as an extra region and shifts every label by one. Detect change points only to
          locate the first boundary, then lay the known grid out from there.

  OSCB NOW SHOWS WAVE ICONS TOO (2026-08-24), measured in Slot A. Every one of its five turned out to
  be a law we already had, so it is an index remap rather than a new family — param 8, five entries.
    - SHAPE ONLY REACHES TWO OF THE FIVE. Swept across the whole dial, Sine stays a pure sine (no
      harmonic above the first exceeds 0.01 at any setting), Tri stays a triangle and Saw stays a
      sawtooth with harmonics at 1/n to two decimals. Only Sqr and DualSaw respond at all.
    - SAW RISES — 0.988 against the rising form, 0.516 against the falling one. Same as OscA, opposite
      to LfoB. That is now three oscillator saws rising and two LFO ramps falling.
    - SQR IS OSCSHPB'S PULSE, law and all: duty 0.500 / 0.375 / 0.250 / 0.125 at Shape 0/32/64/96,
      i.e. 0.5 - 0.49*shape, exactly what we already draw. AND IT SHARES THE 8-SAMPLE FLOOR: at the top
      of the dial it lands on 0.055 rather than 0.010, and 0.055 of a 145.6-sample cycle is 8.0
      samples — the same floor OscShpB's Pulse was measured to have, so that floor belongs to the
      instrument's pulse generator and not to one module.
    - DUALSAW IS OSCSHPB'S DBLSAW at 0.999 across the dial, runner-up 0.87 at best.
    - THE MANUAL MISCOUNTS OSCB (p174): "one of five waveforms", then it lists six by adding a
      symmetric pulse. There is no sixth — writing 5 to the waveform parameter returns a cycle
      IDENTICAL to DualSaw at 1.000, so the device clamps and our declared range of 5 is right. That
      was the open question and it is closed.
    - Verified on screen: the rising saw and the narrow pulse both draw correctly on the face.

  OSCB AND OSCA NOW CARRY THE GREEN-ON-BLACK GRAPH TOO (CT's call, 2026-08-24). Neither is in the
  original's graph list, so this is us going further than it did rather than catching up — CT: "it's
  worth adding, to fill the space".
    - ON OSCB THE GRAPH EARNS ITS KEEP DIRECTLY: Shape genuinely changes two of its five waves, so
      the box moves as the dial does. On the other three it simply draws the wave larger.
    - ON OSCA IT IS STATIC, and that is the honest description: OscA has no Shape dial at all, so its
      graph is a legible enlargement of the picker icon and nothing more.
    - OSCA'S BYPASS HAD TO MOVE and the first move was wrong. anchorBottomRight {-3,-3} put it exactly
      where the audio OUTPUT already sits, and the two drew on top of each other. It now sits at
      {-3,-14}, stacked above the connector, with the graph above that — three things down the right
      edge of a three-row face, which is the shortest of the group (OscShpB is four rows, OscB five).
    - THE RENDERER NO LONGER KNOWS ABOUT MODULES. render_oscshpb_waveform_graph() used to carry a
      chain of isShpA / isLfoShpA / isLfoB tests to find each module's waveform and Shape, duplicating
      what module_wave_sample() already knew for the icons; adding OscB would have made that a third
      copy. Two resolvers — module_wave_value() and module_shape_value() — now answer those questions
      once for both the big graph and the little button, and the graph calls module_wave_sample() like
      the icon does. So a law corrected in one place corrects both, and a module added there gets both.

  A PROCESS TRAP WORTH NOT REPEATING: do-uncrustify reflows moduleResources.h wholesale, so I had got
  into the habit of reverting that file after every format pass. It then silently reverted a REAL edit
  (OscB's graphLocationList row), and the resulting build had the graph dispatch calling for a module
  whose table row was gone. An hour went into suspecting the refactor before rebuilding into a clean
  derived-data path showed the same source rendering perfectly. Back the file up, run uncrustify, then
  restore the backup — never git checkout a file you have edited.


  LFOA AND LFOC NOW SHOW WAVE ICONS (2026-08-24). Both use lfoWaveStrMap, the same map LfoB uses, but
  all SIX entries rather than the four LfoB clamps to — LfoA keeps the waveform in a parameter
  (index 4), LfoC in a mode (index 0). The first four delegate straight to LfoB's measured cycle, so
  only the two random ones are new.
    - A RANDOM WAVE HAS NO CYCLE TO MEASURE, so these are the only wave icons in the app that are a
      REPRESENTATION rather than a fitted law, and the code says so at the definition. What the icon
      has to convey is the one distinction between the two entries: RndSt holds each value until the
      next step, Rnd glides between them. Six steps at fixed levels, from a static table — drawing
      from a real random source would make the icon flicker on every redraw, and two entries that
      never look the same twice cannot be compared.
    - VERIFIED BY COMPILING THE REAL FUNCTION, as with every other wave: RndSt holds across 234 of 239
      sample pairs, Rnd changes across 239 of 239, and the seam rule does the right thing for both —
      RndSt ends a step away from where it began (+0.55 to -0.60) and gets its vertical, Rnd closes on
      itself (+0.55 to +0.54) and correctly gets none.
    - BOTH OPEN CHECKS ARE NOW DONE (2026-08-24, after a power cut that cost nothing — the captures
      were already on disk and the MacBook was on battery):
        1. LFOA ACCEPTS ALL SIX WAVES, settling the standing assumption. Waves 4 and 5 are nothing
           like Squ — RMS 0.006 against Squ's 0.019, median flat runs of 8 and 1 samples against
           Squ's 134 — where LfoB's 4 and 5 were measured as indistinguishable from it. So the
           trailing RndSt/Rnd in lfoWaveStrMap do belong to LfoA and LfoC, as suspected.
        2. RndSt STEPS AND Rnd GLIDES, in that direction. Each wave captured to ITS OWN file rather
           than as a sweep, so there are no segment boundaries to mis-read, then judged from the
           distribution of sample-to-sample differences — bimodal for a staircase, not for a glide:
                          p50/p99    differences at zero    longest hold
                RndSt      0.057           43.4%             19 samples
                Rnd        0.123           21.1%              8 samples
        3. BOTH FACES LOOK RIGHT. LfoA's icon sits clear at the top right; LfoC's wider button had
           the room and does not overflow the module. Verified on screen.
      TWO METHOD NOTES WORTH KEEPING:
        - DO NOT READ STEP-VS-GLIDE OFF A PLOT. Eyeballing 10 ms windows gave the OPPOSITE answer at
          one point, because two captures autoscale to different vertical ranges and the eye compares
          shape rather than hold time. The statistic was right where the picture was not.
        - ONE SETTING PER FILE when the quantity being measured is not periodic. A sweep needs its
          boundaries located, and for a signal with no cycle there is nothing to locate them with.
      STILL OPEN, AND SMALL: Rnd holds 21% of the time, so it is more likely a slewed or smoothed
      random than the linear interpolation the icon draws. The direction it differs from RndSt in is
      settled; the exact interpolation is not.

  RECT AND SHPSTATIC FINISH THE SET (2026-08-24), and they are a different KIND of picture: transfer
  curves, not waves. The box plots output against INPUT, -1 to +1 across, so the cycle-seam rule had to
  be suppressed for them — a rectifier's two ends are its extremes, and joining them would draw a
  vertical through the middle of the icon. Everything else goes through the same plumbing.
    - MEASURED WITH A DRY/WET RIG, which is the right shape for any transfer curve: a triangle sent
      BOTH straight to one output and through the shaper to the other, so pairing the two channels
      sample by sample gives the curve directly and the source cancels out.
    - FIT THE GAIN, DO NOT NORMALISE. Peak-normalising the recovered curve first pulled every exponent
      toward 1 (x3 read as 2.58), because the two channels have unknown relative gain and an input
      that never reaches full scale looks exactly like less curvature. With gain free the same data
      gives x3 = 2.97. Worth remembering for any transfer curve measured this way.
    - RECT IS EXACTLY WHAT THE MANUAL SAYS (p207) — discard negatives, discard positives, mirror
      negatives up, mirror positives down — which is worth recording precisely because the manual has
      been wrong so often elsewhere in this work.
    - SHPSTATIC IS y = sign(x).|x|^p:
          x2      p = 1.98   rms 0.00002        x3      p = 2.97   rms 0.00002
          Inv x2  p = 0.65   rms 0.00079        Inv x3  p = 0.49   rms 0.00122
      The two positive powers are exact. THE TWO INVERSE CURVES ARE NOT: they fit a pure power law
      forty times worse and sit well above their nominal 1/2 and 1/3. The drawn exponents are the
      measured shape rather than the named one, which is good enough for a 30-pixel icon and wants a
      second look before anything depends on it more precisely.
    - Both faces verified on screen: HalfPos draws flat-then-rising, FullPos a V, Inv x3 steep through
      the centre, x3 flat through it. No spurious seam verticals.

  THE WAVE SET IS NOW COMPLETE. Pickers on OscShpA, OscShpB, LfoShpA, LfoB, OscA, OscB, OscC, OscD,
  LfoA, LfoC, Rect and ShpStatic; graphs on OscShpA, OscShpB, LfoShpA, LfoB, OscA and OscB.

    - (STALE, and cleared 2026-08-29: this bullet listed LfoA, LfoC, Rect and ShpStatic as still
      wanting pickers, but the entries above record all four as done on 2026-08-24 and the summary
      line says the set is complete. Kept only as a note that it was checked, so nobody chases it a
      third time.)

  THE SOUND ENGINE HAS ITS OWN, DIVERGENT, AND NOW KNOWN-WRONG COPY OF ALL THIS - osc_shp_wave() in
  soundEngine.c. CT raised this twice ("when we recreate the sound engine, this analysis might also
  help there"), and it is worse than a missed improvement, because the two implementations disagree on
  things the measurement has now settled:
    - it maps the dial as t = (shape - 0.5) / 0.49 CLAMPED AT ZERO, so nothing at all happens below raw
      64. The capture shows the harmonics growing from raw 16 upward. HALF THE DIAL IS DEAD.
    - its Sine3 is a rectified sine ("rectification is what produces even harmonics") - the
      even-harmonic reading the spectrum disproves.
    - its Sine4 is a tanh soft clip, its Sine1 returns -cos of a warp whose breakpoint runs to 0.02
      (wrong phase AND wrong endpoint), its DblSaw detunes by only half a cycle * t, and its SymPulse
      detunes DblSaw by only a quarter cycle. (Its SymPulse emitting 0.0 between pulses is NOT a bug -
      I first wrote that it was; the drawn wave does the same and correlates 0.995, so the three-level
      shape is correct and only the width law was wrong.)
  THIS IS EXACTLY THE SPLIT paramCurves.c ALREADY EXISTS TO PREVENT - it was pulled out of
  renderParams.c so a curve could not be corrected in one place and left wrong in another, and these
  wave models want the same treatment: one platform-free file included by both moduleGraphics.c and
  soundEngine.c, the way paramCurves.c is shared by the dial text, the drawn curves and the engine.
  DONE 2026-08-23: osc_shp_wave() now carries the same laws, law for law, with the dead half of its
  Shape dial removed.
  AND DONE PROPERLY 2026-08-29 — src/waveModels.c and .h, platform-free, included by both
  moduleGraphics.c and soundEngine.c. The two copies are gone; there is now one home for every
  measured constant, which is what paramCurves.c does for the dial curves and what this always
  wanted.
  THE SPLIT IS NOT "MOVE THE FUNCTION", and that is the part worth understanding before touching it.
  The two implementations were 230 and 128 lines and they did NOT share their code — they shared
  their LAWS:
    - SINE1..SINE4 ARE COMPLETE and identical, character for character. Closed-form, no
      discontinuity, so every consumer wants the same number at a given phase. waveModels.c returns
      the value and both callers use it as it is.
    - TRISAW, DBLSAW, PULSE AND SYMPULSE EXPORT PARAMETERS ONLY — the peak, the detune, the duty, the
      half-segment. All four contain a STEP, and how a step is rendered belongs to what it is being
      rendered into, not to the wave: the engine BAND-LIMITS (an unlimited step aliases across the
      spectrum), while the editor draws a 200-point curve and has to add explicit narrow ramps at the
      crossings, because a hard step between two sample points is simply missed. A single shared
      "sample" function would have forced one of them to undo the other's work.
  Net effect: 230 lines became 66 in moduleGraphics.c and 128 became 51 in soundEngine.c, and the
  measurement documentation moved to waveModels.c where it describes the one implementation.
  PROVED EXACT, not assumed: a harness compared the original formulas, written out inline as they
  stood, against the shared file over 10,173,084 points — every wave, 1271 Shape values, 2000 phases
  — and found ZERO differences. It first reported 4912 differing by 3.3e-16, which was THE HARNESS,
  not the code: the old copies were static and got inlined while the shared ones were out-of-line
  calls, so sin() vectorised in one and not the other. Marking the old copies noinline made it exact.
  Worth remembering before trusting a last-bit comparison between an inlined and a called function.
  The drawn output is byte-identical: the same backdoor SCREENSHOT of SimpleLead before and after.
  waveModels.c is in do-vst3's manual source list, added at the same time — that list is exactly the
  trap this file warns about elsewhere, and it has now caught three new files in a week.

  WHICH MODULES WANT ONE: the natural list is the modules whose wave the user PICKS, which the
  original editor draws as image selectors rather than text — OscA, OscB, OscShpA, LfoA, LfoB,
  LfoShpA, Rect and ShpStatic (see the resource-file survey; those eight carry waveform ICONS on
  their selector buttons). The manual's oscillator pages should settle any that are ambiguous.
THE G2 DOES NOT WORK OUT ITS OWN UP-RATING — the editor is authoritative, and a scripted patch never
told it. Found by CT 2026-08-24 looking at the envelope measurement patch ("I'm not sure the connector
colouring, cable colouring or possibly audio processing promotion is working on that patch"), fixed
the same day.
  update_module_up_rates() was called from every UI path — canvasDrag, menus, mouseHandle, selection,
  undo — AND FROM NO SCRIPTED ONE. backdoor.c's CABLE/DELCABLE inherited the from-connector's current
  colour, which the comment there correctly describes as the same rule the drag path follows, but that
  rule is only half of it: the drag path then RE-ASSESSES the whole slot, because a new cable can
  promote the module at its far end. A scripted patch never re-assessed, so nothing downstream of an
  audio source was ever promoted.
  IT WAS NOT COSMETIC, which was the surprise. Measured on the hardware with OscB -> LevMod -> 2-Out,
  the same rig as the envelope work, comparing the carrier's spectrum before and after the fix:
        before (control rate) : non-harmonic energy above 2 kHz  -15.9 dB rel fundamental,
                                broad alias peaks about -32 dB scattered over 13-17 kHz
        after  (up-rated)     : the same measure  -34.1 dB, an 18 dB improvement, with the
                                residue only up at 23-24 kHz and -48 dB or below
  So eMsgCmdSetModuleUpRate really does set the rate the instrument RUNS the module at. Every
  backdoor-built patch until now has been running its promoted modules at control rate with an alias
  floor 16 dB under the carrier — worth remembering for any earlier measurement made through one.
  THE ADSR CURVES WERE NOT AFFECTED, checked rather than assumed: re-measured after the fix, attack k
  2.87 against 2.81 and decay 4.35 against 4.51, both inside the scatter of the repeats. The alias
  products scale WITH the carrier, so they ride the envelope instead of distorting its shape, and a
  10 ms RMS window averages them away. LinLin's straight-line fit is what would have caught it
  otherwise.
  THE ASYMMETRY IS GONE, 2026-08-29 (CT: "Backdoor recolour is worth doing"). backdoor.c's CABLE now
  calls cable_chain_recolour() on the to-end before update_module_up_rates(), in the same order
  canvasDrag.c uses, so a scripted patch derives a chain's colour across the whole tree exactly as a
  drawn one does. cable_chain_apply_colour() sends eMsgCmdSetCableColour per cable it changes, so the
  instrument follows — a RECOLOUR, not a write, which is what stops the G2 holding each cable twice.
  DEMONSTRATED, not assumed. A sourceless input-to-input link — the case the old note predicted would
  be wrong — built offline through the backdoor on the build before and after:
      before:  cable loc=VA from=1:0 link=0 to=2:0 colour=1   (blue, inherited from the from-connector)
      after:   cable loc=VA from=1:0 link=0 to=2:0 colour=6   (white)
  White is what the manual calls a "non-functional input-to-input connection", and it is what the drag
  path has always produced.
  A TRAP IN TESTING IT: cableLinkTypeFromInput is 0 and cableLinkTypeFromOutput is 1, so "link=1" on
  the CABLE command is the ORDINARY output-to-input cable and "link=0" is the input-to-input one. The
  first attempt at this test used link=1, built two perfectly ordinary cables, and showed no
  difference before or after — which looked like the fix doing nothing.

THE DRAWN ENVELOPE WAS NEVER THE ENVELOPE YOU HEARD — one law, two constants. Fixed AND MEASURED
2026-08-24. HEADLINE CORRECTED 2026-08-29: it read "the constant itself is still a guess and wants a
capture", which was true when written and was made false further down THIS SAME ENTRY a few hours
later — see "SETTLED ON THE HARDWARE" below, and ENV_ATTACK_SHARPNESS / ENV_FALL_SHARPNESS in
paramCurves.c. Read to the end of an entry before quoting its first line: this one was relayed as
open work on the strength of the headline alone.
  soundEngine.c shaped its segments with exp(+/-5t) and moduleGraphics.c drew them with exp(+/-4t).
  Same law, same normalisation, same shape-index mapping (both give Log/Lin/Exp/Lin for envShapeStrMap's
  LogExp/LinExp/ExpExp/LinLin) — only the sharpness differed, so an EnvADSR's face and its sound were
  two different curves and nothing announced it, because each file was self-consistent.
  THIS IS THE SECOND TIME THE SAME SPLIT HAS BITTEN (osc_shp_wave() was the first, 2026-08-23), and it
  is what paramCurves.c exists to stop.
  NOW ONE DEFINITION: tEnvShape, env_attack_level() and env_fall_level() live in paramCurves.c behind
  ENV_CURVE_SHARPNESS, and both callers go through them. moduleGraphics.c keeps only envadsr_decay_level(),
  which is the part that really is a drawing concern — a drawn segment runs between two arbitrary levels
  (full to Sustain, Sustain to the release target) where the engine's always run 1 to 0.
  WHAT MOVED AND WHAT DID NOT, checked numerically over 1000 points per shape:
    - the engine is BIT-IDENTICAL (max |delta| 0.0) — 5.0 was already its number, so nothing audible changed
    - the drawn Lin/LinLin segments are identical to float noise (1.1e-16)
    - the drawn EXPONENTIAL segments moved by up to 0.076 of full scale: a Log attack a fifth of the way
      through now reads 0.6364 where it drew 0.5609
  SETTLED ON THE HARDWARE 2026-08-24, AND IT NEEDS TWO CONSTANTS, NOT ONE. Neither 4.0 nor 5.0 was
  right, and the rise and the fall are not equally curved:
        shape    dial   attack k   decay k
        LogExp    80      2.90       4.25
        ExpExp    80      2.81       4.51
        ExpExp    64      2.78       4.20
  ENV_ATTACK_SHARPNESS is now 2.83 and ENV_FALL_SHARPNESS 4.32. The fall was nearly right at the drawn
  copy's 4.0; the RISE was wrong in both copies and by a wide margin.
  THE RIG, for whoever repeats it: EnvADSR modulating a LevMod on a steady 523 Hz OscB into 2-Out,
  captured on QU-24 5/6 and fitted. Do NOT capture the envelope output directly — it is a slow control
  signal and the audio output is AC coupled, which bends the curve being measured. Fit the amplitude of
  a tone the envelope opens instead.
  FOUR TRAPS, all of which bit before the numbers were trustworthy:
    - LevMod's ModType must be 127 or the VCA never closes. At its default 64 the oscillator ran
      straight through at about a quarter level, and the "envelope" was a tone with a bump on it.
    - THE OTHER SLOTS WERE SOUNDING. A note excites every enabled slot, so slot 0's patch was dominating
      the capture; with our own oscillator bypassed the level barely changed, which is what proved it.
      NEWPATCH on the other three slots first.
    - a fixed amplitude threshold for the segment start CLIPS AN EXP ATTACK, which leaves zero slowly,
      and the fitted curvature comes out low. Anchor on the peak instead.
    - fit the segment LENGTH freely and it trades against k — an asymptotic tail sitting in the noise
      looks just like a longer, shallower curve, which is how the decay first "measured" 5.78 s. Pin the
      length to what LinLin measures.
  THE CONTROL SETTING IS WHAT MAKES THE REST BELIEVABLE: LinLin fits a straight line to rms 0.014, so
  the VCA and the capture chain are linear; and its fitted segment came out 3.20 s against
  adr_time_seconds(80)'s 3.208 s, so the time law is confirmed in the same take. k also came out the
  same at two different time dials (2.78 at 1.02 s segments, 2.81 at 3.20 s), which is what says the
  shape is applied to a normalised progress rather than being a fixed time constant.
  HEARD AND ACCEPTED (CT, 2026-08-24): "Envelope sounds OK". Everything above is a curve fit, so that
  check was the one thing the measurements could not supply themselves - the constants are now both
  measured AND listened to.

MOVING A CABLE FROM ONE CONNECTOR TO ANOTHER — DONE 2026-08-22, and the user who reported it is
right about the modifier. Manual p65: "right-click on a connection and select Disconnect, or
double-click-hold or Ctrl-click on a connection... and 'pull out' the connector by dragging the
connector symbol away from the input/output and release the mouse button. If you place the
'disconnected' plug on another connection instead, the cable will be rerouted."
  CTRL-CLICK on a connector now picks up the cable already there and drags its FAR end, so the end
  under the cursor is the one that moves. Everything after that is the ordinary drag: release on a
  connector and the cable is re-routed, release on nothing and it is gone. A connector with no cable
  falls through and draws a new one, exactly as an unmodified click does.
  IT IS A DELETE PLUS THE EXISTING CONNECT, both done at RELEASE inside handle_cable_connect()'s undo
  bracket rather than at press. That is what makes it one undo step, and it has to happen before the
  input-already-has-a-cable test or dropping a cable back on its own input would be refused by its
  own presence. The old to-end is recoloured on the way out, the way a module delete recolours what
  it orphans.
  VERIFIED on a two-module patch: Ctrl-drag Clk -> Rst moved the cable (to=2:0 became to=2:1, still
  ONE cable, not two — the device treats a write as an add, so a re-route that forgot the delete
  would have left both); Ctrl-drag to empty canvas removed it; Undo put it back where it was.
  THE PICKED-UP CABLE IS HIDDEN WHILE THE DRAG IS IN FLIGHT (CT reported it still showing in its old
  position, 2026-08-22, and confirmed the fix). It still EXISTS until release — that is what keeps
  the re-route to one undo step — so render_cables() skips it in both of its passes instead. Leaving
  it drawn showed the cable in its old socket while the rubber band drew the new one, which reads as
  a second cable being made rather than this one being moved; the manual's "pull out the connector"
  is what it should look like, the cable leaving its socket and following the cursor.
  NOT IMPLEMENTED: the manual's other route into the same gesture, double-click-hold. Ctrl-click is
  the one the user asked for and the one that needs no click-timing machinery.
  MULTIPLE CABLES ON ONE CONNECTOR — WE ARE CURRENTLY WRONG, and the reference is unambiguous about
  what should happen. I had assumed the original faced the same ambiguity; it does not, because it
  never picks a cable at all.
  ITS MODEL IS HOLE-TO-HOLE, NOT CABLE-BY-CABLE. CCableDragDropManager::Drop() dispatches three
  actions, all of them in terms of HOLES: InternalCableDisconnect(hole), InternalCableConnect(holeA,
  holeB) and InternalCableMove(holeA, holeB). The move goes to
  CPatchData::GetMoveCableNodeMolecules(), which does CTree::find() on the connector's node and then
  RE-PARENTS THE WHOLE NODE: it emits a delete plus a connect for the cable to the node's PARENT, and
  then loops over get_child() emitting a delete plus a connect for EVERY CHILD. So moving a connector
  moves EVERYTHING PLUGGED INTO IT, as one operation.
  So the answer to "which cable?" is "all of them". For an input there is only ever one and our
  behaviour is already right; for an output carrying three, ours moves one at random and should move
  all three.
  AGREED WITH CT 2026-08-22: MOVE ALL OF THEM, and where the target cannot take them all — an input
  accepts exactly one — ABORT THE WHOLE MOVE AND PUT THEM BACK WHERE THEY WERE. All-or-nothing. (CT
  first said "move what fits", then changed to abort, which is the safer of the two: a partial move
  leaves the patch in a state nobody asked for and has to be unpicked by hand.) The original's tree
  model cannot express the case at all, so this is our rule rather than a recovered one — and abort
  is the easier one to get right, because validating every cable BEFORE deleting any is the natural
  shape of the code.
  BUILT 2026-08-22 — BUT NOT VERIFIED, see below. What went in:
    - gCableDrag records the picked-up HOLE (module index, io count, direction) instead of one cable
      key; the rubber band draws from the first far end it finds.
    - render_cables() skips EVERY cable touching that hole, not just the one — otherwise the
      untouched ones stay drawn in their old position, which is the bug CT reported on the single
      cable version.
    - handle_cable_connect() finds the target connector FIRST, then for each cable on the hole builds
      the new key by substituting the moved end, keeping the rule that the TO end is always an input
      and linkType carries the FROM end's direction (both-ends-output is invalid, both-ends-input is
      a legal white link). VALIDATE THEM ALL FIRST: if any one cannot be made, change nothing.
    - Dropping on nothing still disconnects — all of them now, not one.
    - It all stays inside the one undo bracket, so the whole move is a single undo step.
    - ONE DRAGGED LINE PER CABLE while the drag is in flight (CT: "seem to work, but we only render
      one of them", 2026-08-22). The rubber band was drawn from the single far end recorded in
      fromModuleKey, which is only there to give the drag a starting point; with a whole hole picked
      up, every cable on it is in flight and each now draws its own line, in its own colour.
  VERIFIED ON HARDWARE by CT 2026-08-22: the move itself, the abort path (a two-cable output dropped
  on a single input changes nothing), Undo restoring the whole move in one step, and dropping on
  empty canvas disconnecting all of them. The one fault found was cosmetic and is fixed — only one
  dragged line was drawn while several cables were in flight.
  (Written blind, because every attempt of mine to drive it failed for an unrelated reason: System Events stopped
  being able to see the app's window part way through (the same fault that appeared on 2026-08-21
  when the machine was not logged in), and with no window, cliclick's clicks go nowhere. Three
  "failures" I recorded against this code were that, not the code: a diagnostic LOG_INFO in the press
  handler never printed once, which is what gave it away.
  SO THIS NEEDS A REAL TEST BEFORE IT IS TRUSTED, and note it also rewrote the SINGLE-cable path that
  WAS verified on 2026-08-22 (Clk -> Rst moved the cable, empty canvas removed it, Undo restored it).
  Both need re-running:
    - all four of those cases have since been run by CT and pass.)

CONNECTOR HIT AREAS ARE EXACTLY THE DRAWN CIRCLE — measured 2026-08-22 after CT asked whether they
feel too small. They do, and here is why: the click region registered for a connector IS the
rectangle render_circle_part() drew, so the target scales with the zoom and nothing else.
      zoom 200%   35.0 pt        zoom  50%    8.8 pt
      zoom 100%   17.5 pt        zoom  25%    4.4 pt
  17.5 pt at 100% is fine. At 50% it is 8.8 and at 25% it is 4.4, which is where "too small" comes
  from — and cable work is exactly what you zoom out to do.
  FIXED 2026-08-22 with CT's suggestion — ONE POINT of padding on every side, hit area only, the
  circle still drawn at CONNECTOR_SIZE. A constant in screen points rather than a scaled fraction, so
  it is worth +46% of target at 25% zoom where it is needed and only +11% at 100% where it is not.
  ONE POINT IS THE CEILING, and that is what makes the modest number the right one: the Gate's
  stacked input pairs leave 7 pt between edges at 100% but 1.8 pt at 25%, so a point a side stays
  clear everywhere except 25% zoom, where they overlap by 0.2 pt — under half a retina pixel. Three
  points a side, which was the other candidate, would have made neighbouring connectors genuinely
  fight, and the registry resolves an overlap by taking the most recently registered: one of a
  stacked pair would have become unreachable. The nearest-connector-within-a-tolerance idea is
  therefore not needed.
  The padded rectangle is stored in connector[].rectangle, which is used for the click region, for
  handle_cable_connect()'s release target and for the hover test and nothing else — so press, drop
  and hover all grew together. The cable end is drawn from .coord, which is untouched.
  PROVED BY A/B against the previous build at exactly the one-point margin. The connector ring spans
  fb x 33..66, so its drawn edge is screen x 74 and x 75 is one point outside it. Same gesture, same
  coordinate, two builds: WITHOUT the padding the press fell through to the module body and left the
  module selected; WITH it, no selection — the press reached the connector.
  Finding a valid test took three attempts and the failures are worth remembering: counting changed
  pixels cannot tell a cable drag from a module drag, and checking whether the module MOVED does not
  work either, because a press on a module body only SELECTS it. The yellow selection border is the
  signal that separates the two cases.
THE ORIGINAL EDITOR'S RESOURCE FILE HOLDS EVERY MODULE FACE AS PLAIN TEXT — surveyed 2026-08-22.
  194 module definitions, each listing every control on the face with its position, its parameter
  index (CodeRef) and its type id (InfoFunc): 502 Knob, 517 Input, 352 Output, 195 Led, 264 TextField
  readouts, 235 ButtonText, 170 ButtonFlat, 75 ButtonIncDec, 32 radio groups, 40 MiniVU meters, 46
  Graph, 31 PartSelector, plus Text/Line/Symbol/Bitmap decoration. Module blocks also carry Height,
  Tooltip and a Version number.
  AN AUDIT OF OUR TABLES AGAINST IT matched 168 modules by name and found remarkably little wrong:
  1 height, 4 connector-count and 7 param-count differences, and of those, ClkDiv/ClkDivFix/CompLev/
  Delay/LevMult appear TWICE in the resource (two faces each), which accounts for all four connector
  differences and Delay's height on its own. SeqNote's 39-vs-37 is the known custom-data pair and the
  resource confirms 37. Left over and worth an eye: EnvD and EnvH (we have 2 params, it has 1),
  FltClassic and OscShpB (we draw an LED where it has none), EnvADSR (2 LEDs vs 1).
  Gate and FlipFlop "missing params" are not missing: their selectors are MODES, and the resource
  numbers modes in the same CodeRef space as parameters.

  LED GROUPING — INVESTIGATED AND FIXED 2026-08-22, see below. Kept here because the reference is
  what settled it.
  TWO THINGS IT COULD SETTLE THAT WE CURRENTLY GUESS AT:
  1. LED GROUPING, which bears directly on the LED work above. Every Led carries a Type of "Green"
     (105 of them) or "Sequencer" (90), and a GroupId. The Green/Sequencer split is the 0x39 2-bit
     stream versus the 0x3a multi stream — Compress's ten and the Seq modules' sixteen are
     "Sequencer", which is why our LED counts differ there and why we handle them as volume meters
     instead. That part of ours is right.
     THE GROUPID IS THE PART TO CHECK. The original walks GROUPS, not LEDs: AddBlinkModule() pushes a
     module once per LED group, and CPanel::Blink() then collects every LED view in that group and,
     where there is more than one, spreads the value across them A BIT AT A TIME. Invert has two LEDs
     in two groups (0 and 1) so it takes two stream indices, exactly as we assume — but 8Counter has
     EIGHT LEDs all in group 0, which is ONE index carrying eight bits, where we now consume eight.
     BinCounter, ADConv, Mux8to1, Mux1to8 and Mux8to1X are the same shape. If that reading is right,
     every LED after one of those modules in the stream is off by seven, which is a candidate for
     "the Invert module's LEDs don't seem to do anything" (CT, 2026-08-22) whenever such a module
     sits earlier in the patch. Wants a capture to confirm before changing anything.
  2. ICONS. The image-based controls carry their pixels inline as colon-separated hex, with
     ImageWidth and ImageCount; they decode cleanly (the Gate's PartSelector is six 90x26 line
     drawings, AND/NAND/OR/NOR/XOR/XNOR, each a gate outline). They are simple enough to REDRAW in
     our own style rather than embed — which also covers the oscillator and LFO waveform pickers, the
     other 25 image radio groups, and the Gate, whose selector should be a symbol rather than the
     text menu we give it.

LEDs on invert might not be working at all.
  ANSWERED — see "DOES THE INVERT MODULE'S LED ACTUALLY DO ANYTHING?" below. Short version: steady is
  correct for an unpatched inverter, and LedGroups.pch2 now drives one so it visibly blinks.
Check my LEDs fix in parse_led_data
  REVIEWED 2026-08-21. The per-LED loop is right — 8 is exactly the largest LED count in
  ledLocationList (Mux8to1/Mux1to8/Mux8to1X/BinCounter/ADConv/8Counter), and no module type mixes
  ledTypePark rows with real ones, so the renderer's ledIndex and the parser's l still agree. Three
  things fixed on top: the exit(1) went (it exits a RELEASE build too, and its LOG_DEBUG prints
  nothing there — LOG_ERROR + EXIT_IN_DEBUG + drop only the surplus instead, per defs.h's own note);
  the startIndex + 40 window went, which was only ever safe while one module consumed one value
  however many LEDs it had (see the reference note below for what replaced it); and
  render_led_common() bounds ledIndex the same way the parser does. Checked against the connected G2
  with LogicMidi: 8Counter draws eight separate LEDs, and Invert's read green while Gate's read black
  — which is the patch, not a fault. That Gate is an AND with nothing driving it, so it sits low and
  both its LEDs are correctly off; the Invert beside it goes high on a low input. Gate LEDs confirmed
  working by CT.

  THEN THE WIRE FORMAT TURNED UP IN THE REFERENCE, in CSynthPort::InterruptHandleBlinkData(), and it
  answers three things at once:
    - PACKING. Four values per byte, lowest bits first, each value read as (byte >> 2k) & 3 with its
      own bits in order. Our reverse_bits_in_byte() + MSB-first read put the VALUES in the right
      order but transposed the two bits WITHIN each one — and render_led_common()'s bit0-red/bit1-
      green mapping was the mirror image of that, so the two errors CANCELLED and the LEDs have
      always looked right. Both sides now match the reference (extraction by direct indexing, green
      on bit 0), which is provably the same picture — verified pixel-for-pixel against a capture of
      the old build on live device data, every LED identical — but neither half now depends on the
      other being wrong. It also stops us writing into the receive buffer.
    - THE WINDOW IS A CONSTANT 40, NOT startIndex + 40. The original walks startIndex..0x28 and
      stops, and UpdateBlink() gives each area Min(itsLeds, 0x28 - used), so 40 is the whole index
      space for VA and FX TOGETHER — a patch with more LEDs than that has the surplus unreported by
      the instrument. LED_STREAM_SIZE in defs.h.
    - THE PER-LED MODEL IS CONFIRMED. AddBlinkModule() pushes a module once per LED group and
      UpdateBlink() counts consecutive repeats to get the index within the module, which is exactly
      what the fix does. VA-before-FX is confirmed too: CPatch::UpdateBlink calls GetBkg(1) then
      GetBkg(0).
  Still an assumption, but now only one and only in one place: WHICH bit is green. If a module whose
  LED is known to be red ever shows green, swap the two lines in render_led_common() and nothing else.
  Also dropped tLed's ledRef and rectangle — both write-only, and with value[] an array a single
  ledRef could only hold whichever LED was drawn last. (tVolume still has the same write-only
  volumeRef.)
Check my maximum modes mod in parse_module_list()
  REVIEWED 2026-08-21, and the guard was doing more harm than the case it guards. exit(1) on
  modeCount > MAX_NUM_MODES takes the editor down in Release as well as Debug, and LOG_MODULE_DATA is
  compiled out in EVERY configuration (no target defines ENABLE_LOG_MODULE_DATA), so it left no
  message at all. Now LOG_ERROR + EXIT_IN_DEBUG, and the surplus mode values are still READ so the
  bit stream stays aligned and the rest of the patch parses; write_module_list() writes a defined 0
  for anything past MAX_NUM_MODES rather than reading off the end of mode[].
  THE BIGGER POINT: 16 -> 2 removed slack that several loops were relying on without checking. The
  fullest type in modeLocationList has 2 rows, so ONE added row would have overflowed module->mode[],
  sModeClickCtx's last dimension, and messageContent.moduleData.mode[]. module_mode_count() now clamps
  centrally (and says so), and create_module_at()'s table walk is bounded too. Max modeCount over all
  12 PatchTestFiles is 2 (LogicMidi's Gate), so 2 fits everything we have — but that is 12 patches,
  not the module set.
When adding or deleting a new module - even name bar, seqval sequencer data seems to update randomly on my test file - SeqOscExp.pch2. Either pane. I had clicked random on the sequencer, if that makes a difference? Moving between variations also seems to randomise.
  NOT REPRODUCED OFFLINE 2026-08-21, which is itself a result: SeqOscExp loaded with no instrument,
  ADDMODULE, PARAMDUMP — the only change was the new module's own line, both SeqVals byte-identical.
  So the local add path (create_module_at -> write_module -> init_params_on_module_all_variations ->
  shift_modules_down) does not do it, and delete_module() memsets the whole record, so a reused index
  cannot inherit the old one's values either.
  SOLVED 2026-08-21 ON HARDWARE, and it was NOT the 0x4d handler I first suspected — that theory is
  recorded here only because the suspicion was reasonable and wrong, and the way it was killed is the
  useful part: a GUI add corrupted the sequences, a BACKDOOR add did not, and the backdoor is the one
  that never sends the module to the device. That isolated it to the round trip. A USB capture then
  showed our writes correctly addressed to the new module, followed by the G2 spontaneously pushing
  sixteen param-changes for module 1:3.
  THE CAUSE WAS A LATCHED PUSH BUTTON. SeqVal parameter 36 is "Rnd", a paramTypePush, and the click
  handler had press and release the wrong way round: it sent 0 on press and 1 on RELEASE, leaving the
  device holding the parameter at 1 for good. That value is stored in the patch, so the G2 acted on it
  again every time it recompiled — which any module add or delete forces — re-randomising all sixteen
  steps and reporting them back. The editor was showing the truth. CT's own "I had clicked random on
  the sequencer, if that makes a difference?" was the whole answer.
  PROVEN BOTH WAYS: clearing param 36 by hand made the next add produce zero param-change traffic;
  setting it back to 1 randomised the sequence within two seconds with no other traffic at all.
  FIXED by firing on PRESS and sending the 0 on RELEASE, so the mouse hold is the pulse width. The
  two must not be sent back to back — a 1 and 0 in the same millisecond leave the device seeing the
  release before it acts on the trigger, and nothing happens; 1 ms apart is enough, so no real click
  can hit it. Verified end to end: Rnd randomises once, param 36 ends at 0, and a later module add
  leaves both sequences untouched.
  THE OTHER paramTypePush BUTTONS were checked on hardware by CT 2026-08-22 and behave — SeqVal's
  "Clr" and the rest of the Seq family share the handler and none of them latches.
The Gate module's face needs work — its type selector should be a SYMBOL, not a text menu. The
original draws it as an image picker (a PartSelector) holding six 90x26 line drawings of the gates
themselves: AND, NAND, OR, NOR, XOR, XNOR, each an outline with its label. We draw two dropdowns of
words instead. The images decode cleanly from the resource file, and are simple enough to redraw in
our own style rather than embed — the same goes for the oscillator and LFO waveform pickers and the
other 25 image radio groups. See the resource-file survey at the top.

  MORE MECHANISMS IN THE WRONG FILE, surveyed 2026-08-24 straight after the backdoor move. None is
  urgent and none is a bug; they are listed in the order the case for moving them is strongest.
  1. THE WAVEFORM MATHS, and this one is not just placement — it is DUPLICATED. moduleGraphics.c
     1286-2168 (~880 lines, a third of the file) is the sample functions for OscShpB/LfoShpA/LfoB,
     render_wave_icon() and the three graph renderers. It already behaves like a module: four
     functions are exported through moduleGraphics.h and only menus.c calls them. Meanwhile the
     measured laws exist TWICE, character for character —
       moduleGraphics.c:1346  b = 0.25 - (0.255 * shape) + (0.060 * shape * shape)
       soundEngine.c:3150     b = 0.25 - (0.255 * shape) + (0.060 * shape * shape)
       moduleGraphics.c:1372  d = 0.5 - (0.51 * shape) + (0.026 * shape * shape)
       soundEngine.c:3171     d = 0.5 - (0.51 * shape) + (0.026 * shape * shape)
     — which is the shared platform-free file the wave section at the top of this file already
     argued for, and which paramCurves.c exists to make unnecessary. The sample functions are pure
     maths with no GL, exactly like paramCurves.c, so the split is available. Constant sets already
     differ by one (the drawn saws carry peak = 0.97, the engine has no counterpart), so the drift

     remaining wave modules land, not after — every module added writes the same law out twice.

  3. BANK AND FOLDER BACKUP/RESTORE inside usbComms.c — 1807-2742 plus 3399-3526, roughly 1060 lines
     of filename building, Patches.txt manifest parsing, folder walking and text-file synth-settings
     backup. It sits there because it runs on the USB thread, but it is a filesystem mechanism, and
     SYNTHEDIT HAS ALREADY MADE THIS EXACT SPLIT: SynthEdit/src/synthBackup.c is the same subsystem
     in its own file. The strongest precedent argument of the group.
  4. THE THREE SETTINGS PANEL RENDERERS, graphics.c 1136-1655 (~520 lines). Their resource tables

     (gNoteLines, gNoteScrollLine, gNoteTextX and the rest). Those statics are the tell: since the

AN OFFLINE EDITOR MAKES A CAPTURE THAT LOOKS PERFECTLY VALID — and this one bit, 2026-08-24, costing
an OscB waveform sweep that was analysed for some time before anyone doubted it. Two copies of the
editor were running, mine had lost the USB claim, and so:
  - DEVADDMODULE, CABLE and every DEVSET in the sweep updated the LOCAL database and posted to a USB
    thread with nothing on the other end. Each returned OK, because each had done its local half.
  - measure.py printed all six settings and wrote its .wav and .json.
  - DUMP showed exactly the patch that was asked for — because DUMP reads the same local database.
  - the file was silence at -100 dBFS, and the first sign of trouble was an autocorrelation of 0.04.
FIXED, both halves: the backdoor gained a COMMS command reporting online/offline, and measure.py now
asks it before recording and refuses to start otherwise, naming the second-copy case in the error
because the editor's own top bar cannot distinguish it — "Offline" means both "no G2 plugged in" and
"another copy has the claim".
THE GENERAL SHAPE OF THIS, worth remembering beyond the G2: a command that does a local half and a
remote half, and reports on the local half, will report success through a total failure of the remote
one. Anything driving hardware needs a way to ask whether the hardware is there, and needs to ask.

A SECOND INSTANCE SPINS ON LIBUSB_ERROR_ACCESS INSTEAD OF BACKING OFF — seen 2026-08-24, worth a
retry delay. Two copies of G2-Edit running and one G2 plugged in: the copy that loses the race logged
  E open_and_claim_device() Failed to claim interface: LIBUSB_ERROR_ACCESS
  D close_device() Device closed
290 times in a few seconds, and while it did so it STOPPED SERVICING THE BACKDOOR — command files sat
unread until the process ended. So the cost is not just a noisy log: a harness run driving the second
instance simply hangs, with nothing anywhere saying why. A claim failure that is ACCESS (someone else
holds it) is not the same as a transient one and should back off rather than retry flat out; the
top-bar state could say so too, since "Offline" is what it shows whether the device is absent or
merely taken.

When saving file, file name (path) needs adding to top of recents list.
  DONE 2026-08-22. recent_files_add() was only ever called from on_file_opened(), so a patch written
  with Save As — the file you are most likely to want back — was the one thing the menu never listed.
  Both save routes converge on on_file_saved() (the browser's callback, and File > Save going
  straight to the remembered path), so the call goes there and covers both; recent_files_add() moves
  an existing entry to the front and returns early when it is already there, so repeated saves cost
  nothing. VERIFIED through the GUI: saved a new name through the Save File browser and it appeared
  at the top of File > Open Recent.
  The backdoor's SAVEFILE deliberately does NOT go through this path and so does not touch recents —
  it is a test hook and should not write into the user's list.
SW1-2 and similar need something new. See manual. The offset box seems to show 0 for first (leftmost) option box, 4 for the next box. Boxes also have names, currently displayed above our menu option. Might need a new param type. New type could also display the offset?
  CONFIRMED 2026-08-22, and your reading is right on both counts. The manual's "Common Switch
  parameters" (p221) calls these CHANNEL SELECT RADIO BUTTONS: one button per channel, blue when
  selected, all others off. "These Channel Select buttons can also be labelled for better overview.
  Right-click on a Channel Select button and select 'Edit name'... the name cannot be longer than 7
  characters because of the size of the ASSIGNABLE DISPLAYS on the synth front panel." They cannot be
  assigned to a Morph Group.
  THE OFFSET BOX IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME FEATURE, and it is not decoration: the Switch modules
  have a Ctrl OUTPUT whose value is the selected channel, and the box displays it. The manual gives
  the rule outright — "value 0 for the initial state, value 4 for the next state, value 8 for the next
  and so on", max 28 at button 8 — because the Mux modules decode 0<4 as channel 1, 4<8 as channel 2
  and so on. So a Switch button always lands on the same Mux channel whatever the button count. That
  is exactly the 0 then 4 you saw. It is DERIVED from the selector, not a parameter of its own.
  WE ALREADY HAVE THE DATA MODEL. parse_param_names() reads a COUNT of labels per parameter into
  paramNumLabels[] and fills paramName[param][label][7] for each — up to MAX_NUM_LABELS — and
  write_param_names() writes them back out. Nothing in the renderer uses more than label 0, and
  action_rename_param_label() only ever edits label 0. So the work is a new param type plus a rename
  that takes a label index; the protocol side is done.
  A SECOND, SEPARATE CONTROL exists that is NOT renamable: a plain radio row with fixed captions or
  waveform icons, used by ~26 other modules (FltNord's LP/BP/HP/BR, FltClassic 12/18/24, the Out/In
  bus selectors, the oscillator and LFO waveform pickers, and so on). Same widget shape, no per-button
  names, no Ctrl offset. Worth building the renamable one first and having the plain one fall out of
  it, but they are not the same control and the plain ones do not want an Edit name entry.

  RENAMABLE (Channel Select) — 6 modules, always parameter 0:
      Sw1-2   2 cols x 1 row    Out 1, Out 2
      Sw1-4   4 x 1             Out 1..Out 4
      Sw1-8   4 x 2             Out 1..Out 8
      Sw2-1   2 x 1             In 1, In 2
      Sw4-1   4 x 1             In 1..In 4
      Sw8-1   4 x 2             In 1..In 8
  Note the 8-button ones are TWO ROWS OF FOUR, not a row of eight — a module face is not wide enough.

  PLAIN radio rows — 26 more, no renaming, listed as module/param/buttons:
      OscB 8/5*    OscA 4/6*     OscShpA 9/6*   Rect 0/4*      ShpStatic 0/4*
      LfoA 4/6*    LfoB 4/4*     LfoShpA 11/6*  (* = waveform ICONS, not text)
      OutBusB 0/3 (1/2, 3/4, CVA)      2-Out 0/6 (1/2, 3/4 x3)   4-Out 0/3 (Out, Fx, Bus)
      2-In 0/4 (1/2, 3/4 x2)           4-In 0/2 (In, Bus)        Fx-In 0/2 (1/2, 3/4)
      FltClassic 4/3 (12, 18, 24)      FltMulti 5/2 (6, 12)      FltStatic 2/3 (LP, BP, HP)
      FltNord 8/4 (BR, HP, BP, LP - VERTICAL) and FltNord 5/2 (12, 24)
      ShelvEQ 2/2 (Hi, Lo - VERTICAL)  DrumSynth 10/3 (HP, BP, LP - VERTICAL)
      LevConv 1/3 (BiPol, Pos, Neg)    PeakFollow 1/2 (Pos, Neg)  MonoKey 0/3 (Last, Lo, Hi)
      ShpExp 3/4 (x2..x5)              Saturate 3/4 (1, 2, 3, 4)
  Three of them are VERTICAL, so the type needs an orientation as well as a button count.

  IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-22 — paramTypeRadioEdit, the renamable half. The six Switch rows now use it.
    - radio_columns()/radio_rows() DERIVE the grid from the button count (up to four across, then
      wrap), so 2 is 2x1, 4 is 4x1 and 8 is 4x2 with nothing tabulated.
    - Buttons are as wide as the WIDEST caption in the group, so a renamed one grows the group
      instead of clipping. Widths use eNoCache — a renamed caption is a runtime buffer and the width
      cache is keyed on the string POINTER.
    - Rename is offered as one menu entry per button, captioned with what that button currently
      reads ("Rename \"Out 3\""). The manual has you right-click the button itself, but the group is a
      single click region and the right-click path is a registry QUERY with no captured rectangle to
      divide up; naming them in the menu avoids keeping a second record of where each box was drawn,
      and makes a 4x2 group renamable without hitting a small box exactly.
    - Renaming one button fills in the rest from what they already read, because the wire format
      sends a parameter's names as a SET — send only the one and the instrument is told an 8-button
      group has one caption. send_set_param_label() now writes every label of every named parameter;
      it only ever wrote label 0, which nothing had needed until now.
    - Sw8-1's captions were sw8to1SelStrMap ("Lo, 64', 32'...", organ footages from somewhere else)
      and are now In 1..In 8 like its five siblings.
    - The group no longer takes name 0 as its own label; that name is button 0's caption and was
      being drawn twice.
  THE SELECTED BUTTON IS RGB_GREEN_ON, the same green every other "this one is on" control in the
  app uses — offOnColourMap's on state, the topbar's active control, the slot selector, the MIDI CC
  list. It was a blue of its own at first because the manual describes the original's selected
  channel as blue; consistency inside our own UI is worth more than matching the original's palette,
  so the blue and its define are gone (CT, 2026-08-22).

  SIZING, settled 2026-08-22 after CT found two faults with the first version:
    - FIXED WIDTH, not fitted to the current captions. It measured the widest caption it was showing,
      so the group changed size — and its click regions moved — the moment a button was renamed, and
      changed back if the new name was shorter. It is now measured against RADIO_WIDEST_CAPTION, seven
      of the widest glyph, that being the most a name can hold (manual: 7 characters, because of the
      synth's assignable displays).
    - THE SECOND ROW OVERLAPPED THE FIRST on 4x2 groups. draw_button() ADDS DRAW_BUTTON_MARGIN to the
      rect it is handed, so a cell of exactly textHeight drew a button four points taller than its own
      row. The cell now carries the margin and the draw hands over the cell minus it. Note that the
      margin cannot be named from this file — the constant lives in synthlibDefs.h's NON-G2_EDIT
      branch, so draw_button() sees it and we do not; draw_button_bounds() with a zero-sized probe is
      the exposed way to ask.
    - SEVEN WIDE GLYPHS ACROSS FOUR COLUMNS DOES NOT FIT A MODULE, so the cell is capped at the width
      remaining on the face (from the row's own x in the table, which is why these rows must stay
      left-anchored) and a caption too wide for its cell is truncated as it is drawn. That keeps the
      group always-fitting and never-moving, at the cost of clipping a genuinely long name in a
      four-column group. SETTLED: CT chose the widest glyph plus clipping over dropping back to a
      narrower reference character that would have fitted without a cap — stable geometry first, and
      a clipped name is legible enough to identify while a group that moves under the cursor is not.
  THE MULTI-LABEL SET PARAM LABEL MESSAGE IS CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE (CT, 2026-08-22): a renamed
  parameter survives a re-read from the instrument. That was the one change in this batch that could
  WRITE bad data — send_set_param_label() now emits every label of every named parameter where it
  only ever emitted label 0 — and it was the only thing holding up a beta.
  VERIFIED: renders correctly against a real patch (SwTest.pch2, whose Sw1-2 already carries the
  per-button names "Box1"/"Box2" — which also proves the multi-label parse), selected button lit
  blue, no duplicate label.
  VERIFIED BY CLICK 2026-08-22, once the machine was logged in again: clicking either box selects it
  (value 1 -> 0 -> 1), right-click offers Rename "Box1" / Rename "Box2", the box being renamed edits
  in place with a caret, and Return commits — the second button now reads "Right", stays selected,
  and the group re-sizes to the wider caption while button 0 keeps its own name. That last part is
  the whole point of the type: two names on ONE parameter, which nothing in the app had ever stored.
  DRIVING THE GUI FOUND TWO REAL BUGS THAT READING IT DID NOT:
    - THE CLICK REGION WAS REGISTERED IN THE WRONG COORDINATE SPACE. draw_button() returns its rect
      AFTER scale_scroll_adjust_rectangle(), which is the space the click registry works in; the
      renderer was returning a rect it built itself in unadjusted module coordinates. The buttons
      drew in the right place and no click could reach them at any zoom or scroll position. The
      renderer now returns the union of the rects the buttons actually drew at.
    - THE RELEASE HANDLER ASKED FOR A CAPTURE THAT WAS ALREADY GONE. dispatch_click_region() clears
      sCaptureActive BEFORE calling a release handler (deliberately — a handler may re-enter
      dispatch), so click_region_capture_rect() returned false and left the group rect zeroed. Taken
      at press now. Found by reading, before the click test could run.
  FOR THE NEXT GUI TEST, two things cost time here: the app must be FRONTMOST or clicks land on
  whatever window is above it, and `cliclick kp:return` does not deliver a key GLFW recognises —
  `osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke return'` does. Backspace and typing
  through cliclick are fine.
  CTRL OFFSET BOX DONE 2026-08-22. It is not a parameter and could not go in paramLocationList, where
  every row consumes a parameter index, so it has its own table: displayLocationList, in the manner
  of ledLocationList, with a tDisplayType saying what the box is DERIVED from. Ten entries — the six
  Channel Select switches plus SwOnOffM/SwOnOffT/Sw1-2M/Sw2-1M, all of which have a Ctrl output. Each
  face puts that output at {3, -3}, so the box sits at {16, -3} on all ten, clear of the connector's
  own label. It registers no click region: it is a readout, and a dead target there would swallow
  right-clicks meant for the module.
  The value is calculated, not tabulated — selection * 4, per the manual, which is the same rule for
  a two-way switch as an eight-way one. The box is sized for "28", the largest value any Switch can
  show, so it does not change width as the selection moves.
  VERIFIED: Sw1-2 reads 4 with Out 2 selected and 0 with Out 1 selected, tracking the click. An added
  Sw8-1 lays its eight buttons out 4x2 (In 1..In 4 over In 5..In 8) with In 8 lit at parameter 7.

  LAYOUT DONE 2026-08-22. The six rows carried the positions they had as a single menu button, and a
  grid is wider and, for the eight-way ones, twice as tall. All six faces were rendered and adjusted
  by eye until nothing collides: Sw1-4's grid moved off its output row into the empty band above it,
  Sw8-1's off its input connectors to the top of the face, and Sw2-1's off its output — that last one
  I had wrongly called fine from a smaller screenshot, and it needed two passes because the first
  position clipped the title bar. Sw4-1 and Sw1-8 were already clear, but their Ctrl offset boxes
  clipped a connector, so those two moved up a row. Sw1-2 needed nothing.
  Mapping positions across from the reference does NOT work here and was tried first: our faces are
  hand-laid approximations, not proportional copies, so the two coordinate systems do not agree even
  after fitting a scale to them. Rendering and looking is the method.
Add an open recent for patches from bank?
Got a: D parse_command_response() Got unknown sub-command 0x4d - must implement!!! That's SUB_RESPONSE_PARAM_LIST. We had that implemented at some point in the past, but removed it. Maybe needs case and call to parse_param_list() adding to parse_command_response()? Assume it's unsolicited. Have attempted a fix, but not seen it again. Check my fix as mentioned.
  REVIEWED 2026-08-21 — the CASE is right, the OFFSET is unknowable from here, so it now tests before
  it trusts. Inside a patch dump every section carries [type][16-bit length] before its payload
  (parse_patch() consumes it, and the original editor's Format_11::R_Base::Prolog reads exactly that
  for 0x4d too). Whether the device's standalone 0x4d does is NOT settled, and this file's own
  handlers disagree for messages of that shape: SUB_RESPONSE_GLOBAL_KNOBS reads a length first,
  SUB_RESPONSE_KNOBS does not. Guessing wrong is not a no-op — parse_param_list() writes a value into
  every variation of every module it walks, so a header read 16 bits out silently rewrites the whole
  patch rather than failing. parse_param_list_message() now checks the header (location <= 2, module
  count sane, variation count 9/10, or an all-zero empty section) at BOTH candidate offsets, parses at
  whichever validates, and if neither does, logs a hex dump and parses nothing. Whichever way it goes,
  the first real capture answers the question in the log.
  CT reports 2026-08-21 that the sequencer-value corruption has stopped, which makes this handler the
  likely culprit — nothing else changed can reach a parameter value. WHICH WAY IT WENT IS STILL WORTH
  READING, because "fixed" has two meanings here and they want different follow-ups. The framing is
  now announced ONCE per run (and again if it ever changes), at LOG_INFO so it survives a Release
  build:
    "NO section length in front"          -> the original call was correct, and the corruption came
                                             from somewhere else and is only hiding.
    "16-BIT SECTION LENGTH in front"      -> the device does send one, the original was 16 bits early,
                                             and THAT was the corruption. Question closed; the
                                             validator can then be simplified to a plain 16-bit skip.
    "header implausible ... NOT PARSED"   -> a real message is being DROPPED, so params can go stale
                                             against the instrument. This one logs every time, with a
                                             hex dump, because it is the case that still needs
                                             decoding.

Check this is correct in parse_command_response()          case SUB_RESPONSE_PARAM_LIST:
            parse_param_list(slot, buff, bitPos);

  See the note above — now calls parse_param_list_message(), which validates the header first.
  Separately, and found while testing it: parse_param_list()'s "unexpected Variation Count" error was
  firing on 6 of the 12 PatchTestFiles at load. An EMPTY area is written as moduleCount 0, variation
  count 0 and there is nothing wrong with that; the check now only complains when there are actually
  modules in the section. (write_param_list() already writes 9/10 even when empty, so this is about
  what we read, not what we produce.)

Second of these modes is showing wrong menu. Shows correct string, but selection is Sine1, Sine2 etc.

  mode's modeRef landed in mode[0] and mode[1] kept 0 — and modeLocationList[0] is OscShpB's waveform,

  module->mode[0].value reads in the OscWave branch were the same bug. UNVERIFIED BY CLICK — the
  backdoor cannot open a context menu; both G1 and G2 render correctly on the instrument, which is as
