  via glfwWindowHint(GLFW_CLIENT_API, GLFW_NO_API) + glfwGetCocoaWindow() — the documented
  Vulkan/MoltenVK route, and both are present in the bundled GLFW 3.5.1.
  THERE IS NO APPLE OPENGL-TO-METAL SHIM to read or adopt (CT asked). Apple deprecated GL and
  shipped no translation layer; on Apple Silicon the system GL driver is already sitting on Metal
  internally, so the shim is what this runs on today and is not something that can be opened. The
  references that do exist: Apple's "Migrating OpenGL Code to Metal" for vocabulary, ANGLE's Metal
  backend for completeness (but it takes GL ES 2.0+, which has no fixed-function pipeline, so it
  would not accept this code either), and — much the closest — Dear ImGui's imgui_impl_opengl2.cpp
  beside imgui_impl_metal.mm. That pair solves exactly this problem: one batched vertex array of
  position/UV/colour, a single font atlas, scissor rects, an ortho projection, alpha blending, no
  depth, painted back to front. MIT licensed; read it, do not paste it, and if anything is borrowed
  it goes in THIRD_PARTY.md.

- 2026-08-28 — METAL BACKEND: IT DRAWS, AND IT AGREES WITH OPENGL TO 17 PIXELS IN 5,166,528.
  SynthLib/src/renderBackendMetal.m, ~500 lines, the same nine gfx_* calls renderBackendGL.c
  implements. Built with:
      xcodebuild ... GCC_PREPROCESSOR_DEFINITIONS='$(inherited) RENDER_BACKEND=2' \
                     OTHER_LDFLAGS='$(inherited) -framework Metal'
  IT RENDERS OFFSCREEN AND PRESENTS NOTHING, deliberately, and that is the intended intermediate
  state rather than an unfinished one. The frame is drawn into a texture the backend owns, so
  SCREENSHOT returns a real frame while the window stays blank. Two reasons to build it this way
  round: gfx_read_pixels_rgb() cannot reliably read a drawable back once presented, and doing it
  offscreen means THE DRAWING can be proved right against OpenGL before any windowing code exists
  to argue with. Which is exactly what happened — see the defect below.
  WHAT THE COMPARISON FOUND, and it is the whole argument for having built the harness:
    - THE SCISSOR RECTANGLE WAS A ROW OUT. OpenGL truncates the flipped bottom edge and the height
      SEPARATELY, which lands its rect one row lower than truncating the top and bottom edges does.
      Two rows out of 1704, invisible to the eye, and it would have gone unnoticed forever while
      quietly costing the ability to say the backends agree. FIXED IN PORTABLE CODE, not in either
      backend: module_pane_clip_begin() now rounds once and gfx_scissor() takes ints, so the
      rectangle covers exactly the pixels whose top-left corner is inside it and neither backend
      gets a say. This CHANGED THE OPENGL OUTPUT by those same rows (and one column, which only
      shows at zoom 2.0 where modules reach that far) — a deliberate, understood, one-pixel change,
      and the last time these two disagree.
    - THE CAPTURES WERE NOT COMPARABLE AT ALL until the persisted state was pinned. zoomFactor,
      the window geometry and the recent-file list all live in prefs.txt and are restored at
      start-up, so a capture inherited whatever the previous run left behind. This produced a
      "difference" of 2058 pixels that moved between runs of the SAME BINARY. The harness now
      writes a fixed prefs.txt before every launch. ANY FUTURE PIXEL COMPARISON MUST DO THIS.
  WHAT IS LEFT, all 17 pixels of it, measured rather than assumed:
    - 15 pixels differ by +/-1 in one or two channels, all of them on the RED CABLES, which are the
      only geometry drawn with per-vertex interpolated colour (render_bezier_curve's lighting).
      Two different interpolators rounding to 8 bits. Not fixable and not worth trying.
    - 2 pixels differ hard (grey against black): single edge pixels on circle geometry where the
      two rasterizers include or exclude a pixel whose centre lands on a triangle edge. Both APIs
      use top-left fill rules; the vertex positions differ in the last float bits.
    Matching the vertex transform to glOrtho's exact form — one multiply-add per axis with the
    scale and bias computed on the CPU, rather than a divide in the shader — was tried and is kept,
    because it is the right thing to do, but it moved none of these 17.
  THE DESIGN DECISIONS WORTH REMEMBERING:
    - shaders are compiled AT RUNTIME from a string in the .m. A .metallib would need a build step
      in do-vst3 (a shell script, not an Xcode target) and a bundle lookup that differs between the
      app and a sandboxed plug-in. A string costs tens of milliseconds once and behaves identically
      in both.
    - a 1x1 opaque white texture is bound for untextured geometry, so ONE pipeline state covers
      both cases with no branch. White times anything is anything, which is GL_MODULATE.
    - the render target is MTLStorageModeManaged with an explicit synchronizeResource before the
      read-back. Shared is not allowed for a render target on Intel macOS and this ships universal.
    - texture handles are INDICES into a table, because renderBackend.h promises a uint32_t and an
      id<MTLTexture> does not fit in one. Slot 0 is never handed out.
    - the read-back flips rows and swizzles BGRA to RGB, because the contract promises what
      glReadPixels gave: tightly-packed RGB, bottom row first.
    - one command buffer per frame, committed at the next gfx_clear(). Without that every frame
      since the last SCREENSHOT accumulated into one buffer, holding a vertex buffer per submission.
  NEXT, and it is the only thing between here and a Metal build you can actually look at: the
  WINDOW LAYER. A CAMetalLayer, and a blit from the offscreen target to its drawable. For the
  application that means glfwWindowHint(GLFW_CLIENT_API, GLFW_NO_API) + glfwGetCocoaWindow() (both
  present in the bundled GLFW 3.5.1), which also retires glfwSwapBuffers() from all three frame
  loops — so presenting has to join the seam. For the plug-in it means a layer-hosting NSView in
  place of the NSOpenGLView in vst3/g2GlView.m, which DELETES the CGLLockContext / -update /
  surface-notification machinery rather than adding to it.
  DO THE APPLICATION FIRST, which reverses what this file said on 2026-08-27. The plug-in is the
  simpler of the two, but simplicity is not the deciding factor — VERIFIABILITY is. The app has the
  backdoor and can be diffed; the plug-in needs a host and a human.

- 2026-08-28 — G2-EDIT RUNS ON METAL. Not a read-back: the application window, on screen, drawn by
  renderBackendMetal.m, captured with screencapture to prove it. Modules, cables, envelope graphs,
  split panes, scrollbars, menu bar — the lot.
      xcodebuild ... GCC_PREPROCESSOR_DEFINITIONS='$(inherited) RENDER_BACKEND=2' \
                     OTHER_LDFLAGS='$(inherited) -framework Metal -framework QuartzCore'
  THE WINDOW IS STILL GLFW'S. glfwWindowHint(GLFW_CLIENT_API, GLFW_NO_API) — the same hint a Vulkan
  application uses — makes GLFW create the window and deliver the events while creating no context
  and no swap chain. glfwGetCocoaWindow() then hands over the NSWindow and the backend puts a
  CAMetalLayer on its content view. That is the whole macOS windowing change, and it is why this
  route keeps the Windows/Linux door open: GLFW stays the window and input layer everywhere.
  PRESENTING JOINED THE SEAM, and renderBackend.h now says so where it previously said the opposite.
  Presenting looked like a window concern; it is not separable from the API. Under OpenGL it is a
  buffer swap on the context GLFW made, under Metal a blit into a drawable a CAMetalLayer vends
  using the same device the frame was rendered with. No form of words covers both without naming an
  API. So: gfx_attach_window() and gfx_present(), and one new portable call, render_present(),
  which submits the batch and then presents.
  ALL THREE FRAME LOOPS LOST THEIR LAST GLFW CALL. render_backend_flush() followed by
  glfwSwapBuffers() became render_present(), in G2-Edit, SynthEdit and EmuUtility alike. The old
  pair could not survive Metal, where there is no context to swap.
  THE PLUG-IN BROKE THE BUILD, once, and the reason is worth keeping: gfx_present() under OpenGL
  calls glfwSwapBuffers, and THE PLUG-IN HAS NO GLFW — not merely no window, no library. It
  includes renderBackendGL.c for the drawing and gets its GL headers via glfw3.h, but nothing links
  libglfw, so the call was an undefined symbol at link time rather than a harmless no-op. Compiled
  out under G2_VST3_BUILD, which do-vst3 already defines. Nothing is lost: a plug-in draws into a
  view the host presents, so g2GlDraw.c still ends at render_backend_flush() and g2GlView.m calls
  [context flushBuffer].
  THE BLIT, NOT A SECOND RENDER PASS. The frame already exists in the offscreen target; rendering
  it again into the drawable would mean a full-screen quad, another pipeline and a sampler to copy
  pixels that are already right. The layer is framebufferOnly = NO so it can be a blit destination,
  layer-HOSTING rather than layer-backed (assign the layer, then set wantsLayer, or AppKit draws
  over it), and contentsScale is the window's backingScaleFactor or every frame presents at half
  resolution on a Retina display.
  STILL 17 PIXELS IN 5,166,528 against OpenGL, unchanged by any of this — 15 at +/-1 on the
  interpolated-colour cables, 2 rasterizer edge pixels on circles. And the OpenGL build is
  BYTE-IDENTICAL across the render_present() refactor, all six captures.
  WHAT IS NOT DONE:
    - the VST3 plug-in is still OpenGL. It needs a layer-hosting NSView in place of the
      NSOpenGLView in vst3/g2GlView.m, and -framework Metal in do-vst3's link line. That DELETES
      the CGLLockContext / -update / surface-notification machinery rather than adding to it.
    - no resize handling for the CAMetalLayer beyond gfx_set_surface() keeping drawableSize in step
      with the target. Worth a real drag-resize test before trusting it.
    - Metal is not the default and should not become one until the plug-in follows and the whole
      thing has been used in anger for a while. renderBackendSelect.h is the one-line switch.
    - vsync under Metal is whatever CAMetalLayer does by default; the GL path explicitly asked for
      glfwSwapInterval(1). Not investigated.

- 2026-08-28 — THE VST3 PLUG-IN EDITOR RUNS ON METAL TOO, and there is now a test host in the
  repository to prove it with.
      G2_VST3_METAL=1 ./do-vst3          (OpenGL remains the default)
  THE TEST HOST IS COMMITTED THIS TIME. tools/vst3host.mm + tools/do-vst3host. It has been written
  twice before and lost twice, both times because it was left in a scratchpad — see the older note
  in this file that says exactly that. It loads the bundle, instantiates the COMPONENT as well as
  the controller (the component's initialize() is what loads the patch; a harness that skips it
  gets a correct-looking editor drawing an empty database), asks for the editor view and puts it in
  a window it owns. --seconds and --shot make it scriptable, so a plug-in rendering change is
  diffed exactly like an application one. It reports whether IPluginFactory2 is present, because
  its absence is what Ableton rejected the plug-in for and the previous harness could not see it.
  ONE FILE, FOUR #ifs. g2GlView.m is ~520 lines of which about thirty differ between backends: the
  superclass, the pixel format, prepareOpenGL/reshape, and the body of -drawRect:. The other 450
  are mouse, keyboard, tracking areas and the drag timer, and none of them care what draws.
  Duplicating all that to avoid four conditionals would have been a bad trade and the copies would
  have drifted.
  THREE BUGS, AND NONE OF THEM WOULD HAVE BEEN FOUND BY LOOKING AT IT:
    1. LAYER-HOSTING KILLED THE EDITOR STONE DEAD. Assigning view.layer and then setting wantsLayer
       tells AppKit the layer's contents ARE the view's, so it stops calling -drawRect: entirely —
       and -drawRect: is how every redraw in this view happens, since they all arrive as
       -setNeedsDisplay:. Blank window, no error. The application does not care because its render
       loop drives every frame and nothing waits to be asked. So: a WINDOW gets the layer assigned
       (application), a VIEW gets it as a SUBLAYER and stays layer-backed (plug-in).
    2. THE LAYER WAS RESAMPLING THE WHOLE FRAME. A host asked for 1367x768 and the view came out
       768.9 POINTS tall; convertRectToBacking truncates 1537.8 pixels to 1537, which is what gets
       rendered — and Core Animation then scaled that 1537-pixel frame into a 1537.8-pixel box.
       A 0.9995 resample never looks broken, just faintly soft. It was 6.5% of pixels and blurred
       every glyph. The layer is now sized at drawableSize/contentsScale so the mapping is exactly
       1:1; it can sit a fraction of a point short of the view, which is invisible, where a
       resampled frame is not.
    3. CORE ANIMATION WAS COLOUR-MATCHING THE LAYER. A CAMetalLayer is colour-managed by default;
       an NSOpenGLView's pixels go to the screen as written. RGB_GREEN_ON arrived as (75,179,87)
       where OpenGL wrote (77,178,77) — invisible, but a real difference in what the user sees, and
       it would have made every future screen-level comparison useless. gLayer.colorspace = nil.
  THE NUMBERS: 99.99% of pixels differing, then 6.5%, then 2.5%, then 0.001% — 58 pixels in
  4,199,424, the same residual class as the application (rasterizer edges and the interpolated
  cable colours). AND NOTE THIS IS A SCREEN CAPTURE, not a framebuffer read-back: it includes
  compositing and colour management, so it is a stricter test than the application's SCREENSHOT
  comparison, which is why bug 3 showed up here and not there.
  UNCHANGED, RE-VERIFIED: the OpenGL plug-in is byte-identical to before this work, and the
  application's OpenGL build is byte-identical across all six captures with Metal still at 17
  pixels. The OpenGL plug-in is what is installed in ~/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3.
  STILL TO DO:
    - a real host. The harness is not Ableton, and the notes above say why that distinction has
      already cost this project once.
    - resize. Neither the application's CAMetalLayer nor the plug-in's has been drag-resized; the
      plug-in's layer is re-sized from gfx_set_surface() on every frame, which should cover it, but
      "should" is not a test.
    - vsync under Metal is whatever CAMetalLayer does by default.

- DONE 2026-08-29 — TEXT ON NON-RETINA DISPLAYS (raised by CT 2026-08-28 as the worst of the 1x
  rendering). Measured on a real 1280x720 display, diagnosed, fixed and verified; the fix and its
  numbers are further down this entry. The heading said OPEN until 2026-08-29 and no longer does.
  METAL DOES NOT HELP AND NEITHER WOULD MSAA. The Metal port renders the identical picture — 17
  pixels in 5,166,528 for the application, 58 in 4,199,424 for the plug-in — so this is not a
  backend question. MSAA is worth nothing here either: glyphs are TEXTURED QUADS and multisampling
  antialiases geometry edges only. It would help the dial rims and cables; it would not touch text.
  ALREADY RULED OUT: the atlas is not being clamped and rescaled. GLYPH_ATLAS_MIN_PX is 4 and a 1x
  window draws text at about 4.6px and 6.6px, so atlas_for_height() returns the requested size and
  the 1:1 blit still holds.
  MEASURED 2026-08-28 ON A REAL 1280x720 DISPLAY, and the complaint is correct: text is about 14%
  smaller than proportion demands. Same "Patch Mode" button, same patch, ink height against its own
  box height:
      framebuffer 884  (scale 0.69)   box 13px   ink 5px   ink/box 0.385
      framebuffer 1120 (scale 0.88)   box 16px   ink 8px   ink/box 0.500
      framebuffer 3032 (scale 2.37)   box 38px   ink 20px  ink/box 0.526
  TEXT'S SHARE OF ITS BOX CLIMBS WITH THE RENDERING SCALE and converges on the Retina value. That
  is the shape of a QUANTISATION error, not a constant factor — which rules out a plain scaling bug
  and rules in the rounding.
  TWO CANDIDATE CAUSES TESTED AND BOTH ARE INNOCENT, so do not spend the time again:
    - FreeType size truncation. FT_Set_Pixel_Sizes takes (FT_UInt)fontSize, so it looked like a
      one-line bug. Instrumented: fontSize comes out EXACTLY 6.000 and 8.000 at 1x, because
      gCanonEmPx equals GLYPH_REFERENCE_PX. Nothing is truncated.
    - Hinting. FT_LOAD_TARGET_LIGHT snaps vertically, which is the axis losing the height. Swapped
      for FT_LOAD_NO_HINTING and rebuilt: cap height stayed at exactly 5px. Not it.
  WHAT IS LEFT is the design's own 1:1 rule. pixelHeight = lround(rectangle.size.h) picks an INTEGER
  em, the glyph bitmaps come out at whatever integer size that rasterizes to, and they are blitted
  at that size with no correction toward the height actually asked for. At an em of 6 one pixel is
  17%; at 21 it is 5%. The sweep above is exactly that curve. Going 6px -> 8px em moved the ink from
  5px to 8px, a 60% jump for one integer step.
  DONE 2026-08-29 — SUPERSAMPLED SMALL TEXT. Below a drawn height of GLYPH_SUPERSAMPLE_BELOW_PX
  (12) the atlas is rasterized at GLYPH_SUPERSAMPLE_FACTOR (2) times the size and the glyph quads
  are drawn scaled down, linear-filtered, at FRACTIONAL positions. Above it nothing changes: the
  crisp one-texel-per-pixel blit and the whole-pixel pen are exactly as they were.
  THE TRIGGER IS THE DRAWN HEIGHT, NOT THE DISPLAY, and that was CT's idea improved rather than
  taken as stated. "Retina or not" is a proxy: gGlobalGuiScale runs continuously with window size
  (0.69, 0.88 and 2.37 all measured on one machine), so a zoomed-out canvas on a Retina display
  draws 6px text and wants the same treatment. A size test needs no platform query, is local to
  atlas_for_height(), and covers that case for free.
  THE SCALE IS CORRECTED FOR THE ROUNDING, not merely halved: glyphScale is
  (rectangle.size.h / pixelHeight) / supersample, so the drawn height is the one actually asked
  for rather than the integer the atlas was built at. That removes the last of the quantisation
  instead of just reducing it.
  IT FORCED THE FILTER PARAMETER utilsGraphics.h had been deferring. That header said there was no
  need for one "because both callers blit one texel per pixel" and that it should be added "then,
  with the case in front of you". This is that case: point-sampling a 2x atlas would throw half the
  texels away. gfx_texture_alloc() and render_backend_texture_create() take a tTextureFilter in
  both backends, and EmuUtility's LCD passes eTextureNearest explicitly.
  RESULT, same button, same 1280x720 display: the ascender gained a row (5px to 6px) and the ink
  went from thin and washed out to solid — the row-darkness profile under the box went from
  2,38,38,44,34 to 2,11,6,9,20,12. Legibility is visibly better across the whole frame.
  VERIFIED, and the second one matters more than the first:
    - METAL AND OPENGL ARE PIXEL-IDENTICAL with the change in, 0 of 439,348 pixels.
    - THE CRISP PATH IS UNTOUCHED. Captured at ZOOM 3.0, where canvas text is well above the
      threshold, before and after: the only differences are in y5..68 — the menu and top bar, which
      do not zoom and so are still small. The zoomed canvas is byte-identical.
  NOT VERIFIED: how this looks on the Retina display, because the machine was on 1280x720 all
  session. By construction nothing above 12px changes, but a zoomed-OUT canvas on Retina will now
  take the new path, which is intended and still worth an eye.

  DONE 2026-08-29 — MSAA, 4x, BOTH BACKENDS, application and plug-in. GFX_MSAA_SAMPLES in
  renderBackend.h is the single value; 0 or 1 disables it.
  IT IS NOT SELECTIVE and that is the thing to know before changing it. Multisampling is a property
  of the render pass, so it smooths EVERY geometry edge at once — the circles and diagonal cables it
  was aimed at, the radial dial pointers and triangles too, and also axis-aligned rectangle edges
  that land on fractional coordinates, which were previously hard. At 1x that reads as an
  improvement (a one-pixel border that used to vanish or double becomes a consistent line) but it is
  a change everywhere, not a targeted one. It does NOTHING for text: a glyph is a textured quad, so
  the only edges it can see are the quad's corners.
  WHERE IT HAD TO GO IN EACH BACKEND:
    - OpenGL: the multisample BUFFER belongs to the pixel format and cannot be asked for after the
      context exists. So it is a GLFW_SAMPLES hint in synthlibWindow.c for the application AND a
      second copy in the plug-in's NSOpenGLPixelFormat in vst3/g2GlView.m, which has no GLFW to
      ask. gfx_init() only switches it on. Keep the two requests in step.
    - Metal: a rasterSampleCount on the pipeline plus a SECOND, multisampled, private texture that
      is rendered into and resolved down into gTarget — which stays the readable, presentable one.
      Every pass uses StoreAndMultisampleResolve rather than resolving once at the end, because a
      frame here is not one pass: the batch is submitted and the pass ended at every scissor and
      texture change, so there is no way to know which pass is last.
  BYTE-IDENTITY BETWEEN BACKENDS IS GONE, deliberately and unavoidably — two rasterizers do not use
  the same sample positions. What replaced it is nearly as good: Metal and OpenGL differ on 0.87% of
  pixels with a MAX CHANNEL DELTA OF 1. Future comparisons need a tolerance of 1, not a cmp.
  MEASURED ACROSS ZOOM (1280x720 display, SimpleLead, share of pixels MSAA changes):
      zoom 0.5   7.29%        zoom 1.0   8.81%        zoom 3.0   6.64%
  So it earns its keep zoomed in as well as out. But ZOOMED IN THE LIMIT STOPS BEING THE SAMPLING
  AND BECOMES THE SEGMENT COUNT: every circle is a fixed-sided polygon regardless of radius —
  moduleGraphics.c draws connectors with 10 segments, splitView.c thumbs with 12, patchAdjuster.c
  knobs with 16, all literals. Enlarged, a connector is a visible decagon; MSAA smooths the joins
  between facets but cannot add curvature. If zoomed-in circles still look wrong, that is the thing
  to fix, and scaling segments with the drawn radius is a cheap change.
  VERIFIED AT RETINA 2026-08-29 (3840x2160 panel, 1920x1080 logical, framebuffer 2800x1574,
  gGlobalGuiScale 2.19):
    - THE SUPERSAMPLED TEXT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL AT RETINA. Nothing above the 12px threshold changes,
      which is what the design claimed and could not be checked while the machine was at 1280x720.
    - MSAA changes 2.36% of pixels at Retina against 8.81% at 1x — less to fix where a jagged step
      is already half a pixel, but still visible on the curves and diagonals.
    - Metal against OpenGL with everything on: 0.36% of pixels, MAX CHANNEL DELTA 58. That is
      larger than the +/-1 seen at 1x and it is the multisample resolve, not a defect: with 4
      samples an edge pixel where the two rasterizers disagree by one sample moves by about a
      quarter of the local contrast. Any future comparison of the two backends needs that tolerance.
  A RACE IN THE TEST HARNESS, not the app, and it cost an hour: the backdoor DELETES THE COMMAND
  FILE BEFORE PROCESSING THE COMMAND, so waiting for that file to disappear is not waiting for the
  command to finish. Every capture script in this file's history did exactly that. It never bit at
  1280x720 because commands completed inside the following sleep; at 4K the app is slower and all
  four captures silently produced no file while every command reported OK — the OK being the
  PREVIOUS command's, still in the result file. WAIT FOR THE RESULT FILE TO APPEAR, having deleted
  it first. Do not wait on the command file.

  NOT VERIFIED: the PLUG-IN comparison. Both backends build and the host runs them, but the editor
  view is 1367x768 POINTS and the screen was 1280x720, so the window does not fit and the capture
  caught a clipped, repositioned window — a meaningless 47% "difference" over a 1280x658 region.
  Redo it on the 4K display. Nothing suggests a real problem; the capture is simply invalid.

  THE REMAINING HALF, CIRCLES (CT reports those as visibly jagged at this resolution, and
  they are): SUPERSAMPLE. Render the offscreen target at 2x and downscale. Text is then rasterized
  at an em of 12-16 where the quantisation is a few percent, and every curve gets antialiased on the
  way down. MSAA would fix the circles and do NOTHING for text, since glyphs are textured quads —
  so supersampling is the single change that answers both complaints.
  THE OLD SUSPECT LIST, kept because it is still the fallback if supersampling is rejected:
    1. PER-GLYPH POSITION ROUNDING. internal_render_text() rounds every glyph to a whole pixel
       (round(originX + xCharOffset)) while the pen advances by the canonical fractional advance.
       The comment there calls it "the right trade at these sizes" — and at 2x it plainly is. At
       6px a half-pixel is a large fraction of a character, so the same trade may be what makes 1x
       text look unevenly spaced. Test by drawing at fractional x with a LINEAR-filtered atlas and
       comparing; note that would also mean giving up the GL_NEAREST 1:1 blit, so it is a real
       trade rather than a free fix.
    2. HINTING. FreeType's default hinting distorts glyph shapes hard at 5-6px. Light hinting, the
       autohinter, or no hinting with accurate positioning often reads better that small.
  THE THIRD OPTION, which sidesteps both: SUPERSAMPLE — render the offscreen target at 2x and
  downscale. Helps text, curves and thin lines at once and needs no decision about hinting. Costs
  4x the fragment work (nothing, at this complexity) and one full-screen-quad pass, because a
  MTLBlitCommandEncoder copy cannot rescale. This is the use the FBO step would have earned; it was
  dropped for the read-back, which was right, but not for this.
  HOW TO GET A 1x FRAME: I could not reproduce one on the Retina machine — the framebuffer stayed
  at 3032x1704 with GLFW_SCALE_FRAMEBUFFER and GLFW_COCOA_RETINA_FRAMEBUFFER both set false, and I
  did not chase why. With a real non-Retina display, the backdoor SCREENSHOT is the capture and the
  comparison method is the one used throughout the Metal port; REMEMBER TO PIN prefs.txt first
  (zoomFactor and the window geometry persist and will otherwise differ between captures).

- SMALL: synthlibWindow.c:259 uses GLFW_COCOA_RETINA_FRAMEBUFFER, which the bundled GLFW 3.5.1
  header marks deprecated in favour of GLFW_SCALE_FRAMEBUFFER "for compatibility with earlier
  versions". Still works; worth the one-word change. Found while trying to force a 1x framebuffer.

- 2026-08-29 — VSYNC UNDER METAL, and a presentation fix for the plug-in. CT asked whether Metal
  syncs to the monitor and reported possibly seeing tearing. It DOES: CAMetalLayer's
  displaySyncEnabled defaults to YES. But nothing in this codebase said so, while the OpenGL path
  asks out loud in two places (glfwSwapInterval(1) in synthlibWindow.c, the swap interval on the
  plug-in's context in g2GlView.m) — an invariant explicit in one backend and inherited in the
  other is one nobody can answer a question about. It is now set explicitly.
  THE LIKELIER CAUSE OF ACTUAL TEARING, and it is fixed: presentDrawable: hands the frame over
  ASYNCHRONOUSLY, outside the Core Animation transaction AppKit is running. When we own the frame
  loop that is correct and cheapest — the application keeps it. The PLUG-IN does not own its frame
  loop: it draws from -drawRect:, inside a view hierarchy the host is laying out, so its pixels and
  AppKit's layout landed at different moments. That shows up as tearing or edge jitter, worst while
  dragging or resizing. The plug-in now sets presentsWithTransaction and presents by hand —
  commit, waitUntilScheduled, [drawable present] — so the frame becomes visible as part of the same
  transaction. Costs one synchronisation point per frame, which is why the application does not pay it.
  VERIFIED: the application's frames are BYTE-IDENTICAL across the change (it only gained the
  explicit displaySyncEnabled, which was already the default), and the plug-in's are byte-identical
  to the pre-change Metal build — so this is a presentation change and not a rendering one.
  STILL UNMEASURED: an actual frame rate. Both apps redraw on demand rather than free-running, so
  there is no steady rate to count without driving a continuous drag. If tearing is still visible,
  that is the experiment to build.

- 2026-08-29 — tools/vst3host would not close until the mouse was moved (CT noticed). -[NSApplication
  stop:] does NOT end the run loop; it sets a flag acted on only after the CURRENT event finishes
  dispatching, so with the pointer still, -run stayed blocked in nextEventMatchingMask: and never
  reached it. Any stray event released it, which is why moving the mouse appeared to let the window
  close. Fixed by posting a dummy application-defined event straight after stop:. It now exits on
  its own timer with nothing touched.

- DONE 2026-08-29 — THE BACKEND IS CHOSEN AT START-UP, AND THERE IS A MENU ITEM FOR IT.
  Experimental ▸ "Use Metal Renderer (on restart)", with a greyed "Renderer: OpenGL" line under it
  saying what is running. Writes renderBackend to prefs.txt; OpenGL is 0 and the default.
  BOTH BACKENDS ARE NOW IN ONE BINARY. They used to be mutually exclusive at compile time — each
  file wrapped in an #if that compiled it to nothing when it was not the chosen one. Two files
  cannot both define gfx_init(), so each backend's eleven entry points are now file-local (gl_*,
  mtl_*) and reached only through a tGfxBackend TABLE, with renderBackend.c forwarding the public
  gfx_* calls to whichever is selected. The cost is one call through a pointer PER BATCH
  SUBMISSION — a few dozen a frame — which is nothing.
  renderBackendSelect.h now holds only the DEFAULT. renderBackendMetal.m is excluded by #ifdef
  __APPLE__, which is a platform fact rather than a preference.
  IT CANNOT CHANGE WHILE RUNNING, and the menu says so rather than pretending otherwise. The window
  itself differs: OpenGL needs GLFW to create a context alongside it, Metal needs GLFW_NO_API and
  then takes the NSWindow. Switching live means destroying and rebuilding the window, the context,
  every glyph atlas and texture, and every callback established around them.
  ORDERING THAT IS LOAD-BEARING: prefs_init("G2-Edit") now runs in main.c BEFORE init_graphics(),
  because synthlib_window_create() reads the choice before making the window. It also still runs
  from setup_main_menu() where it always did — prefs_init clears and re-reads, so twice is harmless.
  THE FIRST ATTEMPT SILENTLY DID NOTHING for exactly this reason: the pref read returned the
  default because prefs had not been initialised yet, and both settings produced OpenGL.
  SYNTHEDIT AND EMUUTILITY WILL NEED THE SAME ONE-LINE ORDERING when they take the pin. Without it
  they simply always get the default, with no error.
  THE PLUG-IN STILL CHOOSES AT BUILD TIME, and that is not laziness: its view's SUPERCLASS is either
  NSOpenGLView or NSView, which is fixed when the file compiles. Selecting at run time would mean
  two view classes and two copies of g2GlView.m's four hundred lines of input handling. So
  G2_VST3_METAL=1 ./do-vst3, OpenGL without it. Worth knowing: while RENDER_BACKEND briefly did not
  exist, g2GlView.m's `#if RENDER_BACKEND == RENDER_BACKEND_GL` compared two undefined macros and
  quietly compiled the OpenGL path because 0 == 0. It tests the macro by name now.
  METAL LINKS ITSELF. No project-file change was needed: clang's module auto-linking pulls in Metal
  and QuartzCore from the #import in renderBackendMetal.m, verified in both Debug and Release. The
  Xcode project is untouched. do-vst3 asks for the frameworks explicitly since it is plain clang.
  VERIFIED END TO END: clicking the item writes renderBackend=1; the next launch reports
  "Renderer: Metal"; and one binary with the pref flipped produces frames BYTE-IDENTICAL to the two
  dedicated single-backend builds it replaced. Plug-in unchanged in both backends.

- DONE 2026-08-29 — THE SAME RENDERER CHOICE IN SYNTHEDIT AND EMUUTILITY. Both now carry an
  "Experimental" menu of their own — neither had one — holding the same toggle and the same greyed
  "Renderer: <name>" line. Copied from G2-Edit's rather than shared: three menu tables that are
  already per-app, and the item is forty lines.
  EMUUTILITY NEEDED THE ORDERING FIX, SynthEdit did not. SynthEdit's main.c already called
  init_settings() before init_graphics() for an unrelated reason (the saved layouts folder), which
  happens to satisfy this too. EmuUtility's prefs_init() sat in setup_main_menu(), after the window
  was made; it is now called from main() first, exactly as G2-Edit's is.
  VERIFIED: SynthEdit's frames differ between the backends by 0.041% with a max channel delta of
  57 — the multisample-resolve signature, smaller than G2-Edit's 0.36% simply because its panel has
  far fewer curved edges.
  EMUUTILITY IS BYTE-IDENTICAL BETWEEN THE TWO, AND THAT IS NOT A FAILURE — it was misread as one
  first. Instrumenting the selection showed the app reporting "running Metal" while producing a
  frame indistinguishable from the OpenGL one. Its panel is almost entirely axis-aligned rectangles
  and text: text is a textured quad that multisampling cannot touch, and a rectangle edge on a whole
  pixel is either fully covered or not, so no pixel lands near a sample-position tie and the two
  rasterizers agree exactly. An identical frame is evidence the port is right, not that the switch
  did nothing — check with a log before concluding otherwise.
  BOTH SIBLINGS NOW HAVE A MENU BACKDOOR COMMAND, ported from G2-Edit's (CT asked for whatever
  access was needed). MENU <bar>[/<item>[/<sub>]], matching a label anywhere in its text and
  case-insensitively; omit the last level and that menu is LISTED rather than clicked, which is how
  a test finds what is there. It is what makes a state-dependent label ("Use Metal Renderer" against
  "Use OpenGL Renderer") checkable at all, since the label IS the state.
  SO THE TOGGLE IS NOW VERIFIED END TO END IN ALL THREE: list the menu, click the item, confirm
  renderBackend flips in the preferences file, restart, and see the menu report the other one.

- 2026-08-29 — THE VST3 PLUG-IN DEFAULTS TO METAL (CT: "the vst3 plugin is experimental anyhow, so
  if it can't switch, I'd default it to metal"). It cannot offer the runtime choice the application
  does — its view's superclass is fixed at compile time — so the default is the decision.
      ./do-vst3                     Metal
      G2_VST3_OPENGL=1 ./do-vst3    OpenGL
  The OpenGL build is kept rather than deleted: it is the path with the most hours in a real host,
  so it is the thing to fall back to if a host misbehaves. Verified the default build is
  byte-identical to the Metal capture it replaces, and installed.

- SUPERSEDED — the survey that preceded the above, kept for the facts in it:
    - SYNTHEDIT'S ORDERING IS ALREADY CORRECT. main.c calls init_settings() before init_graphics(),
      with a comment explaining that prefs must be loaded first for an unrelated reason (the saved
      layouts folder). That happens to satisfy this too. Nothing to change.
    - EMUUTILITY'S IS NOT. prefs_init("EmuUtility") sits in setup_main_menu() (misc.mm:45), which
      main.c calls AFTER init_graphics(). Same one-line fix G2-Edit needed: call prefs_init before
      init_graphics. WITHOUT IT THE SETTING IS SILENTLY IGNORED — the read returns the default and
      both values give OpenGL, with no error and nothing in the log. That is exactly how it failed
      here the first time.
    - NEITHER HAS AN "EXPERIMENTAL" MENU. SynthEdit has File/Device/Controls/Layouts/Backup/Restore,
      EmuUtility has Device/Controls. CT wants the menu itself, not just the item — which is right:
      it is where anything new goes until it has settled, and both apps will want somewhere to put
      such things. G2-Edit's open_experimental_menu() in appMenuBar.c is the model, and
      action_toggle_render_backend() beside it is the item, both small enough to copy rather than
      share.
  AND THE PIN. Both siblings are behind, and EmuUtility already has an uncommitted emuGraphics.c
  carrying the texture-filter change; that has to land in the same commit as its pin bump.

- DONE 2026-08-29 — AN ABOUT BOX, because a .vst3 in a host's plug-in folder carries nothing that
  says where it came from (CT, after the Metal plug-in was confirmed working in Ableton). Help ▸
  About, in G2-Edit, in the plug-in, and in both siblings. It reports four things:
      G2-Edit
      Version:  V0.6.4-beta.5-9-g4fc085a-dirty
      Compiled: Aug 29 2026 07:52:37
      Renderer: OpenGL
  THE RENDERER LINE EARNS ITS PLACE now the backend is a preference: "it looks wrong" and "it looks
  wrong on Metal" are different reports, and nothing else on screen says which is running.
  THE VERSION COMES FROM GIT, and the SEQUENCING IS THE SUBTLE PART — CT flagged it before it was
  written, which saved a wrong answer being shipped:
    - ./do-vst3 standalone uses `git describe --tags --always --dirty`, giving the last tag, the
      commits since, the commit, and -dirty when the tree matches none of them.
    - ./do-release MUST NOT. It builds the .dmg BEFORE the tag exists — it computes the NEXT version
      from the newest tag and only prints the `git tag` command to run once the .dmg is checked. So
      `git describe` at that moment names the PREVIOUS release, and a .dmg labelled 0.6.4-beta.6
      whose plug-in says 0.6.4-beta.5 is worse than no version at all, because a wrong version
      number is believed. do-release therefore passes G2_VERSION_STRING="$VERSION" to do-vst3 and
      the same value into the app's build, and that overrides the git lookup.
    - A PLAIN XCODE BUILD SAYS "development build". Xcode cannot run git without a Run Script phase,
      and MARKETING_VERSION in the project is stale and documented as not being the source of truth,
      so reporting it would be the same lie. The compile time identifies a dev build instead. If
      Xcode builds ever need the real version, a Run Script phase writing the define is the way —
      it was not added because it means editing the project file.
  __DATE__/__TIME__ ARE THE MOMENT synthlibVersion.c WAS COMPILED, which is exact for do-vst3 and
  do-release (both compile everything every run) but can lag on an incremental Xcode build. Hence
  the label says "Compiled", which is true either way, rather than "Built", which would not be.
  ALSO FIXED: G2_VERSION in g2Vst3.cpp was hardcoded "0.1.0" — nine minor versions stale — and is
  what the plug-in reports to the host in PClassInfo2. Left alone for now because changing what a
  host sees as the plug-in version can invalidate saved projects; worth a decision rather than a
  drive-by edit. NOTED HERE SO IT IS NOT FORGOTTEN.
  The plug-in's Help menu function is open_plugin_help_menu(), named apart from the application's
  open_help_menu() which appMenuBar.h declares and g2Menu.c can see.
  VERIFIED IN ALL FOUR: the box was opened and read in G2-Edit, SynthEdit and EmuUtility, through
  the MENU backdoor command (which is why that was ported in the first place), and the plug-in's
  binary was checked with `strings` to carry V0.6.4-beta.5-9-g4fc085a-dirty. The three applications
  say "development build" when built in Xcode, which is the intended answer there.

- CROSS-PLATFORM IS DEFERRED BY DECISION, NOT FORGOTTEN (CT, 2026-08-29: "Not sure I'm quite ready
  to make the CMake jump yet. Lots to complete on MacOS, before making this cross platform.").
  Nothing is half-built — everything below was surveyed, not started, so there is no partial state
  to clean up. Recorded so it is not worked out from scratch again.
  THE BLOCKER IS CMAKE, and nothing else can start without it. The only build systems here are an
  Xcode project and shell scripts. It can be written and validated ENTIRELY ON MACOS — build the app
  with CMake on the Mac and diff the backdoor screenshots against the Xcode build to prove it is the
  same program — before any other platform exists.
  DO NOT GENERATE ONE BUILD SYSTEM FROM THE OTHER. No supported tool reads an .xcodeproj and emits
  CMake, and here there would be almost nothing to read: the project is 468 lines with FIVE
  PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroups, so it contains NO FILE LISTS AT ALL — only about a dozen
  settings (11.5 deployment target, gnu17, the warning flags, ARC/modules, hardened runtime, the
  three static libs). The reverse, cmake -G Xcode, is the conventional single-source answer and
  would be a REGRESSION here: generated Xcode projects emit explicit file lists, so it would lose
  synchronized folders and reintroduce exactly the list-maintenance problem worth avoiding.
  THE ANSWER IS file(GLOB ... CONFIGURE_DEPENDS). Globbing is normally discouraged in CMake; here it
  is right, because it reproduces the synchronized-folder behaviour this project already depends on.
  Then NEITHER build system holds a file list. Roughly a dozen settings stay duplicated; they change
  rarely (the deployment target moved once, in August) and want a comment in both files.
  AND IT WOULD RETIRE A KNOWN TRAP: do-vst3 keeps its source list BY HAND, which the notes already
  warn about ("a new file called from a shared one breaks only the plug-in") and which bit twice on
  2026-08-29 alone, adding renderBackend.c and synthlibVersion.c. A globbing CMake build could
  subsume the plug-in and make CMake a net REDUCTION in list-maintenance.
  HOW TO BUILD AND TEST EACH PLATFORM FROM THIS MAC, since CT has no Windows or Linux hardware:
    - LINUX: a Docker/Colima/OrbStack container, and it can RUN as well as build. The backdoor
      SCREENSHOT is glReadPixels on the framebuffer, not a screen grab, so it needs no visible
      window: Xvfb for a display, Mesa llvmpipe for OpenGL in software — and llvmpipe is ample
      because renderBackendGL.c is entirely GL 1.1. Build, run headless, capture, and diff the PNGs
      against the macOS ones with tools/pngdiff. No VM licence, no GPU.
    - WINDOWS: mingw-w64 from Homebrew cross-compiles from macOS, and is EASIER than cross-compiling
      to Linux because it ships the whole Win32 SDK where a Linux target needs a sysroot with X11
      headers. But it is a COMPILE CHECK ONLY — Wine on Apple Silicon emulates x86 and translates
      OpenGL, so a failure there proves nothing. Real Windows (Parallels or hardware) is needed for
      the runtime and for libusb, which additionally wants the WinUSB driver via Zadig.
    - Straight cross-compilation to Linux from macOS (clang --target + sysroot, or zig cc) works but
      is not worth it: the effort goes into assembling a sysroot and cross-built dependencies, and
      the result cannot be run.
    - GITHUB ACTIONS is the cheap half of all this and comes first: a Linux build job costs nothing
      and catches the compile breakage that otherwise rots silently.
  NOTHING IS INSTALLED for any of it as of 2026-08-29 — no docker, colima, podman, orbstack, mingw
  or zig — but Homebrew is, so each is one install away.

- FIXED 2026-08-29 — TWO PROJECTS WERE LINKING AN OLD GLFW, and it took CT asking to find it ("Did
  you rebuild glfw across all 3 projects when we updated it?"). They were not.
  THE MECHANISM, which is the part to remember: THE BUILT STATIC LIBRARIES ARE NOT TRACKED IN GIT.
  Each project links its own SynthLib/ThirdParty/glfw/build/src/libglfw3.a, and a submodule pull
  brings the new SOURCE but never a new BINARY. So updating GLFW in SynthLib and advancing all three
  pins leaves two of the three still linking whatever they last built.
      G2-Edit     libglfw3.a built 2026-08-26 -> 3.5.1   (rebuilt at the time of the update)
      SynthEdit   libglfw3.a built 2026-07-28 -> 3.5.0
      EmuUtility  libglfw3.a built 2026-07-28 -> 3.5.0
  All three had 3.5.1 HEADERS, so those two were compiling against one version and linking another.
  It linked and ran — a patch-level GLFW does not break ABI — which is exactly why it went unnoticed
  for a month.
  REBUILT BOTH with the recipe in Third Party build notes.txt (delete build/ first: cmake caches the
  architecture and deployment target, so re-running over an existing build directory silently keeps
  the old values). All three are now 3.5.1, minos 11.5, x86_64+arm64.
  NOTHING CHANGED ON SCREEN, checked rather than assumed: captures before and after differ by 455
  and 432 pixels, all of them the word "Help" in the menu bar — the menu added earlier the same day.
  THE OTHER TWO LIBRARIES ARE FINE, checked at the same time. FreeType is 2.14.3 from the same source
  in all three, all 11.5 and universal; the differing checksums are embedded build paths, not
  versions. libusb is present ONLY in G2-Edit and that is correct — neither sibling references it in
  its project file, because only the G2 talks over USB.
  A STANDING CHECK, worth running whenever a ThirdParty submodule is updated:
      for p in G2-Edit SynthEdit EmuUtility; do
          strings $p/SynthLib/ThirdParty/glfw/build/src/libglfw3.a | grep -oE '^3\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+ [A-Za-z]' | head -1
      done
  and compare against GLFW_VERSION_* in each project's glfw3.h. The same trap applies to FreeType and
  libusb, and it will apply to any ThirdParty library added later.

- WINDOWS / LINUX BUILDS — wanted eventually (CT, 2026-08-26). Nothing started, no schedule; this is
  a note of where the port surface actually is, so it is not re-surveyed from scratch later.
  ALREADY PORTABLE, and mostly on purpose: GLFW covers Win32/X11/Wayland and is pinned at the 3.5.1
  release (2026-08-26); the drawing API is behind the four render_backend_* calls above; settings
  are a plain text file via SynthLib's prefs.h rather than NSUserDefaults; file dialogs are
  SynthLib's in-window fileBrowser.cpp, not NSOpenPanel; the menus are the in-window bar in
  appMenuBar.c. Each of those was a deliberate step away from Cocoa and they add up.
  WHAT IS STILL MAC-ONLY, and it is a short list:
    - src/misc.mm (79 lines) — the minimal native Cocoa app menu, and NSWorkspace sleep/wake
      notifications, which have no cross-platform equivalent in this codebase
    - src/audioOutput.c — CoreAudio/AudioToolbox
    - src/midiInput.c — CoreMIDI
    - the build itself: an Xcode project, plus do-release/do-vst3 as shell scripts. This is
      probably the biggest single piece of work, not the code.
  libusb is already cross-platform, so the G2 comms layer should not need touching.
  soundEngine.c is platform-free and stays that way — it names CoreAudio only in comments.
  NOT IN SCOPE OF THAT LIST: the VST3 plug-in's editor (g2Editor.mm, g2GlView.m) is a second
  platform layer and a separate question from the application.

- Compress(150) — module height may be 1 row too many (currently 5 rows, moduleResources.h). The
  original editor's module-info structs are opaque in the reference (SModuleInfo/CBuildModule are
  placeholder structs, heights not recoverable as readable data); needs validation by running the
  original editor and comparing.

- SynthLib file-naming consistency — DEFERRED (cross-repo, coordinate carefully). SynthLib/src mixes
  two camps: bare (alertDialog, bankBrowser, clickRegion, contextMenu, fileBrowser, geometry,
  menuBar, prefs, utils, utilsGraphics) and synthlib-prefixed (synthlibDefs/Globals/Host/Persistence/
  Queue/Scale/Types). Proposal (owner): unify on a short prefix, e.g. "sl" -> slUtils.c, slDefs.h,
  slQueue.c. Do NOT drop the prefix entirely — synthlibDefs/Types/Globals would collide with each
  app's own defs.h/types.h/globalVars. Blast radius: ~130 #include lines across all three apps, plus
  the renames + internal cross-includes. Must be one coordinated pass: rename in SynthLib, update
  every consumer, advance all three pins together, build-test each. Mechanical but wide; schedule
  when all three projects can be built/committed together.

- BIG / long-term idea — add a built-in sound-generation (DSP) engine so the editor can produce
  audio itself, not just edit/drive the hardware G2. Reference: the DEMO editor (the
  standalone editor that ran patches without hardware) — its DSP/voice code is the model. (That
  reference may not be on hand; the one here is the HARDWARE
  editor, whose module-info/DSP internals come through as opaque placeholder structs — CVoice/
  CVoiceMap/CPatchData symbols survive but bodies don't.) Would let module waveform/response
  graphics be driven by real DSP instead of hand-derived approximations, and enable offline
  auditioning. Very large effort; capture the demo-editor reference alongside this note when
  starting.

- Cross-platform build — Linux + Windows, with a CMake build derived from the Xcode project as the
  master (Xcode stays authoritative for day-to-day Mac dev; CMake is the portable build).
  (A) Build system. The Xcode projects use folder-synchronized groups
      (PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup on src/ and SynthLib/src/), i.e. there is NO manual
      per-file source list — it just globs those two folders. So a CMakeLists can file(GLOB) the same
      folders and stay in sync automatically; there is little to "derive" beyond the folder set, the
      frameworks/libs and the compile flags. Practically: one CMake that compiles src/ +
      SynthLib/src/, links GLFW + FreeType + libusb (all cross-platform; copies in ThirdParty/), and
      swaps the Mac-only bits per-platform. Could add a script to sniff the framework/lib list out of
      project.pbxproj so the CMake stays honest. Do the same for EmuUtility/SynthEdit, ideally with
      the SynthLib half shared.
  (B) Platform abstraction.
      PORTABLE ALREADY: core protocol.c / dataBase.c / msgQueue.c / moduleResources (pure C); USB via
        libusb (~40 calls); GLFW + OpenGL + FreeType rendering; SynthLib fileBrowser.cpp
        (std::filesystem, already handles Windows USERPROFILE/APPDATA + Linux XDG) and prefs.cpp.
      MAC-ONLY, needs a portable seam:
        - misc.mm (Obj-C/Cocoa): NSUserDefaults and the NSWorkspace sleep/wake notifications
          (register_sleep_wake_notifications). The native NSMenu is now largely gone — the app draws
          its own in-window menu bar (menuBar.c / appMenuBar.c) — so what remains can be #ifdef'd out
          elsewhere; sleep/wake needs a Linux/Windows equivalent or a no-op stub.
        - usbComms.c: 2 IOKit references (the rest is libusb) — likely USB hotplug / sleep-wake device
          notification; replace with libusb_hotplug_* or stub per-platform.
        - persistence.c: NSUserDefaults-backed settings — should move onto SynthLib's prefs.cpp
          (synthlibPersistence), which is already the portable mechanism, rather than porting the
          Cocoa version. (Overlaps the commonalise work below.)
      Suggest: get the pure-C + SynthLib core compiling under CMake on Linux first (fastest signal),
      behind a thin platform shim header (synthlibPlatform.h?) with mac/linux/win implementations of
      the handful of OS hooks. Windows after Linux. Dovetails with the commonalise priorities below —
      the platform shim is a natural SynthLib citizen shared by all three apps.


NEEDS A SECOND OPINION (hardware/protocol questions found in code comments — real G2 access and/or
testing needed before touching; do not guess)

- usbComms.c:3650-3651 — "consider if this is the right thing to do here" / "not sure we need to do
  this here" around patch-change-indication handling — a behavioural judgement call; confirm current
  behaviour is correct before changing it.
- usbComms.c:3834 — commented-out eMsgCmdReloadAllPatchData case, with an open question about
  whether a full reload does more than intended. Needs understanding of the active path's scope.
- protocol.c:1759 — write-side parsing doesn't match the read-side logic in usbComms.c; a real fix
  needs careful protocol comparison and hardware verification, not a guess.
- mouseHandle.c:164 — gControllerCount[slot] "seems to default to 2, so might need to set up
  defaults" — whether some default controller assignment should be pre-populated on a new patch.
  Needs product/hardware judgement, not a code fix.
- moduleGraphics.c:744,751,758 — "Should come from volume location list!? Shouldn't be in
  gModuleProperties" — a data-modelling question (where per-module volume-type info should live)
  needing a design decision, not a mechanical fix.
- mouseHandle.c:929 — "Close if clicked outside" on a context menu — current behaviour looks
  reasonable; flag only if it has actually been observed to feel wrong in use.
- usbComms.c:3980 — "Don't like early returns. Use retVal" — style preference, not a bug.
- usbComms.c:3526 — "send data from the editor" for currently-read-only items — a future
  write-support idea, not a bug.
- usbComms.c:4047 — "maybe some more items we need to get here" for perf settings — open-ended idea,
  not a known gap.
- globalVars.c:87 — commented-out gHiddenCableMask with "Send to G2 when changes" — an unimplemented
  future feature (sync cable-hide state to hardware), not a bug.


MISSING FEATURES (parity with the original editor — cross-checked against g2manual.txt 2026-07-25;
none of these exist in G2-Edit at all, confirmed by source search)

- Pop-up overlay views — BUILT 2026-08-02 (src/paramOverlay.c / .h), needs an owner eyeball.
  All five of the original's overlays, reached from the VIEW MENU rather than the function keys
  (owner's call, as with Parameter Pages): Parameter Values, Morph Groups, Knob Assignments, MIDI
  CC Assignments, Parameter MIDI Values. Mutually exclusive as in the original, and picking the
  one already showing turns it off, so the entries act as a radio group with the active one ticked.
  DELIBERATELY NOT IN SYNTHLIB - every mode is about a G2 module parameter (morph groups, the 120
  Parameter Page knobs, the patch controller table), none of which exist in the other two apps.
  SHAPE: moduleGraphics.c lost ~50 lines of overlay bookkeeping and now makes one call,
  param_overlay_note_param(module, paramIndex, rectangle, buff), for every parameter it draws. The
  overlay module decides for itself whether that parameter earns a label, so the renderer knows
  nothing about the modes; the old hover-only knob/CC labels became just overlayModeNone inside
  the same switch rather than a separate path. render_param_common() already holds the formatted
  display string the widget drew, so it is passed through rather than re-deriving per-type
  formatting here.
  DRAWN OVER THE PARAMETER, not under it (owner: that is what the original does, and there is no
  room underneath in a dense module) - but NOTHING is blanked underneath. The chip's backing is
  translucent (OVERLAY_BOX_ALPHA) and every control stays visible: dial, button and menu box alike.
  Two earlier attempts are worth not repeating. Masking the widget opaque so the label could not
  clash with it turned dials into blank white squares, and it hid exactly the button text the view
  was meant to annotate. What makes going maskless work is that THE CHIP NEVER REPEATS WHAT THE
  WIDGET ALREADY SHOWS: it carries the raw wire value alone, so it stays two or three characters
  wide. Raw and formatted still end up next to each other as in the original - the formatted half
  just comes from the control itself.
  Placement follows from the same idea: centred on a dial, which has nothing inside its circle to
  collide with, and in the TOP-LEFT CORNER of a widget that carries its own text, where centring
  would land the chip across the word ("Semi" under a "0" reads as "S0mi"). The caller handing
  over a non-empty display string is what distinguishes the two.
  WHAT THE VALUES VIEW SHOWS: the raw wire value always, and the formatted value ONLY where the
  box has covered it. Since dials became dial-anchored the mask covers just the circle, so a
  dial's own "Pitch / 0.0" is still on screen above the box and repeating it would print the same
  string twice; a button's text IS its value and is covered, so that one is reproduced.
  Z-ORDER: param_overlay_render() runs immediately after the modules and cables, BEFORE the top
  bar, menus and every panel - it used to run near the end of the frame, which let it paint over
  open menus.
  STILL TO LOOK AT:
   1. A corner chip still covers the first character or so of a button's text ("saw" reads as
      "2aw", "FM Lin" as "0M Lin"). Nudging it further out would clear the word but push it over
      whatever sits above. Owner to judge from examples.
   2. overlayModeMidiValues assumes a plain proportional scale into 0-127 for parameters whose
      range is not 128. NEEDS A HARDWARE CHECK - the manual only says the view shows "how each
      knob is actually sent and received over MIDI" without stating the mapping.
   3. MAX_PARAM_OVERLAYS is 1024 rows; beyond that queueing simply stops, which drops labels off a
      very crowded canvas rather than misdrawing. Not yet seen to trigger.
  FOUND ON THE WAY, worth knowing: contextMenu.c calls action(index) with the item's POSITION in
  the menu, not the payload - actions fetch their own value from gContextMenu.items[index].param,
  as every action in menus.c does. Using the argument directly made "Parameter Values" select
  "Knob Assignments".

  ORIGINAL REFERENCE (the manual's own descriptions):
    F5        View Parameter Values     — each parameter's current value
    F6        View Morph Groups         — which of the 8 morph groups each parameter belongs to
    F7        View Knob Assignments     — which panel knob (if any) each parameter is mapped to
    F8        View MIDI CC# Assignments — the MIDI CC# (if any) mapped to each parameter
    Ctrl+F8   View Parameter MIDI Values — how each knob is actually sent/received over MIDI
  None implemented — no F5-F8 key handling anywhere in the source.
- Parameter Pages window — BUILT 2026-08-02 (src/paramPages.c / paramPages.h). The graphical
  stand-in for the G2 front panel's PARAMETER PAGES and their ASSIGNABLE KNOBS/BUTTONS (manual
  p.19 and p.89-91). Opened from Settings > Parameter Pages; NO keyboard shortcut, owner's
  explicit call 2026-08-02 (the original's Ctrl-F was implemented and then removed).
  Full-canvas panel per the owner's form decision: title bar with Slot A-D buttons and Close, a
  Patch/Global toggle, the 15 pages as a 3-wide x 5-high A1..E3 matrix, and the selected page's
  eight knobs in a row. Each cell shows position number, module name (Slot-prefixed on a Global
  page, since those can point into any Slot) and the live param widget.
  HOW IT'S PUT TOGETHER, since the shape matters more than the line count:
    * The knob widgets ARE the canvas's widgets — render_param_common() is called directly, so a
      dial looks like a dial, a toggle like a toggle, and every value string is formatted by that
      param type's own rule with no second implementation to keep in step. renderParams.c's ~30
      renderers hard-coded moduleArea (canvas zoom + scroll); they now take it from a file-scope
      area that set_param_render_area() (renderParams.h) switches to mainArea around the panel's
      render pass. A mode, not a parameter, to avoid threading an argument through 30 signatures
      and their function-pointer types.
    * Hit-testing reads back gParamRectangle, which render_param_common() has just written, so
      there is only one description of where a control is. Dragging fills in gParamDragging, i.e.
      the canvas's own drag machinery: cursor_pos() tracks it and sends the value. Mouse-up goes
      through the new finish_param_drag() (mouseHandle.h), extracted from the canvas's mouse-up so
      the undo push is shared — without it the panel's drags would be silently missing from Ctrl-Z.
    * Cross-Slot correctness: render_param_common(), the renderParams.c widgets, cursor_pos()'s
      drag branch and finish_param_drag() all now read module->key.slot rather than gSlot. A no-op
      on the canvas (which only ever draws the selected Slot) but required for a Global page, whose
      knobs can point at modules in any of the four Slots, each with its own active Variation.
    * The reverse direction was FREE, which answers the old open question: turning an assigned
      knob on the G2 arrives as an ordinary param change, usbComms.c's parse_param_change() writes
      it into the same module param, and the panel redraws from it. No new message handling. One
      change was needed: that function only woke GLFW for locationMorph, so it now also wakes
      whenever the panel is up.
    * Right-click a cell opens the canvas's own param context menu, so a knob can be reassigned or
      cleared from the page it's shown on.
  VERIFIED OFFLINE via the backdoor + cliclick 2026-08-02: panel chrome, page matrix (including the
  lighter shade marking a page that has assignments), Patch/Global toggle, per-Slot switching,
  empty vs assigned cells, and a real edit — changing a waveform from the panel's menu-type widget
  updated the module on the canvas. The stray knob-assignment hover overlay this first exposed is
  fixed (moduleGraphics.c now suppresses the canvas hover check while the panel is up; it was
  matching against a gParamRectangle the panel had overwritten and painting the label in
  moduleArea's scrolled space).
  NOT VERIFIED, and needs the owner:
   1. DIAL DRAGGING. Untestable through the harness — a synthetic cliclick drag moves no dial, on
      the panel OR on the patch canvas, so the test says nothing either way. The code path is the
      canvas's own, but it has not been seen to work.
   2. The knob index -> page mapping (page = index / 24, bank = (index % 24) / 8, position =
      index % 8). The panel follows what menus.c and moduleGraphics.c already assumed, so all
      three agree, but A1,A2,A3,B1... vs column-major is still unconfirmed against the hardware.
   3. Anything involving a real G2: the hardware-knob-turn refresh, and whether an edit made in
      the panel reaches the device (offline it only reaches the database and the queue).
   4. Whether 8-across is right at small window sizes. The panel now sizes itself to its content
      (see the layout note below) and only clamps once that exceeds the window; not tried below
      about 800 logical px, where the clamp starts to bite and overlaps can return.
  LAYOUT, reworked 2026-08-02 after the owner reported knob boxes overlapping each other. Two
  separate causes, both from the panel choosing rectangles that the canvas normally gets from
  paramLocationList:
   * NOTHING CLIPS - there is no scissor anywhere in SynthLib - and every widget paints
     left-anchored from the rect it is handed, growing rightwards by whatever its content needs.
     A fixed cell width therefore can't hold a wide value string or a wide strMap button, and the
     next cell's background then paints over the overflow. Cells are now measured
     (knob_metrics()) and the panel's width follows from the widest widget on the page, so
     switching pages can resize the panel.
   * paramTypeEnable is the exception to "the renderer sizes itself": render_paramType1Enable()
     draws a button exactly rectangle.size.w wide with the param's LABEL inside it. Handing it a
     fixed 26 is what put "Mod" in the button and "Amt" outside it on page A1 knob 6, whose param
     the patch has renamed "Mod Amt". The rect width is now per param type.
  Dials are centred on their cell (owner's request), and everything else has its painted block
  centred. That costs width: a centred dial draws its label and value from the dial's left edge
  rightwards, so the cell has to be about twice the content width. The sizing pass asks for that,
  and each cell falls back to block-centred and then to left-anchored if the window is too narrow
  to grant it.
  STILL OPEN: the manual describes click-and-hold on a panel knob also FOCUSING the parameter in
  the patch window (and moving the real front-panel knob). Neither is implemented - the panel
  edits the value but does not scroll the canvas to the module or highlight it.
  Also still worth checking whether SynthEdit's render_page_tabs() should have been reused for the
  page selector rather than this second one (see the page-tab unification note in the commonalise
  section) - the A1..E3 matrix is a grid rather than a tab row, so it may not fit.

- Parameter Overview window — BUILT 2026-08-02 (src/paramOverview.c / .h). All fifteen Parameter
  Pages' assignments at once as a 15-row x 8-column grid, opened from Settings > Parameter Overview.
  NO keyboard shortcut (the original's Ctrl-L), matching the owner's standing menu-only call for
  this family of panels.
  BOXES, NOT LIVE WIDGETS - unlike the Parameter Pages panel, which renders the canvas's own param
  widgets. The original's overview is a grid of display boxes carrying names, and 120 live widgets
  would be both unreadable at this density and a lot of per-frame work. A box shows the module name
  on line 1 and the param name on line 2; View MIDI swaps line 2 for the parameter's CC# ("CC 34",
  or "--" where it has none), which is exactly what that button does in the original.
  SHARES ITS RESOLUTION WITH PARAMETER PAGES rather than reimplementing it: tKnobTarget and
  param_pages_knob_target() / _module_display_name() / _knob_param_label() moved from private
  statics in paramPages.c to paramPages.h, so the two panels can never disagree about what a knob
  points at. param_ref_for_index()'s walk of paramLocationList is the tricky part and now has one
  home.
  DRAG A BOX ONTO ANOTHER BOX to move that assignment. It OVERWRITES the destination rather than
  swapping, which is what the original does - a swap would silently resurrect an assignment the
  user was deliberately replacing. The patch case sends ONE eMsgCmdWritePatch rather than the
  deassign/deassign/assign triple the right-click Assign menu uses, since write_knobs() is already
  part of push_slot_to_device() and a burst of small slot commands is exactly what loses edits to
  the patch-version race (see the bulk MIDI CC entry above). Global knobs have no whole-perf push
  to ride on - write_global_knobs() is only used when SAVING a performance file - so those still go
  as individual commands.
  ASSIGN MIDI / CLEAR MIDI sit on this panel too, as they do in the original. They act on the Slot
  THIS panel is showing, which is why midi_cc_assign_all_knobs()/midi_cc_clear_all() now take an
  explicit slot instead of reading gSlot. Both are hidden on the global pages: those knobs each
  name their own Slot, so there is no single controller table for "assign every CC in this view" to
  write.
  VERIFIED LIVE 2026-08-02 against a real patch with assignments (owner's, on hardware): grid
  layout and A1..E3 / 1..8 labelling, populated boxes resolving both default type+index module
  names (LevConv1, Mix4-1S1) and custom ones (Cutoff Freq, Filter Overdrive), View MIDI showing
  real CC numbers off the controller table, the Patch/Global toggle, and slot + bulk-MIDI buttons
  correctly hidden on the global pages. The panel also re-lays itself out when a patch loads
  underneath it, since cell width is sized to content.
  STILL TO DO:
   1. DRAG-TO-MOVE IS UNVERIFIED. Synthetic drags do not reach the app at all, and neither do
      synthetic KEY events - key_callback() logged nothing for a cliclick Escape, so the panel's
      Escape-to-close is untested too. Both need the owner at a real keyboard/mouse.
   2. Assign MIDI / Clear MIDI on this panel were deliberately NOT clicked during verification: the
      G2 was online and they would have written to real hardware. Same code as the Tools menu
      entries, so they are only unverified in the sense that path is.
   3. The original also drags a box OUT onto a module parameter in the patch window to CREATE an
      assignment (and Alt-drags a module knob INTO the overview). Neither is possible while this is
      a full-canvas modal overlay - the canvas is behind a dimming layer. Making assignments stays
      on the canvas's right-click Assign menu. Would need the panel to become a genuine floating,
      non-modal window first, which is a bigger change and would also serve the Patch Window Split
      Bar and Virtual Keyboard items below.
- Bulk MIDI CC tools (Tools menu) — "Assign MIDI" and "Clear MIDI" BUILT 2026-08-02 as Tools >
  Assign MIDI CC to Knobs / Clear All MIDI CC (menus.c, midi_cc_assign_all_knobs /
  midi_cc_clear_all). Numbering follows the manual (p.126): lowest free CC# to the first assigned
  knob in page order, and parameters that already carry a CC keep it, so a hand-numbered patch is
  filled in rather than renumbered. Per-parameter right-click "Assign MIDI CC" already existed and
  was not touched.
  THE FIRST VERSION LANDED ONLY SOMETIMES, and the reason generalises to any future bulk operation
  on this device. Every slot command is stamped with the slot's patch version by usb_cmd_slot()
  (usbComms.c), and that version is only ever refreshed when the G2's ASYNCHRONOUS
  SUB_RESPONSE_PATCH_VERSION_CHANGE (0x38) notification is parsed — nothing bumps it locally after
  a send. int_rec() returns the moment it sees the expected SUB_RESPONSE_OK and does not drain
  what else is pending, so in a burst, command N+1 races the version bump from command N and the
  G2 rejects whichever loses. It was silent because send_assign_midi_cc()'s return value is
  discarded by its caller and the local table had already recorded the change — so the UI showed
  CC#s the device never accepted. Single right-click assigns never hit it: one command, with idle
  time either side for the 0x38 to arrive.
  FIXED by sending the whole slot as ONE eMsgCmdWritePatch instead. write_controllers()
  (protocol.c) already serialises the entire controller table, so all 120 assignments cross in a
  single versioned command and there is nothing to race. eMsgCmdWritePatch was already used this
  way from the UI thread for something as ordinary as a patch-notes edit (mouseHandle.c,
  mousePanels.c) — no synth stop, no ceremony.
  UNDO SURVIVES, and was in fact broken the same way before this: apply_midi_cc() (undo.c) replayed
  the same burst of per-entry deassign/assign commands, so Ctrl-Z after a sweep landed only partly.
  It now drives the local table to the target image and sends the same single patch write. Fixing
  it also fixed a second bug it always had — it never maintained the hasMidiCC shadow on the module
  params, which is what the canvas and the MIDI CC overlay actually read, so an undone CC stayed
  lit on the module it used to belong to.
  STILL TO DO:
   1. UNCONFIRMED ON HARDWARE. Builds clean; the diagnosis above is from code reading, not from a
      capture. To confirm: uncomment ENABLE_USB_LOG (defs.h) and read ~/G2_usb.log — byte 2 of each
      TX is the version stamp. The old symptom was that stamp staying flat across consecutive TXs
      while the 0x38 RX lines lagged behind.
   2. Global pages are not covered — the sweep walks gKnobArray[slot] only, and Global-page knobs
      live in gGlobalKnobArray. The original's Assign MIDI sits on the Parameter Overview, which
      has a Global Pages toggle.
   3. The button half of a combined knob+button assignment is skipped: tKnob.isLed is unused by the
      sweep, but the manual's View Buttons section says a Sequencer module's knob and its
      pushbutton can each hold a DIFFERENT CC#. One CC per knob entry is half the job. Ties into
      "View Buttons" below.
   4. next_free_midi_cc() starts at CC 0 — worth confirming the G2 accepts 0 (Bank Select). The
      manual only says "first available MIDI CC#" without stating a floor.
   5. "View Buttons" (toggle the popup overlay between a combined dial+button assignment's knob
      half and button half, e.g. on Sequencer modules) is not built.
- Assign / Deassign MIDI to Selection — BUILT 2026-08-02 as Tools > Assign MIDI CC to Selection /
  Deassign MIDI CC from Selection (menus.c, midi_cc_assign_selection / midi_cc_deassign_selection).
  A DIFFERENT OPERATION from "Assign MIDI CC to Knobs" above despite the similar name, and the two
  are easy to confuse: that one walks the 120 PANEL KNOB ASSIGNMENTS in page order, so it only ever
  touches parameters already placed on a Parameter Page. These walk EVERY PARAMETER OF THE SELECTED
  MODULES, knob or no knob (manual p.130, p.143: "all parameters of the selected modules will be
  automatically assigned to MIDI CC# numbers"). One fills in a control surface, the other blankets
  a few modules.
  Everything else is shared with the bulk tools: one undo bracket over the whole controller table,
  the assign fills gaps rather than renumbering, and a single whole-patch write at the end.
  Deassign walks the controller table BACKWARDS, because remove_controller_entry() compacts by
  swapping the last entry into the hole and a forward walk would step over whatever moved down.
  Both menu entries grey out with no selection, as the original's do.
  STILL TO DO: unverified — needs a selection made on real hardware. Note it only covers modules in
  the SELECTED Slot; a selection can't span Slots today, so that guard is belt-and-braces.

- Virtual Keyboard — BUILT 2026-08-02 (src/virtualKeyboard.c / .h), opened from TOOLS > Virtual
  Keyboard (owner's call 2026-08-02, and where the original keeps it — Parameter Pages/Overview
  stay under Settings). NO Ctrl-K, consistent with those two.
  Three octaves of clickable keys with the four scroll buttons the original has (double arrows an
  octave, singles a note), C labelled with its octave number, the sounding key shown both by
  highlight and by the black dot the manual describes, and the note sustaining while the button is
  held. One note at a time, which is what a mouse and the manual's "single notes" imply. Every
  change of note funnels through set_sounding_note(), so closing the panel or scrolling the range
  always sends the note-off first and a note can never hang.
  DRONE AND REPEAT added 2026-08-02, the original's other two buttons (manual p.128 — the owner
  asked for "Hold", which the original calls DRONE, so the button says Drone):
    * Drone — "make the next played note start sounding infinitely. Click the Drone button again
      to disengage." Mouse-up stops sending the note-off; disengaging silences whatever is running.
    * Repeat — "make the last played note play repeatedly." Re-struck note-off-then-on (so the
      patch's envelopes actually retrigger) from virtual_keyboard_tick(), driven by the render
      loop. THE RATE IS A GUESS: 250ms, since neither the manual nor the original exposes one. If
      the real editor locks it to the master clock, VKB_REPEAT_MS is the constant to replace.
      This is also why graphics.c grew a virtual_keyboard_wants_ticks() branch — the idle path
      sleeps in glfwWaitEvents(), which would stall a repeat until the next input event.
    Neither toggle survives closing the panel: both leave the synth making a noise, and reopening
    to find it already droning would be a nasty surprise.
  NOTE ON/OFF CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE 2026-08-02, after one real bug: THE FLAG BYTE IS INVERTED FROM
  THE OBVIOUS READING. ZERO SOUNDS THE NOTE, ONE RELEASES IT. Sent as 1-for-on first, which gave a
  keyboard that was exactly backwards — silent on press, sounding on release. So CMPlayNote::
  EAction's value 1 is the note-OFF action and the reference's `action == 1` is asking "is this a
  release?", not "is this a press?". Nothing in the reference says which way round the enum runs;
  only the hardware did. A good example of why a reference settles the SHAPE of a message but not
  its polarity.
  Worth recording how it was found, since the same confusion could recur: the symptom looked like a
  missing note-off, and the UI path was suspected first. It was traced and cleared —
  mouse_button() → convert_to_mouse_button() maps GLFW_RELEASE to mouseButtonLeftUp →
  handle_virtual_keyboard_mouse() → set_sounding_note(-1) → send_note(note, false) — and a
  temporary LOG_DEBUG in send_note() then showed ON and OFF being sent in the right order at the
  right times, which pinned it on the wire encoding rather than the plumbing. That log line has
  since been removed.
  OWNER CONFIRMED 2026-08-02: the keyboard and all six of its buttons work on hardware — keys,
  the four scroll buttons, Drone and Repeat.
  THE WIRE FORMAT IS REVERSE-ENGINEERED. SUB_COMMAND_PLAY_NOTE (0x56) was already
  in defs.h but had never been sent. CMPlayNote::WriteStream() in the reference is:
      CMIDIOutStream::Initialize(stream, 0x56, 0, 1);
      <write>(stream, action == 1);      // note-on/note-off flag
      <write>(stream, note);             // MIDI note number
  Both values go through the same vtable slot (+0x58) that CMCtrlAssign::WriteStream() uses for the
  location and param-index bytes of SUB_COMMAND_ASSIGN_MIDICC, which G2-Edit already sends as plain
  8-bit fields — hence 8 bits each. No slot field in the class, so send_play_note() frames it as a
  SYS command and lets the synth route the note by its own keyboard assignment.
  Sent with COMMAND_WRITE_NO_RESP and no send_and_receive(), deliberately: a held key fires many of
  these and an unconsumed ack is exactly what desynchronises the next command (see the bulk MIDI CC
  entry). send_set_param_value() is the precedent. IF NOTHING SOUNDS, that framing is the first
  thing to doubt — the panel itself is ordinary drawing and hit-testing.
  Velocity is accepted by the caller but not sent; the reference writes two values only.
  STILL TO DO:
   1. Repeat's 250ms rate works but has never been compared against the original's, which exposes
      no control over it either. VKB_REPEAT_MS is the constant if it should track the master clock.
   2. The original expands its range by drag-resizing the window frame, and hides its button bar
      the same way. This panel has a fixed 37-key span and the scroll buttons instead.

- ASSIGN MIDI CC WIRE FORMAT CONFIRMED 2026-08-05 from an owner USB capture of the ORIGINAL editor
  performing a MIDI Learn. The single command it sent was:
      00 0c 01 28 01 22 01 01 07 10 79 3d
      len=000c  ver=28  cmd=01  sub=22 (ASSIGN_MIDICC)  loc=01 mod=01 param=07 cc=10  crc=793d
  So the payload is location/module/param/CC as four plain 8-bit fields, and THE CC IS WRITTEN WITH
  ITS TOP BIT CLEAR (0x10 for CC 16). That is byte-for-byte what send_assign_midi_cc() already
  builds, so G2-Edit's assign is correct as it stands.
  This substantially weakens the "top bit set" reading recorded below, since assign and deassign
  were supposed to share that encoding. Deassign itself was not in the capture, so it stays open —
  but do not "fix" it on the strength of the inferred widths alone.

- WHILE DECODING PLAY NOTE, A QUESTION ABOUT send_deassign_midi_cc() TURNED UP. CMCtrlDeassign::
  WriteStream() (0x23) writes a 1-bit field of value 1 (vtable +0x44) followed by the CC number as
  SEVEN bits (+0x3c, which takes an explicit width) — i.e. a byte of 0x80|cc. G2-Edit writes eight
  bits of cc, so 0x00|cc. CMCtrlAssign (0x22) ends the same way. If the reference's widths are read
  right, both our assign and deassign have the top bit clear where the original sets it. DELIBERATELY
  NOT CHANGED: the vtable-slot widths are inferred, per-param assign is believed to work, and this
  wants a USB-log comparison against the real editor rather than a guess. Worth settling, since it
  would also explain any deassign that silently does nothing.
