- Patch Adjuster — BUILT 2026-08-02 (src/patchAdjuster.c / .h), Tools > Patch Adjuster. The manual's
  chapter 8 (p.113), and NOT the Mutator despite both being ways to shape a patch without hunting
  for parameters: the Mutator generates new patches at random, this is deterministic and semantic.
  Eight knobs — Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release, Mod. Rate, Timbre, Resonance, Effects — each of
  which nudges EVERY parameter of its category across the whole patch at once.
  NOT PATCH PARAMETERS AND NOT MORPH GROUPS. The manual is explicit that they "act as remote,
  relative editing tools" and consequently "cannot be assigned to MIDI Controllers or to physical
  knobs on the synth". Nothing about the knobs is stored in the patch; only the parameters they move
  are, which is why none of this needed a protocol change.
  THE CURVE, decoded from CPatch::ApplyDistribution() in the reference — knobs run -50..+50 with 0
  at centre, working from a BASELINE snapshot rather than from live values:
      amount > 0:  new = orig + (max - orig) * amount/50      (toward maximum)
      amount < 0:  new = orig * (50 + amount)/50              (toward zero)
      amount == 0: untouched
  Working from a baseline is what makes the manual's described behaviour fall out: returning a knob
  to centre restores its category exactly, and several knobs can be off-centre at once without
  fighting, since each owns a disjoint set of parameters.
  SENT ONE PARAMETER AT A TIME, the opposite of the bulk MIDI CC tools above, and deliberately:
  send_param_value() is a COMMAND_WRITE_NO_RESP write expecting no acknowledgement — what the
  canvas's own dial drags have always used — so there is no ack to be left in the pipe and no
  patch-version race to lose. Only parameters whose computed value actually CHANGES are sent, which
  is the original's own test and matters because a Timbre sweep over a dense patch touches a lot.
  COMMIT: the manual says the change becomes permanent "as soon as you move the focus to another
  variation or add or remove a module". check_for_commit() notices both by polling the active
  variation and a module count once a frame, which keeps those code paths ignorant of this panel.
  CLASSIFICATION IS AN APPROXIMATION, and this is the part to be sceptical of. The original matches
  the G2's own parameter-TYPE ids (Attack 0x0b; Decay 0x0c,0x10; Sustain 0x0d; Release 0x0e; Mod
  Rate 0x16,0x41,0x88; Resonance 0x12,0x87; Timbre 0x05,0x06,0x11,0x22,0x8b; Effects 0x29,0x83 —
  all recorded in patchAdjuster.c). G2-Edit's paramLocationList does NOT carry those ids, so this
  classifies on (module type, param type, label) as mutator.c's classify_param() already does. The
  manual is loose in the same places ("various waveshape parameters", "some Time parameters"), so
  exact parity was never reachable from the documentation either. Unambiguous cases are claimed;
  ambiguous ones are left alone, on the grounds that a knob missing a parameter beats one mangling
  an unrelated parameter you won't notice until you hear it.
  STILL TO DO:
   1. NO UNDO. Every other edit in the app pushes one; this pushes nothing, so a commit cannot be
      taken back with Ctrl-Z. Returning a knob to centre before the commit is the only way back.
      The right shape is a new eUndoCmdParamBulk holding before/after images, exactly as
      eUndoCmdMidiCc and eUndoCmdCableEdit already do — the baseline array IS the "before" image,
      so most of the work is already sitting there.
   2. UNVERIFIED BEYOND RENDERING. The panel draws correctly (checked 2026-08-02) but no knob has
      been moved: synthetic drags don't reach the app, and the test instance was online with real
      hardware, so a sweep would have edited a live patch. Needs an ear on it.
   3. Classification gaps, all the same kind — a parameter whose module gives it meaning but whose
      type is the catch-all paramTypeCommonDial: LfoB's rate (two undistinguished CommonDials), FM
      and shaper amounts that Timbre should claim, dry/wet on FX modules not labelled "Dry/Wet".
      Each is a module-name case in adjuster_classify_param().
   4. The reference has a special case this doesn't: module type 0x54's param 3 counts as Decay or
      Release depending on the value of its param 7. Which module that is hasn't been identified.

- Delete Unused Modules / Select All / Paste Params / Send Controller Snapshot — ALL FOUR BUILT
  2026-08-02.
  * DELETE UNUSED MODULES — module-area right-click menu, beside the existing "Delete unused
    cables". "Unused" means no cable touches the module at either end, which is the original's own
    test and deliberately blunter than "contributes no audio": a module feeding a muted chain is
    still wired up, and guessing at intent would delete work. Goes through the SELECTION rather
    than deleting directly, so it inherits the existing undo_push_delete_selection() bracket and
    one Ctrl-Z takes the whole sweep back.
  * SELECT ALL — Cmd-A, plus a module-area menu entry. Scoped to the location being viewed, not the
    whole patch: the canvas only ever shows VA or FX, so selecting modules you cannot see and might
    then Cut would be a trap. Given a shortcut, unlike the panels, because Cut/Copy/Paste/Undo
    already carry theirs and this is an edit command of the same kind. VERIFIED 2026-08-02.
  * PASTE PARAMS — module right-click menu. Pastes the copied module's parameter values onto the
    right-clicked module, leaving that module, its cables and its position alone. All variations
    are pasted, not just the active one, because that is what was copied and pasting one would
    leave the others silently disagreeing. Greyed unless the clipboard holds a module of the SAME
    TYPE — a Decay from an EnvADSR has no counterpart on an OscB. No Ctrl-E: it needs a right-
    clicked target, and there is no "focused module" concept to hang a shortcut on.
  * SEND CONTROLLER SNAPSHOT — Tools menu, greyed offline (it asks the G2 to transmit, so there is
    nothing to transmit from). SUB_COMMAND_CTRL_SNAPSHOT (0x55) was in defs.h but had never been
    sent. CMSendCtrlSnap::WriteStream() in the reference is a BARE command with no payload at all,
    and the (id, 0, 1) Initialize shape is CALIBRATED rather than guessed: CMCurrentNotesRequest
    has the identical shape with id 0x68, which G2-Edit already implements as usb_cmd_slot
    (COMMAND_REQ) with no payload — and which works. UNTESTED on hardware; needs an external device
    listening to confirm anything actually goes out.
  ONE REAL BUG FOUND BY LOOKING AT IT: inserting Paste Params into the module menu's array shifted
  the entries under two hard-coded indices, so the exclude-from-mutation label overwrote "Delete"
  and the entry vanished from the menu entirely. Both indices are now named constants
  (kItemPasteParams / kItemExclude) so the next insertion is harmless. The Tools menu's items[]
  had the same shape of latent problem and was resized twice today; worth a look at whether any
  other menu still hard-codes an index.
  STILL TO DO: only Select All and the two menus' contents have been verified on screen. Delete
  Unused Modules, Paste Params and Send Controller Snapshot are unrun — the test instance was
  online with real hardware and all three write.

- MIDI Learn (L key) — BUILT 2026-08-05. Click a parameter, press L, and it takes whichever MIDI
  CC# arrived most recently (manual p.145). Only became possible once the local MIDI input existed —
  before that nothing in the app knew what the "last received" CC was.
  FOUR SMALL PIECES:
   * midiInput.c records every incoming CC into an atomic, EXCEPT 120/123 (All Sound Off / All Notes
     Off are channel-mode messages, not controllers anything could sensibly be learned from). It is
     stored before the morph handling, so a CC this editor does nothing else with is still learnable.
   * gParamFocus (types.h/globalVars) remembers the last parameter CLICKED. gParamDragging could not
     serve: it is cleared on mouse-up and only ever covers the draggable param types, whereas a
     button can carry a CC too.
     SET IN param_click_handler() (moduleGraphics.c), NOT in mouseHandle.c. This cost a round trip
     and is worth knowing generally: handle_module_press()/handle_module_press_for_module() LOOK
     like the canvas click path and are not — every widget registers a click region at render time
     and dispatch_click_region() matches first, so those functions are legacy fallback that no
     longer runs for a canvas parameter. mouse_button() says so in a comment; I put the focus there
     anyway and it was simply never executed.
   * assign_midi_cc_to_param() extracted VERBATIM out of action_assign_midi_cc() (menus.c) so the
     right-click "Assign to CC# nn" menu and MIDI Learn cannot drift apart. The menu action is now
     four lines calling it. Undo, the CC steal and the device message are all unchanged.
   * The L key is the only BARE-key shortcut in the app — every other one is Command-modified — so
     it needed its own branch in key_callback() ahead of the gCommandKeyPressed block.
  STEALING IS CORRECT, not a side effect: the manual is explicit that "a MIDI CC# can only be
  assigned to one single knob in the patch and if L assigns a MIDI CC# to a knob that was already
  assigned to another knob the other knob will loose its assignment".
  SILENT WHEN IT CANNOT ACT (no focused param, no CC yet, focus in another slot) — reason goes to
  the log only. It is the fast alternative to the right-click menu, and a dialog on every stray L
  would defeat the point. The manual makes the same trade, calling the menu "a more safe way".
  STRICT ORDER: select a parameter, THEN press L. An "either order" version was built at the
  owner's request and then withdrawn by them the same day — arming on L and letting the next click
  take the CC leaves hidden state lying around, which is the opposite of what a one-key shortcut
  should do.
  L CONSUMES THE FOCUS, whether or not the assignment goes through, so a second press cannot
  reassign the parameter still sitting in focus from the first. The right-click "Assign to CC# nn
  (last received)" entry clears it the same way, so neither route can be repeated by accident.
  That menu entry was also removed and then restored at the owner's request; it is the manual's own
  alternative to L (p.145 — right-click and "first check if the Assign to CC# xx menu item indeed
  shows the MIDI CC# that the other instrument is sending"), and the 128-entry picker beside it
  cannot answer that question. When no CC has arrived it shows a greyed "No MIDI CC received yet"
  rather than vanishing, so the absence explains itself.
  NO ON-SCREEN FEEDBACK, by the owner's decision: the transient notices and the green focus ring
  were both built and removed. Everything logs instead.
  A LESSON FROM THE RING, worth keeping even though the ring is gone: gParamRectangle holds the rect
  the widget actually drew, in WINDOW coordinates — which is why mouseHandle.c hit-tests it against
  the raw cursor position. Drawing it through moduleArea applies the canvas zoom and scroll a SECOND
  time, which put the ring well below its parameter. The overlay chips DO use moduleArea correctly,
  because they are queued from the rectangle passed INTO the renderer, not the one it returns.
  A transient on-canvas notice for the other two moments (a CC arriving, and the L outcome including
  its refusals) was built and then REMOVED the same day at the owner's request, along with the
  "Assign to CC# nn (last received)" right-click entry. The refusals log instead. Worth knowing if
  the question comes back: the L key is deliberately quiet, so when it declines there is now nothing
  on screen saying why — the ring showing no parameter is focused is the only visible clue.

  ALSO ADDED 2026-08-05, after the owner reported L doing nothing with no way to tell why: the param
  right-click menu now carries "Assign to CC# nn (last received)" above the 128-entry picker, or a
  greyed "No MIDI CC received yet" when none has arrived. This IS the manual's own workflow — p.145
  says to right-click and "first check if the Assign to CC# xx menu item indeed shows the MIDI CC#
  that the other instrument is sending", calling it the safe alternative to L — so the 128-entry
  picker, which cannot answer that question, was only ever half of that menu. It doubles as the
  diagnostic: if it reads "No MIDI CC received yet" then L is correctly doing nothing.
  WHERE "LAST RECEIVED" COMES FROM — REWORKED 2026-08-05 after the owner pointed out the obvious:
  THE SYNTH IS THE THING WITH A MIDI IN SOCKET, so in the setup the manual describes it is the G2
  that sees the controller and the editor never does. Learning from the editor's own CoreMIDI input
  only works when the controller is plugged into the Mac. Both are now accepted, synth first.
  The synth side goes through SUB_RESPONSE_MIDI_CC (0x80), which parse_midi_cc() has always decoded
  into a channel and a CC byte and then THROWN AWAY. Now recorded.
  TWO REAL BUGS FIXED HERE:
   * 0xff IS A SENTINEL meaning "nothing to report", and it was being stored as though it were CC
     255. Controller numbers stop at 127, so anything above that is now rejected. This is what the
     owner saw as "thinks the last CC received was 255" — it was the reply to the request issued at
     CONNECT, before any controller had been touched.
   * THE REQUEST WAS ONLY EVER SENT AT CONNECT (send_get_midi_cc(), usbComms.c:3469/3528), i.e.
     exactly when the answer is guaranteed to be "none". New eMsgCmdGetMidiCC lets the UI ask again.
  L NOW COMPLETES ASYNCHRONOUSLY when online: the press parks the target parameter, posts the
  request, and midi_learn_poll() (once a frame, from render_frame) finishes the assignment when a
  FRESH reply lands — freshness being a rise in the arrival count, not merely a non-negative value,
  since the previous answer is still in hand. Gives up after ~2s and logs that the synth reported
  nothing. The param right-click menu re-requests as it opens and names its source, so the label
  reads "Assign to CC# 74 (last, from synth)" or "(from MIDI in)".
  SETTLED 2026-08-05 BY AN OWNER USB CAPTURE of the original editor doing a Learn (new patch, all
  MIDI deassigned, one filter, controller into the G2's MIDI IN, dial moved, parameter clicked, L
  pressed). THE G2 PUSHES IT UNSOLICITED — nothing is requested:
      t=3.722  G2 -> editor   82 01 04 00 80 00 10 e5 cd
                                          ^^ ^^ ^^
                                     sub=80  chan=00  cc=10
      t=19.213 editor -> G2   00 0c 01 28 01 22 01 01 07 10 79 3d      (ASSIGN, cc=10)
  So SUB_RESPONSE_MIDI_CC arrives on its own when the synth receives a CC that is assigned to
  NOTHING, carrying the channel and then the controller NUMBER — which also settles the old
  "CC Numb/value" ambiguity in parse_midi_cc(): it is the NUMBER. The editor just remembers it, and
  the Learn is a single assign command with no query anywhere in it.
  SUB_COMMAND_GET_MIDI_CC'S REPLY IS ONE RECORD PER SLOT — the owner spotted it from a clean log
  showing exactly four records, the first carrying CC 20 and the other three 0xff. So it is the last
  CC EACH SLOT has seen, not a list of assignments, and 0xff means that slot has seen none. It is
  what makes Learn work immediately after the editor starts, instead of demanding the controller be
  moved again. gLastDeviceMidiCC is now per slot and midi_learn_last_cc() reads the current one.
  (An intermediate version treated this reply as assignments and DISCARDED it, which was wrong for
  the same reason the very first version was wrong — it is the useful message, not the noise.)
  IT HOLDS SEVERAL RECORDS SEPARATED BY A REPEAT OF THE 0x80 SUB-RESPONSE BYTE, while the
  unsolicited notification is a single record with no separator. A flat two-byte stride walked the
  reply out of step, which is how it announced itself: "channel 129", i.e. a 0x80 separator read as
  a channel. The loop now consumes a separator between records and stops when the next byte is not
  0x80, and files each record by POSITION when solicited, by the message's own slot when not.
  STILL UNSETTLED: whether the first byte of a record is the MIDI CHANNEL or the SLOT. Every sample
  so far has 0 in it, so nothing distinguishes them; it is logged, not used. If it is the slot, the
  0xff records will carry 1, 2 and 3 — which the next connect log will show.
  A MIDI CHANNEL IS 0..15, so a channel byte above 15 now aborts the walk with an error rather than
  reporting invented controllers — every value after a desync is worthless and MIDI Learn would
  otherwise take the last of them. Worth applying wherever a parser has a field with a known range.

  THE REASON IT HAD NEVER WORKED, found by the owner reading parse_midi_cc(): its loop guard asked
  for THREE bytes (`(BIT_TO_BYTE_ROUND_UP(bitPos) + 3) <= length`) while the payload is TWO, so the
  loop never ran and the message was parsed into nothing. Two separate mistakes in the same loop:
    * the guard did not match the body, which read channel + controller + a trailing byte it hoped
      would be another 0x80 marking a further record;
    * there is no marker byte. The capture is 82 01 04 00 80 00 10 e5 cd — sub-response, then the
      two-byte payload, then the CRC. The third read was taking the CRC (seen as 0xa5 in the log).
  Now `(... + 2) <= length`, and the trailing read is gone. Note the guard must require a WHOLE
  record rather than merely a byte remaining: `< length` happens to work for a lone two-byte payload
  but would let a stray trailing byte start a record that reads past the end.

  CONSEQUENCE: the poll-the-synth-on-L machinery built earlier the same day was the wrong shape and
  has been REMOVED (eMsgCmdGetMidiCC, request_synth_last_cc(), midi_learn_poll() and its per-frame
  call). L assigns synchronously from the value parse_midi_cc() has already recorded, exactly as the
  original does. send_get_midi_cc() at connect is untouched — it is pre-existing and harmless.
  WHAT REMAINS UNPROVEN: whether 0x81 answers anything useful. It might instead be the stored assignment TABLE — parse_midi_cc() loops over
  MULTIPLE records, which fits a table better than a single last-received value, and the name says
  "GET_MIDI_CC" rather than anything about receiving. No dedicated learn command exists among the
  SUB_COMMAND_* constants (checked: the only never-sent ones are CLEAR_BANK, MIDI_DUMP,
  QUERY_PARAM_NAMES, QUERY_PARAMS, SET_ASSIGNED_VOICES, SET_KEY_SPLIT, SET_PERF_MODE — none of them
  plausible). THE TEST: with a controller into the G2's own MIDI IN, move one control, then press L
  or right-click a parameter. If the CC appears, 0x81 is the right question. If it stays "No MIDI CC
  received yet" while the synth is plainly receiving, then the G2 does not report received CCs this
  way and the feature needs a different message — which would want a USB-log capture of the real
  editor doing a Learn to settle.
  NOT VERIFIED, and it cannot be through the harness: synthetic KEY events do not reach the app at
  all (the same limitation already recorded for the Parameter Overview's Escape), and neither do
  synthetic clicks, so BOTH halves of "click a knob, press L" are out of reach. What IS known: the
  assign half is the pre-existing menu path moved verbatim, so only the CC tracking, the click
  focus and the key branch are genuinely new. NEEDS THE OWNER: send a CC from a controller, click a
  knob, press L, and confirm the module shows the CC and that the previous owner lost it.
- Patch Window Split Bar — STEP 1 OF 4 DONE 2026-08-02, in SYNTHLIB (utilsGraphics.c/.h), so it
  lands in that submodule's own commit, not this repo's.
  WHAT MADE THIS WIDE-RANGING, and what step 1 addressed: tArea is only an enum, and `moduleArea`
  selects a transform chain — scale by gZoomFactor, subtract the scroll, offset into module_area()
  — where all three were SINGLETONS. There was exactly one of everything, which is what a split
  view breaks. Step 1 introduced a MODULE PANE owning its scroll position and its slice of the
  canvas band, with MAX_MODULE_PANES 2 but only one pane in use, so behaviour is unchanged.
  New API: set_module_pane() / module_pane() / module_pane_count() / set_module_pane_count() /
  set_module_pane_extent() / module_area_for_pane(). The transform reads the CURRENT pane in the
  same "mode rather than argument" style set_param_render_area() already uses for the parameter
  renderers — rendering is sequential, so a mode suffices and no pane argument has to be threaded
  through the hundreds of render calls.
  SLICES ARE FRACTIONS of the canvas band, not pixels, so a window resize redistributes the split
  proportionally rather than stranding a pane at a fixed height. Pane 0 is {top 0.0, height 1.0},
  which is arithmetically the whole band — the same rectangle module_area() returned before.
  ZOOM STAYS GLOBAL, deliberately. Zoom is G2-Edit's own invention (the original editor has none),
  so there is no parity to satisfy and it was a free design choice: matched scale is what makes two
  areas comparable across the divider, and it keeps scale() a plain global multiply for the many
  callers that have no idea panes exist. Promoting zoom into the pane later is contained.
  VERIFIED BY A/B 2026-08-02, and the method is worth reusing for any "no visible change" refactor
  here: screenshots at default zoom, at ZOOM 1.5 + SCROLL 0.5 0.4, and at ZOOM 0.5 + SCROLL 1.0 1.0
  (the last two exercise exactly the changed code), captured before and after via the backdoor.
  Byte comparison ALONE IS USELESS — the same build differs between runs, because the module LEDs
  are live and blinking. The discriminator is magnitude: LED noise touches tens of rows, a broken
  transform would move every module and touch ~1000. Control (same build twice) was 22/0/0
  differing rows; test (panes vs baseline) was 23/0/0. Both zoomed captures pixel-identical.
  STEP 2 OF 4 DONE 2026-08-02 — src/splitView.c/.h in this repo, plus a clipping addition in
  SynthLib. Two panes, Voice Area above and FX Area below, a draggable bar between them carrying
  the manual's three buttons (top full height / bottom full height / restore). The VA and FX topbar
  buttons became the manual's "give this area the whole window", and clicking the area that is
  ALREADY full re-opens the split, so one button round-trips both ways without a separate control.
  The divider is remembered across a trip through FULL. Default is still ONE pane showing the Voice
  Area, i.e. the pre-split behaviour, so nothing changes until the split is asked for.
  gLocation IS SET FROM THE PANE A PRESS LANDS IN, before the click is interpreted — that one line
  is what lets all 40-odd existing gLocation readers keep working without learning about panes.
  TWO REAL BUGS FOUND BY LOOKING AT IT, both invisible to the compiler:
   1. NOTHING IN SYNTHLIB CLIPPED. Modules taller than their pane, or scrolled to straddle the
      divider, drew straight on into the neighbouring pane; and render_modules() deliberately still
      draws connectors for CULLED modules (so cables keep their endpoints), which used to be safely
      off-screen and now landed in the other half of the window. Fixed by adding
      module_pane_clip_begin()/_end() (glScissor) around each pane's render pass — one fix for both
      symptoms, since the strays are outside the pane rect too. NOTE the flip: glScissor is in
      framebuffer pixels with a BOTTOM-left origin, everything else here is scaled window units
      with a TOP-left origin.
   2. tTriangle's second and third vertices are RELATIVE to the first, not absolute. Passing three
      absolute points drew garbage, which is what the bar's arrow buttons did at first.
  ALSO: param_overlay_begin_frame() moved out of render_modules() into render_frame(). That
  function now runs once PER PANE, and resetting the overlay queue between panes threw away what
  the first pane had queued. The rubber band, also inside render_modules(), is now drawn only in
  the focused pane for the same reason.
  VERIFIED 2026-08-02: default single-pane view unchanged; the split lays out, clips cleanly at the
  divider, and the bar chrome draws correctly. Pane 1's render path was proven by TEMPORARILY
  pointing both panes at the Voice Area and confirming two independent, independently-scrolled
  copies — worth knowing because NONE OF THE TEST PATCHES HAVE MODULES IN THE FX AREA (RndFilter
  and EnvFx both put everything in the Voice Area, FX cycles read 0.0%), so an empty lower pane is
  correct for them and proves nothing on its own.
  STEP 2 FINISHED 2026-08-03. Four changes on top of the above, all verified on screen:
   * THE DIVIDER IS NOW PATCH DATA AND ALWAYS ON SCREEN. gPatchDescr[slot].barPosition is the source
     of truth, in PIXELS of Voice Area height, so it round-trips to file and device for free and
     each Slot remembers its own. The separate "one area full height" MODE is gone: full height is
     just the divider at an end. tSplitView lost split/divider/fullLocation/restoreDivider.
   * A GAP EITHER SIDE OF THE BAR (SPLIT_BAR_GAP = MODULE_MARGIN), so modules never butt against it
     — the same courtesy the top bar already gets. The bar's vertical FOOTPRINT is therefore larger
     than the bar, which is what the pane extents reserve.
   * VA/FX LIGHT ONLY WHEN THAT AREA HAS THE WHOLE WINDOW, neither when both are visible — driven
     from split_view_apply() each frame so it is right after a patch load too, not just after a
     click. This could not use set_exclusive_button_highlight(), which always lights exactly one.
   * THE WHEEL ACTS ON THE PANE UNDER THE CURSOR, not the focused one — hover the FX half and scroll
     and the FX half moves, no click needed. set_x/y_scroll_bar() already write through to the
     current pane, so pointing the current pane at the hovered one is the whole mechanism.
  NEW-PATCH DEFAULT is now 300 (a visible split) rather than the reference's 4000 (Voice Area takes
  everything) — owner's call, on the grounds that the divider is the point of the window. Patches
  from file or device carry their own value and are untouched.
  A TESTING LESSON WORTH MORE THAN THE FEATURE: a run of "the drag doesn't work" results were
  BOTH OF US DRIVING THE MOUSE AT THE SAME TIME. Synthetic clicks and a human hand collide silently
  and the failures look like real bugs — one nearly became a false diagnosis about the device
  round-trip reverting the divider. When driving the app, put the window fully on screen and say so,
  and treat any weird interactive result as suspect until it reproduces with nobody else touching it.
  FULLY CONFIRMED 2026-08-03 on "1 Mini Emulator" (Bank 1 Loc 10 on the owner's G2), which is the
  first patch tried that has REAL CONTENT IN BOTH AREAS — none of the PatchTestFiles do, so use a
  factory patch for this from now on. Voice Area above with its modules and cables, FX Area below
  with Fx-In/DelayB/Reverb/Mix4-1S and cables, clipped cleanly at the divider, gap either side,
  and both topbar buttons correctly unlit. Loading the patch also snapped the divider to that
  patch's OWN stored barPosition — the per-patch persistence working end to end.
  STEP 3 DONE 2026-08-03 — per-pane scrollbars, plus two owner-requested changes that landed with
  them:
   * ONE VERTICAL AND ONE HORIZONTAL BAR PER PANE, each spanning only that pane and driving only its
     own scroll. The window's single shared pair could not do this: two panes scroll independently,
     so one thumb could only ever tell the truth about one of them. The horizontal was tried as a
     single shared bar following the focused pane first and it was WRONG for exactly that reason —
     the wheel already scrolls each pane's X independently, so a shared bar either misreported a
     pane or dragged them into lockstep and undid what the wheel had just done. The reference agrees:
     its layout adds a scrollbar's height into the TOP pane's extent, so the original has one each
     too. The top pane gives up SCROLLBAR_WIDTH at its foot for its bar; the bottom pane's lives in
     the strip the canvas band already leaves above the window bottom.
   * THUMBS HAVE ROUNDED ENDS — body rectangle short by one radius at each end, capped with a filled
     circle, radius half the thumb's short side. Square ends read as truncated, as though the thumb
     had been clipped by its track rather than sitting in it.
   * THUMBS ARE NOW PROPORTIONAL — length is the fraction of the content currently visible, so it
     grows as you zoom out and shrinks as you zoom in, with the position being that fraction slid
     along the remaining track. The old bars drew a FIXED-LENGTH block that said nothing about how
     much content there was, which was the least standard thing about them. Clamped at
     PANE_SCROLL_MIN_THUMB (24px) so a big patch can never shrink the thumb below grabbable, and
     inset by PANE_SCROLL_INSET so it floats in its track rather than filling it.
     Both are DERIVED from the pane's scroll percent each frame rather than tracked alongside it, so
     they cannot drift out of step with a pane scrolled by the wheel or by a zoom.
     gScrollState's own xThumb/yThumb are no longer drawn or hit-tested; only the dead corner square
     is still drawn by render_scrollbars().
   * VA AND FX TOPBAR BUTTONS REMOVED (owner's call), since the divider is permanently on screen and
     the bar's own up/down arrows already give "this area takes the whole window". Dropped from
     topbarControls.def, and with them went their init colours and the highlight-follows-state
     helper added earlier the same day.
  SMOKE TESTED 2026-08-03 before the community release, on "1 Mini Emulator" with both areas
  populated and the G2 online. Verified: right-click on a module in the BOTTOM pane opens the module
  menu anchored correctly with the right entries; clicking a MENU-type parameter in the bottom pane
  (Reverb's room size) opens its dropdown with the right options — i.e. a click in the lower pane
  resolves to the correct module AND parameter, which was the one failure mode that would have been
  data corruption rather than cosmetics. No crash, no error lines in the log across the session, and
  no crash reports generated.
  STILL TO DO: 4. per-pane zoom, only if wanted — and nothing needs it yet.
  KNOWN ROUGH EDGES GOING INTO THE RELEASE, none blocking but worth knowing:
   * Module drag, rubber band and cable drag are still NOT clamped to the pane they start in. A drag
     that crosses the divider keeps computing in the originating pane's coordinate space, so a
     module dropped over the other pane lands at a far row rather than where the cursor is. Not a
     regression (the old single canvas behaved the same past its edge) but the divider makes it look
     like it ought to work.
   * The Patch Adjuster has NO UNDO and its parameter classification is approximate.
   * Send Controller Snapshot has never been exercised — needs an external device listening.
   * Assign/Deassign MIDI CC to Selection, Delete Unused Modules and Paste Params are built and
     reviewed but were not run on hardware.
  NOT YET RUN: the proportional thumbs and the button removal are built and formatted but were not
  driven before hand-off; the owner was testing at the machine.
  DRAGGING THE BAR IS VERIFIED WORKING 2026-08-02, after the owner reported it didn't. Traced end
  to end with a flushed log: press sets the drag, each move recomputes the divider (y 331 -> 0.376,
  y 255 -> 0.247), and the bar visibly moved. What almost certainly went wrong is that THE BAR IS A
  14px TARGET WITH NO CURSOR FEEDBACK — miss it and the press lands in a pane and starts a
  rubber-band select, which looks exactly like a dead drag. The grab area is now taller than the
  drawn bar (SV_BAR_GRAB_MARGIN), the usual treatment for a thin splitter. A resize cursor over the
  bar would finish the job and is not done.
  TWO METHOD LESSONS FROM THAT HUNT, both of which cost time:
   1. LOG_DEBUG goes to stdout, which is BLOCK-BUFFERED into a file and lost entirely on kill. An
      absence of log lines proves nothing. Uncomment ENABLE_USB_LOG (defs.h) instead — LOG_DEBUG
      also feeds _USB_LOG, which fflushes every write to ~/G2_usb.log.
   2. SYNTHETIC DRAGS DO REACH THE APP — cliclick dd: / m: / du: works. The earlier claim in this
      file that they don't was wrong, and was based on dials specifically not responding. That
      means the Patch Adjuster knobs and the Parameter Overview's drag-to-move CAN be tested this
      way after all, and should be.
  STILL TO DO ON STEP 2 ITSELF:
   * The three bar buttons (top full / bottom full / restore) are drawn and wired but unclicked.
   * A resize cursor over the grab area.
   * THE DIVIDER POSITION IS IN THE PROTOCOL AND G2-EDIT ALREADY HANDLES IT — found 2026-08-03 on
     the owner's hint. It is gPatchDescr[slot].barPosition, the 14-bit field in the patch
     description read at protocol.c:89 and written back at protocol.c:163. So it already round-trips
     to file and device; patches are NOT being corrupted today. Nothing reads it — the split view
     needs to start honouring it.
     CONFIRMED AGAINST THE REFERENCE: NSFile_V11::CPatchHeaderData_11::Get/SetSplitterPos, a short
     clamped to 0..0x3fff — the same 14 bits.
     THE UNIT IS PIXELS, not a fraction. The reference's layout code computes
     the top pane's bottom edge as toolbarHeight + scrollBarHeight + splitterPos, clamps it to the
     window less the scrollbar less 11, and gives the bar itself a height of 0xb = 11 px. So
     splitterPos IS THE VOICE AREA PANE'S HEIGHT IN PIXELS.
     DESIGN CONSEQUENCE, and it needs an owner decision. tSplitView.divider is a FRACTION of the
     canvas band, which redistributes proportionally on a window resize. The stored value is pixels,
     which does not — the original keeps the top pane's pixel height and lets the bottom absorb the
     change, clamping when it runs out. Two options:
       (a) PIXEL-FAITHFUL: make barPosition the source of truth and derive the fraction each frame.
           Round-trips exactly, matches the original's resize behaviour.
       (b) PROPORTIONAL: keep the fraction internally and convert at the protocol boundary using the
           current band height. Nicer on resize, but the stored value then depends on the window
           size at the time, which is a lossy round-trip.
     Leaning (a) for parity and exactness.
     ALSO: the original's default for a NEW patch is SetSplitterPos(4000) — far larger than any real
     window, so it clamps to "voice area takes everything". G2-Edit's new-patch default is 600
     (mouseHandle.c:127), i.e. a ~600px voice area. Worth aligning to 4000.
     And the original's bar is 11 px; ours is SV_BAR_H = 14.
   * Module drag, rubber band and cable drag are NOT yet clamped to their originating pane. Starting
     a drag in one pane and crossing into the other is currently undefined and is the next real
     hazard to close.
   * A patch with genuine FX-area content still needs loading to see the two areas side by side for
     real.
  THE HARD PART IS STILL AHEAD, and it is not the bar: gLocation is read in 40 places across 10
  files and conflates "the location being displayed" with "the location I am editing". Setting it
  from whichever pane a click lands in should resolve most, but each site needs reading. Module
  drag, rubber band and cable drag all need clamping to their originating pane. Hit-testing itself
  should fall out free, since module/param/cable rectangles are all stored in SCREEN space at
  render time and will already be pane-correct.

- The two bullets that used to sit here — the original's Patch Window Split Bar description and the
  Virtual/On-Screen Keyboard — were the pre-implementation notes for features that are now BUILT.
  Their real entries, with what was done and what is still open, are above. Removed 2026-08-03 so
  the parity list stops claiming them as missing.
- Cable popup — ALL FIVE of the original's commands now implemented (2026-07-31), plus the white
  cable invariant they all depend on. Built, uncrustified, unit-tested; NOTHING YET CHECKED ON
  HARDWARE OR AGAINST THE REAL EDITOR.
  New shared layer: src/cableChain.c / cableChain.h — chain/tree walking (find root, collect
  subtree / branch / whole tree), colour derivation, connect, delete, and the Disconnect and Break
  cores. menus.c keeps only menu plumbing; mouseHandle.c and selection.c call the recolour.
  HARDWARE STATUS 2026-08-01: DELETE and DELETE UNUSED CABLES confirmed end-to-end (sent, acked,
  and gone from the device's own patch dump after a restart). DISCONNECT, BREAK and CABLE COLOUR
  are still unconfirmed — and note that any Disconnect test predating 2026-08-01 is void, because
  it was run against the duplicate-cable bug in item 4 below.

  MODEL MISMATCH: RESOLVED, and it was never real. Every one of the original's cable commands is
  keyed on a CONNECTOR, not a cable — GetDisconnectCableNodeMolecules (163473),
  GetBreakCableNodeMolecules (163878), GetRecolorCableMolecules (158993) and
  GetDeleteCableChainMolecules (158869) all take an NSFile_V7::CConnector const&. The original's
  popup is opened by clicking a cable, but nothing downstream of that uses the cable identity. So
  they all port onto G2-Edit's per-connector popup, and NO CABLE HIT-TESTING IS NEEDED anywhere —
  the earlier note claiming Disconnect required sag-curve sampling was wrong.

  THE THREE SCOPES (this was the real subtlety — all three exist and they differ):
    CCompleteTreeIterator   (ctor 30321) — calls CTree::CIterator::base() first, so it walks UP to
                                           the chain root and covers the WHOLE tree. Used by CONNECT.
    CCompleteBranchIterator (ctor 30775) — no base() call, so it stays at the clicked connector and
                                           covers that BRANCH only. Used by COLOR and DELETE.
    partial / find_partial              — chain-ID-matched subtree, used inside BREAK.
  That is what reconciles the manual's two lines: "Cables in a serial cable chain will always have
  the same color" (connect repaints the whole tree) vs "Cables in a branch connection may have
  different colors" (COLOR only repaints one branch).

  WHAT EACH COMMAND DOES NOW:
   * DISCONNECT — splices the clicked connector out and rejoins the chain around it. One surviving
     neighbour becomes the new parent (the connector's own parent; the first child when the
     connector is the chain root, i.e. an output), and every other neighbour reattaches to it.
   * BREAK — unchanged in behaviour, refactored onto the shared layer. Still a no-op on a
     sourceless chain, still whitens the dead tail.
   * COLOR — now applies to the BRANCH (was: cables touching the clicked connector). Refused on a
     sourceless chain, because white is a state, not a user colour.
   * DELETE — new. Branch-scoped: the cable feeding the clicked connector plus its whole subtree.
     Matches "if you want to delete a complete branch connection, this must be done from the cable
     origin of the branch".
   * DELETE UNUSED CABLES — new. Patch-wide sweep. Computed from LIVE source-reachability rather
     than from the stored colour, since a patch loaded from a file or from a G2 written by an older
     build can carry a colour that predates the invariant, and this is not undoable.

  *** USER-VISIBLE CHANGE THE OWNER MAY WANT TO VETO: the old "Delete cable" menu item is GONE. It
  matched none of the original's five (it deleted every cable touching the clicked connector) and
  the todo already flagged it as needing an honest rename. The menu is now exactly the original's
  five, in the manual's order: Disconnect / Break / Cable colour / Delete / Delete unused cables.
  Say the word and it can come back alongside them. ***

  MAPPING WORK IS CLOSED — both MapConnectorColorToCableColor overloads are decoded:
   a. (EConnectorColor const&) at 158224 — table is Control->blue, Logic-at-full-bandwidth->orange,
      Logic->yellow, catch-all->red. Semantically IDENTICAL to G2-Edit's existing
      cable_colour_for_connector_type().
   b. (CConnector const&) at 159536 — builds a CConnectorSpec, calls GetConnectorColor (159516),
      then applies the exact same table. GetConnectorColor resolves module -> panel -> HOLE WIDGET
      and calls its virtual GetColor() at vtable +0xd0, i.e. CPnl*Hole::GetColor() — which is
      bandwidth-dependent for logic holes. That is precisely what effective_connector_type(type,
      upRate) already does, so the correspondence is confirmed and no colour-mapping work remains.

  STILL TO VERIFY (needs the real editor and/or hardware — do not assume):
   1. Whether the branch scope really includes the cable feeding the clicked connector. We include
      it for COLOR and DELETE (cable_chain_collect_branch), inferred from the manual rather than
      proven: CCompleteBranchIterator's end sentinel is the tree end, not next_tree(node), and the
      iteration bound is not cleanly recoverable from the reference. Easy to check in the real
      editor: right-click a mid-chain input, pick a colour, and see whether the cable ARRIVING at
      that connector changes too.
   2. Whether COLOR really is refused on a sourceless (white) chain. That is an inference from
      white being a state rather than a colour, plus the chain-ID guards in Disconnect and Break.
      Not proven — 151152 is CPatch::InternalRecolorCable, which is a thin wrapper with no guard of
      its own, so any guard would sit in the caller we have not located.
   3. Disconnect at an OUTPUT. The original's IsBase() branch makes the first child the new parent
      and chains the other children under it, leaving the lot sourceless and white. Unit-tested to
      behave that way, but it is an odd-looking operation and worth eyeballing in the real editor.
   4. CLOSED 2026-08-01, and it was a REAL BUG — worse than "white might not stick". Recolour went
      out as a full SUB_COMMAND_WRITE_CABLE (0x50) against an existing cable, and on the G2 a cable
      write is an ADD, not an upsert. So every drag-connect left TWO cables on the device (one in
      the created colour, one recoloured), the editor's own write_cable() coalesced them so nothing
      looked wrong, and a later delete removed only one copy — which is what "Disconnect doesn't
      remove the cable on the hardware" actually was. Fixed by sending SUB_COMMAND_WRITE_CABLE_COLOUR
      (0x54) via a new eMsgCmdSetCableColour; header byte is (location << 3) | colour per
      CMCableRecolor::WriteStream (G2Editor.c:18192). Callers: cable_chain_apply_colour() and
      apply_cable_edit() in undo.c when a cable exists and only its colour differs.
      HARDWARE-CONFIRMED: two drags now leave two cables on the device (was four), and deleting
      them leaves none. NOTE: patches written by older builds may still carry duplicate cables.
      METHOD WORTH REUSING: decode the device's OWN cable list out of the patch dump in the USB
      log rather than trusting the editor's view — find the newest EX message with data[1]&7 ==
      slot, scan for a `52 00 LL` section, then parse per protocol.c parse_cable_list(). A one-off
      script settled in seconds what days of reasoning from the reference could not.
   5. Far more cables will now come out white than before (any input-to-input link with no source).
      The topbar has a white visibility toggle (gPatchDescr[slot].visible[6]), so hiding white is
      now a much more destructive-looking action than it used to be.

  RECOLOUR TRIGGERS — the invariant is maintained at three points: cable connect
  (handle_cable_connect, mouseHandle.c, whole-tree scope), module delete (delete_module_and_cables,
  selection.c), and inside Disconnect/Break. Per the design note below this is a conditional
  recompute keyed on source-reachability, so over-calling it is harmless and idempotent; the
  failure mode is a MISSED trigger, which strands a coloured sourceless chain.
  STILL UNATTRIBUTED in the reference: GetRecolorCableMolecules call sites. Worth
  walking backwards to their enclosing functions to see whether they are triggers we have missed
  (cable move / drag-an-end-elsewhere and paste are the obvious candidates — neither recolours in
  G2-Edit today). 151152 is now attributed: CPatch::InternalRecolorCable, the COLOR menu entry.

  All five ARE now undoable (2026-07-31) — see the undo sweep entry below. Built, not yet tested on
  hardware; testing it only became meaningful once the duplicate-cable bug in item 4 was fixed.

- White cables — RULE IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-31, previously only understood. The invariant is:
      every cable in a chain shares ONE colour = the source output's signal-type colour,
      or WHITE (6) if the chain has no source output at all.
  MapConnectorColorToCableColor can never return 6; white only ever appears as the literal 6 on the
  no-chain-ID branches. That is what makes white unambiguously mean "this chain has no source".
  Colour is STORED and USER-OVERRIDABLE (the COLOR menu offers green and purple, which no signal
  type produces), so the recompute is conditional on SOURCE-REACHABILITY CHANGING, never
  unconditional — a blind recompute after every edit would destroy the user's colour choice.
  The minimal case that motivated all this now works: on a new patch, linking two mixer inputs to
  each other produces a WHITE cable immediately, because the new cable's chain has no source.
  Previously G2-Edit showed it red, a state the original cannot represent.
  Unit-tested headless (32 assertions over the shipped functions: white-on-sourceless, whole-tree
  repaint on connect, branch vs tree scope, Break's no-op and whitening at both an input and an
  output, Disconnect's splice at mid-chain and at the root, and the unused sweep). The harness is
  NOT in the repo — it lives in the session scratchpad and links cableChain.c / dataBase.c /
  moduleResourcesAccess.c with a stubbed msg_send. Worth reconstructing if this area is touched
  again; there is no test target in the Xcode project to host it.

- Bit-stream loop guards audited 2026-08-05, after the parse_midi_cc() bug (see MIDI Learn above).
  THE PATTERN TO LOOK FOR: a loop guard that asks whether ANY bytes remain while the body reads a
  whole RECORD. It fails in both directions — a payload smaller than the body's appetite is skipped
  entirely (which is what hid every received CC), and a truncated one is read past.
  FOUR LOOPS CARRIED IT. All now require the bytes they are about to read:
   * protocol.c parse_patch()  — guard covered the 1-byte type, body then read a 16-bit count.
   * protocol.c parse_perf()   — same.
   * protocol.c (category scan) — same, breaks out instead of failing.
   * usbComms.c parse_resources_used() — guard wanted 1 byte, the record is a fixed 29
     (RESOURCES_RECORD_BYTES, tallied in a comment there).
  The three TLV ones already bounds-checked the VALUE after reading the count; it was the header
  itself that was unguarded, so the overrun was at most two bytes and into the same buffer — wrong
  values rather than a crash, which is why it has gone unnoticed.
  VERIFIED: all nine PatchTestFiles still load with their expected module counts, and Corrupt.pch2
  still fails deliberately (0 modules) rather than being newly rejected for the wrong reason.
  NOT AUDITED YET: the many straight-line parsers that read a fixed layout without looping. They
  cannot loop past the end, but nothing checks `length` against what they read either.

- Sweep up the missing undos — undo coverage grew per-feature and is now patchy, so Ctrl-Z does
  something for some edits and silently nothing for others, which is worse than uniformly absent.
  CABLES ARE NOW COVERED (2026-07-31, eUndoCmdCableEdit): all five popup commands plus drag-connect
  in handle_cable_connect(). Recorded as a before/after SNAPSHOT of every cable in the location
  rather than a change list, because Disconnect deletes, re-creates AND recolours in one command and
  the affected set is only knowable by walking the chain. undo_begin_cable_edit/undo_commit_cable_edit
  bracket the edit; commit compares as a SET (delete-and-recreate reshuffles database indices) and
  pushes nothing when unchanged. Colour lives in the snapshot, so a user's colour override survives
  undo along with the topology. BUILT, NOT HARDWARE-TESTED.
  PRINCIPLE DECIDED (owner, 2026-08-01): PATCH CONTENT is undoable; DEVICE-GLOBAL CONFIGURATION is
  not. Ctrl-Z after editing a patch must not silently reach over and change the synth's own global
  settings. That makes this a bounded sweep rather than an open-ended one.

  DONE 2026-08-01 (all built, NONE hardware-tested):
   * menu_action_create (Add Module) — eUndoCmdCreate. Snapshot taken AFTER creation so redo puts
     back exactly what was made rather than re-deriving defaults; undo goes through
     delete_module_and_cables() so anything patched to it since goes with it. recreate_module() was
     refactored to take slot/location instead of a delete payload so both paths share it, and the
     module-snapshot code came out of undo_push_delete_selection() into snapshot_module(), now
     shared by delete-undo, create-redo and the clipboard.
   * action_set_module_colour — eUndoCmdModuleColour, plain old/new.
   * action_assign_midi_cc / action_deassign_midi_cc — eUndoCmdMidiCc, a bracketed before/after
     image of the SLOT'S WHOLE CONTROLLER TABLE. Assigning a CC that another parameter already owns
     steals it AND assigns it, so one click is two changes and per-entry recording would
     misrepresent it. Apply deassigns whatever is not in the target, then assigns the target.
   * action_assign_global_knob / action_deassign_global_knob — eUndoCmdGlobalKnob, same pattern,
     performance-wide (no slot). Apply only messages the knobs that actually differ.

  NOT DOING, per the principle above: action_setting_u8 / action_setting_i8. They write through
  gSettingU8Target into SYNTH settings — device-global config, not patch content. (Reversing this
  would need the target's IDENTITY recorded, not just its value; the pointer-based design gives an
  undo payload nothing stable to name the field by.)

  FALSE POSITIVE: action_rename_morph_label only opens the inline editor. The commit goes through
  the shared param-name path, which already pushes undo_push_param_name. Confirm when testing.

  STILL OPEN — action_copy_variation, the last real gap and the biggest:
    Copying variation N over variation M rewrites EVERY parameter of EVERY module in the slot, so
    undo has to snapshot the whole target variation before the copy: up to MAX_NUM_MODULES (128) x
    MAX_NUM_PARAMETERS (128) tParam, which is real memory per undo entry and would want storing
    only for ACTIVE modules at their actual module_param_count(), not the full rectangle. Redo can
    just re-run the copy. Note the action also copies each param's morphRange[] across all
    NUM_MORPHS, so the snapshot must be the whole tParam, not just .value. Worth checking whether
    the existing eUndoCmdParam machinery can carry a batch rather than inventing a third snapshot
    shape.
    Also unchecked: action_perf_setting_u8 (perf settings — undo_push_perf_setting already exists
    for that domain, so it may just need wiring rather than new machinery).

- Reconnect clobbers offline edits — BUILT 2026-08-01, mostly hardware-verified. Kept below for the
  reasoning; what shipped is at the end of this entry.
  Original report (2026-07-31): edit a patch with the G2
  unplugged (entirely normal, and the backdoor test channel exists precisely to drive the app
  offline), then plug the G2 in: the app fetches the synth's patch and overwrites the in-memory
  database, and the offline work is gone with no warning and no way back.
  PREREQUISITE, and probably most of the work: G2-Edit HAS NO DIRTY TRACKING. Nothing matching
  gDirty/isDirty/modified/unsaved exists anywhere in src/. Without a notion of "the editor's copy
  has diverged from the synth's" there is nothing to base any prompt on, and a prompt that fires on
  EVERY reconnect regardless would just be a nag people learn to dismiss — which is worse than
  useless, because they will dismiss the one that mattered.
  THREE CANDIDATE BEHAVIOURS (owner raised A and B; C is a suggestion):
   A. ASK WHICH WINS — "keep the synth's patch" vs "push the editor's patch to the synth". Best
      ergonomics, but the second branch is a destructive write to the synth's edit buffer, so it
      needs the same care as the Bank Restore work.
   B. OFFER TO SAVE FIRST — save the editor's patch to a file, then load the synth's. Strictly
      safer, and maintains both, but it is a save dialogue at an awkward moment and it does not let
      you carry on working on the editor's version, only archive it.
   C. AUTO-PRESERVE, DO NOT ASK — always write the editor's patch to a recovery file, load the
      synth's, and just report "your edits were saved to <path>". No modal decision at the moment
      someone has plugged in a cable, and nothing is ever lost. Cheapest to build once dirty
      tracking exists, and it degrades gracefully: if the divergence did not matter, the user
      ignores the message.
      A and C combine well — auto-preserve ALWAYS, and additionally offer the choice only when the
      editor's copy is actually dirty.
  OPEN QUESTIONS: is dirty tracked per SLOT (there are 4 plus the performance) or per patch, since
  a reconnect reloads everything and the conflict may only affect one slot? And discarding the
  editor's patch must clear the undo history, which would otherwise let Ctrl-Z "undo" into a state
  that never existed on the synth — ties into the undo sweep entry above.
  SCOPE NOTE: this is not really about reconnect. It is the general "editor copy has diverged from
  the synth copy" problem, and the same conflict exists on slot change and on any other reload from
  the device. Worth solving once, generally, rather than special-casing the reconnect path. The
  mechanism below is reusable for those, but is currently wired to the reconnect path only.

  WHAT SHIPPED (owner chose A + C: auto-preserve, then ask):
   * DIRTY TRACKING TURNED OUT TO BE FREE. While online every edit is sent to the G2 as it is made,
     so the copies can only drift while it is away — and those edits are already recorded in the
     USB command queue, which state_handler() only drains when online. Anything sitting in it at
     reconnect was enqueued while the G2 was gone, and each message carries its slot. So the drain
     IS the divergence check, per slot, with no dirty flag anywhere. src/deviceSync.c.
   * THE QUEUE IS ALWAYS DISCARDED, NEVER REPLAYED. It holds increments addressed to a patch the
     reconnect is about to replace; replaying them would edit whatever now occupies each index.
     "Send to Synth" therefore pushes WHOLE patches (push_slot_to_device per dirty slot), which is
     also what the owner asked for.
   * THE PULL IS DEFERRED, not done-then-regretted: new eCommsAwaitingSyncDecision state parks the
     USB thread until the user answers. That deferral is the actual bug fix.
   * RECOVERY FILE FIRST, then the dialog: one .pch2 per dirty slot in
     ~/Library/Application Support/G2-Edit/Recovery, so no answer — including Escape — can lose
     the work, and the dialog says so. Pruned to the newest 10 on each write, and deleted only on
     a successful "Save As..." (never on the Pull branch, where it is the only copy left).
   * Three-way dialog: Send to Synth / Save As... / Pull from Synth. Escape means Pull. Undo is
     cleared on every branch, because after a wholesale push or replacement its entries describe a
     device state that no longer exists.
   * Needed a 3-button alert (SynthLib show_choice) — see the SynthLib entry under AWAITING OWNER
     VERIFICATION for the submodule commit + pin bump that still has to happen.
  VERIFIED ON HARDWARE 2026-08-01 by real unplug/replug: detection, per-slot mask, deferred pull,
  recovery file, dialog layout, and the Pull branch resolving cleanly (twice).
  NOT YET EXERCISED: the "Send to Synth" push branch, and "Save As..." including its recovery-file
  cleanup. Both need one more unplug/replug cycle; Send overwrites the synth's edit buffer.
  STILL OPEN: dirty state is per SLOT and only covers the reconnect path. Slot change and any other
  device reload can still clobber silently, and the performance domain is not covered at all.
- Close button moved TOP LEFT and drawn as a cross — DONE 2026-08-02, needs an owner eyeball.
  macOS puts the close control top left; every panel and popup had a "Close" text button top right.
  Now a 14px square with a drawn cross (two render_line() diagonals, never the character 'X').
  SHARED AT LAST: draw_panel_chrome() and draw_panel_close_button() moved out of graphics.c into
  SynthLib (utilsGraphics.c), joined by panel_close_button_rect() for hit-testing outside the
  render pass. The three popups that used to replicate the chrome by hand - alertDialog.c,
  fileBrowser.cpp, bankBrowser.cpp, each with a comment admitting it mirrored G2-Edit's copy
  pixel-for-pixel - now just call it, and their close_button_rect() is a one-line delegation.
  The title text indents past the button, and the Slot A-D buttons in the Patch Settings and
  Parameter Pages title bars moved right into the corner the old Close button had occupied.
  THIS ONE DOES REACH THE OTHER TWO APPS, unlike the dial-anchoring change: SynthEdit and
  EmuUtility both use alertDialog/fileBrowser/bankBrowser, so their popups get the new close
  button too. All three build; only G2-Edit has been looked at on screen.
  TRAP WORTH REMEMBERING: utilsGraphics.c compiles WITHOUT G2_EDIT defined, so inside it
  RGB_BACKGROUND_GREY is synthlibDefs.h's other branch - 0.30, the same value as the RGB_GREY_3
  title bar. The first cut used it for the button fill and drew a black cross, which made the
  button vanish into the banner with an almost invisible mark on it. The colours are now derived
  from RGB_GREY_3/RGB_GREY_7 (0.30/0.70 in BOTH branches) with contrasting_text_colour() picking
  the stroke, so it is stable whichever app compiles the file. Any future SynthLib drawing code
  should assume the non-G2_EDIT palette.
  Line weights are split - PANEL_CLOSE_BOX_LINE 1.0 for the frame, PANEL_CLOSE_CROSS_LINE 1.5 for
  the cross - because the frame's axis-aligned lines stay crisp while the diagonals antialias, and
  equal numbers made the box dominate the mark. BORDER_LINE_WIDTH (2.0) is a slab at this size.
  Fits inside both title bar heights in use (24 in G2-Edit's panels, 26 in the SynthLib popups);
  the button spans y+6..y+20. No popup has content that high - fileBrowser's Up button is the
  closest at y+30.
  SynthLib is dirty again: commit and re-advance all three pins.


ARCHITECTURE — RATIONALISE AND COMMONALISE FOR THE OTHER PROJECTS

***PRIORITY*** (identified 2026-07-25 by diffing G2-Edit / EmuUtility / SynthEdit directly — concrete,
mostly-mechanical dedup + decoupling wins at the host<->SynthLib boundary; do these before the bigger
open design items below):

1. [DONE 2026-08-20, all three apps] Shared graphics/window bootstrap.
   BUILT as SynthLib/src/synthlibWindow.{c,h}: synthlib_window_create(config, callbacks) does GLFW
   init, the two window hints, creation, the TARGET/minDivisor minimum and locked aspect ratio, the
   context, the initial scale query, callback registration and the blend setup; synthlib_window_close()
   is the teardown half, registered as the close callback.
   BIGGER THAN THE ESTIMATE, because six of the twelve callbacks turned out to be character-identical
   in all three apps and moved wholesale: error, framebuffer size, content scale, window size, window
   position and window close. An app now supplies only the seven that reach into its own domain (key,
   char, cursor, mouse button, scroll, focus, refresh) and any of those may be NULL.
   AND IT CAUGHT ONE PIECE OF LIVE DRIFT: EmuUtility registered a window-focus callback that its
   close handler never unregistered, because that handler was copied before the focus callback
   existed. The shared version cannot drift that way — it unregisters exactly what it registered,
   from the same table.
   G2-Edit's init_graphics() is 120 lines lighter and its graphics.c has no GLFW callback in it that
   is not about this app. Verified live against the hardware: window opens Online with the patch
   rendered, menu bar opens and tracks hover, resize re-lays out correctly through the shared
   framebuffer/scale path, the saved size is restored on the next launch (so the shared size/pos
   callbacks reach synthlibPersistence), and closing the window exits cleanly.
   NOT EXERCISED: the key callback. Synthetic keystrokes (cliclick and System Events alike) do not
   reach this app's GLFW window, so the key path could not be driven from a script — but the
   pre-change build behaves identically, which is what rules the change in or out. Worth an owner
   keypress.
   ALL THREE APPS ARE CONVERTED (2026-08-20, once the owner pushed SynthLib 57356ff and checked it
   out everywhere): G2-Edit -120 lines, EmuUtility -107, SynthEdit -126, and every app's graphics.c
   now holds only callbacks that are about that app. All three build clean and were launched and
   screenshotted: G2-Edit renders its patch Online, EmuUtility its panel and LCD, SynthEdit its page
   tabs and dials in the right colours — which incidentally re-proves the theme is reaching SynthLib,
   since a missing configure_synthlib_theme() is what once drew its power buttons black on black.
   PINS: G2-Edit's is current. EmuUtility and SynthEdit still lag — see item 3.

2. [MOSTLY DONE 2026-08-20 — registry + facade; see the two deliberate exclusions below] Popup lifecycle coordinator.

   WHAT LANDED. floatingPanel.{c,h} moved from G2-Edit into SynthLib, which is the coordinator's
   foundation rather than a side-quest: it already WAS the registration model this item asks for — a
   tFloatingPanelEntry of {panel, render, mouse, key}, one sort, draw back-to-front and hit-test
   front-to-back. tMouseButton went with it into synthlibTypes.h; it had been G2-Edit's alone, which
   is precisely why neither of the other two apps could adopt a panel.
   Added floating_panel_mouse(), the three-way press/release preamble (PassThrough / Consumed /
   Content) that each panel used to write out by hand, and floating_panel_set_bounds().

   AND THE THREE SETTINGS PANELS ARE NOW FLOATING PANELS — owner's ask, and the direct answer to the
   Escape gap recorded further up this file: "Synth Settings" and the others should behave like the
   Virtual Keyboard. Synth Settings, Performance Settings and Patch Settings each gained a
   tFloatingPanel, lost draw_dialog_background_overlay() and their per-frame (renderW - boxW) / 2
   re-centring, and joined the render, mouse and key registries. They are draggable, they raise on
   click, they close on Escape through the same front-to-back sort as everything else, and two of
   them can be open at once — none of which was possible when each was modal.
   The bespoke settings_panel_escape() helper written earlier the same day is GONE, replaced by
   registration. It walked the three panels in a hand-fixed order, which is right only while that
   order matches what is drawn — the exact drift this registry exists to prevent.

   PANELS ARE NOW BOUNDED BY THE CANVAS CHROME (owner's observation): floating_panel_set_bounds() is
   set per frame to the render area minus SCROLLBAR_WIDTH on the right and bottom, so a panel cannot
   be dragged over the scrollbars. Overlapping the TOP bar is still allowed, deliberately — a panel
   has to start somewhere and the bar is not something you scroll. The clamp runs every frame, after
   the drag has moved the panel, which is what makes it a drag limit rather than only a placement
   rule. A panel bigger than the bounds is pinned top-left and allowed to overflow: at the 640x360
   minimum window, Synth Settings is taller than the whole canvas.

   A TRAP THIS UNCOVERED, worth more than the change that found it: types.h must include defs.h
   BEFORE anything that reaches synthlibTypes.h, because synthlibTypes.h pulls in synthlibDefs.h,
   where every RGB_* colour sits behind #ifdef G2_EDIT — and G2_EDIT is defined in defs.h. Get the
   order wrong and synthlibDefs.h is consumed with the guard closed, its include guard then makes the
   later #include a no-op, and the build fails in unrelated files with "undeclared identifier
   RGB_GREY_9". There is now a comment in types.h saying so.

   THE FACADE IS BUILT TOO — SynthLib/src/synthlibPopups.{c,h}, same day. Registration, not a fixed
   call order: every popup carries a LAYER, SynthLib's own five are registered internally, and an app
   registers its own into the same ordering. G2-Edit now does, with layers chosen to reproduce exactly
   what its eight hand-ordered render calls did — patch notes and the two progress panels above the
   context menu and below the browsers, the device-busy overlay above the browsers and below the
   alert. What the host used to carry, and no longer does:
     * the modal click cascade (file browser / bank browser / alert dialog, each with its own early
       return, its own mouse-down-vs-up gating, and the alert's routing around its bank-picker
       dropdown) -> synthlib_popups_dispatch_click(). 13 routing refs down to 5, of which 2 are a
       debug log line.
     * the same cascade for keys, for scroll and for character input -> _dispatch_key / _dispatch_scroll
       / _dispatch_char. All four input channels were separately hand-cascaded.
     * the render tail, eight calls -> synthlib_popups_render().
     * the hover tick, which the host had to remember per popup -> synthlib_popups_tick().

   TWO THINGS DELIBERATELY LEFT WITH THE HOST, and the reasoning matters more than the code:
     * THE MENU BAR IS NOT RENDERED BY THE COORDINATOR. It is chrome that floating panels sit ABOVE —
       a panel may overlap the top bar — so drawing it in the popup tail would put the bar over a
       panel that still took the click. Only its hover tick is registered.
     * CLICKS FOR THE MENU BAR AND THE CONTEXT MENU ARE STILL THE HOST'S, for the same reason: both
       sit BEHIND the floating panels in the input pipeline, and the coordinator is consulted before
       the panels are. Dispatching them there would silently move them in front. Their mouse entries
       are NULL and say so. They follow once the hosts' own pipeline steps are registered here too.

   ALL THREE APPS ARE ON IT (2026-08-20, after the owner pushed SynthLib d50d626):
     * SynthEdit — the full set. Its modal cascade came out exactly as G2-Edit's did: 11 routing refs
       to ZERO, all four input channels through the coordinator, three hover-tick calls to one, the
       render tail to one. It carries GLFW's button/action pair rather than a tMouseButton, so the
       normalisation happens at its single dispatch call site.
     * EmuUtility — only the menu bar and context menu exist there today, so it gains the hover tick
       and the render; the browsers and the alert dialog come for free if it ever opens one.
     * G2-Edit keeps 2 references to the *_active() predicates, both in one debug log line.

   A PRE-PUSH CHECK WORTH REPEATING for anything added to SynthLib: SynthLib/src is a synchronized
   folder in all three Xcode projects, so a new file starts compiling into EVERY app the moment it is
   pulled, converted or not. Compile it with `clang -fsyntax-only -I SynthLib/src` WITHOUT the app's
   own defines before pushing — that is how floatingPanel.c's missing <stddef.h> was found, which
   built only by luck of transitive includes in G2-Edit and would have been someone else's build
   failure. Nothing in either new file touches the #ifdef G2_EDIT half of synthlibDefs.h.

3. [PART DONE 2026-08-20] Move mouse-callback plumbing into SynthLib (ties into 1 & 2).

   DONE — THE SHARED PRIMITIVES, on the platform-free input seam (declared in inputState.h,
   implemented in inputStateGlfw.c beside set_modifier_state_from_glfw):
       tCoord       synthlib_window_to_logical(double x, double y);
       void         synthlib_mouse_coord(tCoord * coord);
       tMouseButton synthlib_mouse_button(int glfwButton, int glfwAction);
   window_to_logical() was character-identical in EmuUtility and SynthEdit and inlined into G2-Edit's
   get_global_gui_scaled_mouse_coord() — where it had LOST the divide-by-zero guard the other two
   kept, so a window reporting a zero dimension (which happens while minimising) would have divided
   by it. The shared one keeps the guard. G2-Edit is converted; both its functions are now one-line
   wrappers, names kept because get_global_gui_scaled_mouse_coord is what synthlib_host_init() is
   given and what nine files call.

   WHY inputState.h AND NOT synthlibWindow.h, having first put them there: synthlibWindow.h includes
   <GLFW/glfw3.h> for the callback typedefs, and SynthEdit's mouseHandle.c deliberately does NOT pull
   the GLFW header in — it hand-declares the handful of GLFW functions it needs and #defines the key
   constants, so including it produced "conflicting types for glfwSetInputMode". These three take
   plain doubles and ints, so the platform-free seam is where they belong; inputState.h now includes
   synthlibTypes.h for tCoord/tMouseButton and stays GLFW-free, which is what lets the VST3 keep
   linking inputState.c.

   AND IT BROKE THE PLUG-IN, silently, exactly as this file warns elsewhere: do-vst3's source list is
   MANUAL, and it still named $HERE/src/floatingPanel.c after that file moved into SynthLib. The app
   builds either way — src/ is a synchronized folder — so only the plug-in fails, and only when
   someone next builds it. Fixed, do-vst3 runs, universal binary, installed. CHECK do-vst3 WHENEVER A
   FILE MOVES OR IS ADDED.

   ALL THREE APPS ARE CONVERTED. Each app's get_global_gui_scaled_mouse_coord() is now a one-line
   wrapper; the name stays because it is what synthlib_host_init() is handed and what nine files in
   G2-Edit call.

   THE CALLBACK ADAPTER IS BUILT AND IN BOTH MIDI APPS (2026-08-20). tSynthLibInputHandlers in
   synthlibWindow.h: supply normalised handlers instead of raw GLFW callbacks and SynthLib installs
   the shims, so a handler receives a logical tCoord and a tMouseButton. EmuUtility and SynthEdit have
   ZERO GLFW shims left in any of the three — G2-Edit converted last, its callbacks having BEEN its
   handlers, so six signatures changed.
   TWO THINGS G2-EDIT NEEDED THAT THE OTHERS DID NOT:
     * IT KEPT ITS RAW-COORDINATE DRAGS, deliberately. Its vertical and horizontal dial modes
       difference RAW window coordinates and the arithmetic around that is tuned — Alt morph offsets,
       the sub-unit accumulator, the Shift-fine divisor. It already had cursor_raw_coord(), so
       cursor_pos() fetches raw there rather than disturbing working maths to change a signature. The
       other two moved to logical units and the owner confirmed both drags feel unchanged.
     * THE SYNTHETIC RELEASE in recover_lost_drag() used to save and restore the modifier state around
       its direct mouse_button() call, because that function began by pushing
       set_modifier_state_from_glfw(mods) and a literal 0 would report every modifier as released —
       which would make a still-held Alt stop meaning "drag the morph offset". The shim does that now,
       and a direct call bypasses the shim, so the save/restore is unnecessary and gone.
   IT FOUND A DRIFT BUG IMMEDIATELY: EmuUtility's own shims never called
   set_modifier_state_from_glfw(), so its shift/ctrl predicates only ever held whatever a previous
   event had left behind. The shared shim does it before every handler runs.
   THE DIAL DRAGS MOVED TO LOGICAL COORDINATES with it, and each app's local delta_to_logical() is
   gone: window_to_logical is a linear scale with no offset, so the difference of two logical
   coordinates equals the converted difference of two window ones. Arithmetically identical, and the
   drag rate is unchanged — but it wants a real drag in each mode to confirm.
   WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO is dispatch popups. Where that sits relative to a host's other
   early checks is a real decision — G2-Edit swallows canvas input during a device operation BEFORE
   consulting the popups — and moving it silently would change behaviour nobody asked to change.

   (superseded, kept for the record) The remaining description follows. The
   thin GLFW shim in each app still repeats the same shape: decode the button/action pair, scale the
   coordinate, offer the event to the popup coordinator, call the app's own handler, request a
   redraw. Sharing it would also unify the two conventions that exist today — EmuUtility's and
   SynthEdit's `*_cb` wrappers calling `handle_*`, against G2-Edit's callbacks being the handlers
   themselves.
   THE BLOCKER THIS ITEM RECORDED IS NOW GONE: it said the app-specific hit-testing could not move
   until gParamRectangle was off the render-time global arrays. That migration is done (see the
   PARTIALLY DONE section), so more of the dispatch can follow the adapter whenever this is picked up.

   ALSO OUTSTANDING: EmuUtility and SynthEdit are pinned to SynthLib 02056da and so do not yet have
   the context-menu click routing, context_menu_contains() or the click-region viewport clip from the
   second batch of fixes. Both want a pull and a pin bump; neither is broken meanwhile.

4. [DONE 2026-08-20, both MIDI apps] CoreMIDI send-primitive extraction.
   SynthLib/src/synthlibMidi.{c,h}: synthlib_midi_set_out_port() + synthlib_midi_send_to(). Each app
   still creates and names its own port inside its own connect logic, then hands it over; scan,
   connect and dispatch are untouched, as this item asked.
   THE TWO APPS' COPIES DIFFERED IN EXACTLY THE WAYS THAT MATTERED, and in both of them SynthEdit had
   already been bitten: a 512-byte stack buffer that silently failed a whole-bank restore (18734
   bytes measured), and a void return that left the caller nothing to test — which is what made
   Restore Bank report success while nothing reached the wire (2026-07-11). EmuUtility still had BOTH
   until now, so this was not a tidy-up: it fixed the same latent bug there for free. The shared
   version uses a static 64KB buffer under the mutex that already serialised sends, rather than a
   64KB stack frame at every call site.

   (superseded, kept for the record) The original text of this item follows.
   EmuUtility and SynthEdit's low-level MIDI send is near-identical (MIDIPacketListInit /
   MIDIPacketListAdd / MIDISend over the same gMidiOutPort/gMidiDest globals; SynthEdit just has a
   bigger sysex buffer). Move the packet-send primitive + the CoreMIDI handle globals
   (gMidiClient/gMidiInPort/gMidiOutPort/gMidiSource/gMidiDest) into SynthLib WITHOUT touching each
   app's scan/connect/dispatch logic (which has diverged for good reason — see the comms bullet below).
   2-of-3 (G2-Edit is USB, not MIDI). Small, safe, unblocks the bigger queue abstraction. Render-loop
   skeleton extraction (synthlib_run_frame_loop) is possible but LOW priority — do it last, after 1-3,
   since the per-frame "flush hooks" are genuinely app-specific.

PARTIALLY DONE / STILL OPEN:
- Migrate G2-Edit's gParamRectangle[slot][location][index][param] onto the click-region registry —
  STARTED 2026-08-20, 3 of 5 readers gone.

  THE FINDING THAT SHAPED IT: parameters ALREADY register click regions, carrying a tParamClickCtx of
  {moduleKey, paramIndex} as userData. So the registry already knew everything the array was being
  read for; what it lacked was a QUERY. dispatch_click_region() answers an event by calling a handler,
  and several things need the same question answered without an event — a right-click wanting a menu
  for whatever is under the cursor, a keyboard nudge acting on the pointed-at widget, an overlay
  deciding what to describe. Each walked the app's own rectangle array instead, which is a second,
  parallel description of where every widget is: free to disagree with the registry about z-order, and
  stale in exactly the frames where a widget was drawn but deliberately not registered.

  ADDED TO SYNTHLIB: click_region_at(coord) and click_region_at_layer(coord, layer), using the SAME
  front-to-back walk as dispatch, so a query and a click can never disagree about what is in front.
  dispatch_click_region() now shares that walk rather than carrying its own copy.

  CONVERTED, and note that only ONE of the three is a hit test — the other two were not queries at all:
   1. canvasDrag.c:402, the rotary drag's angle: it re-read the rect every mouse-move to find the
      widget's centre. Now CAPTURED into gParamDragging.rect when the drag is armed, which is what
      pointer capture is for — the owner of a gesture holds what it needs, so nothing that
      re-registers or re-renders underneath it mid-drag can disturb it. This was one of the two
      readers this entry used to cite as the reason the array had to stay readable.
   2. paramPages.c:495: it rendered a widget and then read the widget's rect back out of the global.
      render_param_common() now RETURNS the rect it registered, so the caller keeps what it drew.
      That was the other cited reason.
   3. paramOverlay.c:286, "is the pointer on this param": now param_is_under_cursor() in
      moduleGraphics.c, which asks the registry and compares against the parameter's own click
      context — its identity there. No tag and no lookup table, and sParamClickCtx stays private.

  ALL FIVE READERS ARE NOW GONE (2026-08-20). The remaining two needed DISCRIMINATION rather than a
  better query: the registry hands back a void * userData and each widget kind registers a different
  struct, so acting on an answer means knowing what came back. Every canvas click context now begins
  with the same {eCanvasWidgetKind kind; tModuleKey key;} pair — the sockaddr idiom, declared as
  tCanvasWidget in moduleGraphics.h — and canvas_widget_at() / canvas_widget_at_any_layer() return it.
   * canvasDrag.c's canvas_right_click(): a walk over every module, then its connectors, then its
     parameters, then its body, is ONE query. The precedence survives without being restated —
     render_module registers the body, then the drag strip, then calls render_module_common, and the
     registry resolves ties by most-recently-registered, so a connector still beats the body it sits
     inside. Its slot/location arguments are now unused: the registry only holds what is on screen.
   * canvas_nudge_param_under_cursor(): two walks over every module (Morph location first, because
     morph knobs draw over the canvas) collapsed to one query. That ordering is not lost, it is
     inherited — morph dials register at eClickLayerPanel, canvas widgets at eClickLayerCanvas, and
     the walk goes front to back. The old code's own comment admitted the duplication: "the click path
     gets this ordering from the click-region layers instead".

  A LANDMINE REMOVED ON THE WAY: the morph dials registered (void *)(intptr_t)i as their user data —
  fine for a handler that knows what it registered, fatal for anything that asks the registry what is
